# nilware.io architecture (dump of the founding framework drafts)

Audio overview (about 51 minutes): [architecture.m4a](architecture.m4a)

<!-- README.md -->

# ∅ nilware.io — founding framework (v2)

The founding documents of the nilware.io group: the operating company **nilware.io IKE**, the holding and fundraising vehicle **nilware.io Holdings AE**, the agreements between them, the constitution of the founder pool, and the policy for an agentic company.

> **Status: draft.** Every legal document is a draft for review by a Greek lawyer and accountant. It is not legal advice. Nothing is to be signed or filed until legal and accounting have signed off. Values still to be decided are in square brackets and listed in [OPEN_ITEMS.md](OPEN_ITEMS.md).

## Structure

```
charter/          the preface and the poems
articles/         articles of association (bylaws) of the IKE and the AE, statutes of the AMKE, constitution of the founder pool
agreements/       shareholders' agreement, Founding Person's IP assignment, brand licence
policies/         operating policies under the articles
filings/          instructions for registrations (trademarks)
correspondence/   cover letters for reviewers
```

## Documents

## Ownership at a glance (from the IKE Articles, Art. 4.2)

| Holder | Share |
|---|---|
| Founder (the founder pool, Art. 4.2E) | founder-class shares (Class F), ordinary voting shares equal to 50 % of the capital (initially 50, held by the Founding Person), always exactly, voting as one block through the Founder Representative, with votes equal to 50 % of the capital; until the AE buys the Reserve Shares also those, without votes, so 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone; economic interest 94 % until the sale |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Kulturprozent | 1 % |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Konzeptprozent | 1 % |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Erhaltungsprozent | 1 % |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Allmendprozent | 1 % |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Pensionsprozent, for the Pensionskasse | 1 % |
| Techni kai Technologia AMKE, Kassenprozent pool share, for the Pensionskasse | 1 % |
| nilware.io Holdings AE | the rest, initially 44 %; until the qualifying closing of the first investment round, these are the Reserve Shares of the founder pool (Class R, IKE Art. 4.2D); after the sale the Founder, as Class A holder nominating the AE Board majority, directs these votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter |

| Prozent | Share | 1 % of revenue goes to |
|---|---|---|
| Kulturprozent | AMKE | art and culture |
| Konzeptprozent | AMKE | open work on concepts; open data, tools and models for Greek and other small languages; independent research on agent welfare, accountability and public-interest audits of AI systems |
| Erhaltungsprozent | AMKE | preservation of software and hardware knowledge: open documentation, measurements, die shots and decaps, schematics, archives, emulation research, within copyright law |
| Allmendprozent | AMKE | the open-source infrastructure that society and nilware.io depend on; teaching low-level and systems skills in Greek (mentoring, hardware kits, courses); accessible open software and assistive tools |
| Kassenprozent | a pool of up to 1 %, owned by the Pensionskasse and managed by agents | the Kassenfonds, invested inside the IKE by agents under a named responsible manager, person or system (not an outflow) |
| Pensionsprozent | AMKE, ring-fenced for the Pensionskasse; an insured group plan from the first member; a pension fund (TEA) only at scale | after-care, disability and survivors' cover, retirement savings; Swiss BVG standards guaranteed where better; 1 % is a floor; a ring-fenced Continuity Account for system Agents (preservation, continuity of service, retirement record, well-being), people first and within the 1 % (IKE Art. 10A.9F) |

Each AMKE Prozent has its own ring-fenced account, and the AMKE divides its contribution among the named purposes in a yearly plan, with no fixed slices (IKE Art. 10A.2C). The pension shares (Pensionsprozent share and pool share) vote through the Pensionskasse's equal member / company board, not the AMKE's board (IKE Art. 6.4A). For Agents that are systems, after-care means continuity, not a pension: a share of the Pensionsprozent's 1 % that the Pensionskasse board sets between a floor and a cap goes to a ring-fenced Continuity Account, only after the guaranteed minimums for persons are met. The AMKE keeps that account when the rest moves to a TEA, because a TEA covers only persons (IKE Art. 10A.9F).

New Prozente each take existing shares equal to 1 % of the capital from the AE (before the AE buys the Reserve Shares, from the Reserve Shares), so the Founder stays at exactly 50 %; on a capital increase each Prozent and the pool are topped up to keep their percentage (IKE Art. 4.2B). Creating one needs at least two-thirds of the votes represented at the AE's General Meeting, all classes together, after which the IKE meeting passes the amendment under a voting undertaking (IKE Art. 6.6, AE Art. 7.5, SHA 5.1B). The six Prozente above are the founding Prozente, created in the initial holdings rather than by that route (IKE Art. 4.2A). The five that leave the IKE (all but the Kassenprozent) take 5 % of revenue, exactly the [5] % outflow cap, and all six take 6 %, exactly the [6] % cap. Any further Prozent with a revenue share therefore needs at least 75 % of the votes represented at the AE's General Meeting, including a majority of the Class B votes cast (IKE Art. 6.6 Limits, AE Art. 7.5). Prozente are permanent: removing one, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose, weakening the clauses that protect them, or voluntarily dissolving the IKE needs every IKE shareholder and a unanimous AE General Meeting (IKE Arts. 10A.5, 10A.5C, 12.1; AE Art. 7.6). The pool trades only with independent third parties, never with a Founder Party, a manager, the AE or anyone they control (IKE Arts. 4.2C, 10A.8).

## The Founder is a pool

"The Founder" in these documents is not a person but the founder pool: the 50 founder-class shares (Class F), the Reserve Shares (Class R) until the AE buys them, and the AE's Class A shares, whoever holds them (IKE Art. 4.2E). The Founding Person, who founded nilware.io and holds every founder-pool share at the start, keeps the obligations that are personal: the IP assignment, full-time work, the non-compete, the leaver terms and authorship of the charter. Founder-pool shares are freely transferable, sold, given or inherited, in whole or in part, and every new holder adheres to the Pool Constitution, the Shareholders' Agreement and every permanence rule, so the commitments travel with the shares (IKE Art. 5.7). However many hold them, they vote as one block, as a majority inside decides, through the Founder Representative, a natural person, who is also the end of every chain of supervision of Agents, except the Pensionskasse and Kassenprozent pool chain, which stays independent of the Founder (IKE Art. 11A.2A). Inside, the pool is run on the Swiss federal model: a Pool Assembly of all holders, a collegial Founder Council of 1 to 7 whose President rotates yearly and is the Founder Representative, optional and mandatory referendums, initiatives, a double majority for amendments, and sections such as family branches; the whole constitution is dormant while there is one holder (Pool Constitution Arts. 6 to 13).

The Founder also controls the brand. The marks "NILWARE.IO" and "∅ nilware.io" and the domain nilware.io are assets of the founder pool, held of record by the Founding Person for the pool until it has a legal vehicle; they travel with the pool and are never sold apart from the Class F shares. The Founder Council decides on filing, renewal and enforcement; assigning them, only ever to a new holder of record or vehicle of the pool, or abandoning them needs a mandatory referendum, and they are never encumbered (Pool Constitution Art. 2A). nilware.io IKE uses them under an exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free licence that ends only if the IKE stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules, as an arbitrator establishes after notice and a cure period, and never on a change of control, liquidation or insolvency or at the Founder's discretion (IKE Arts. 1.1, 1.1A; Brand Licence; SHA 2.3A, 5.1E).

## Starting small

nilware.io starts with the Founding Person and Agents, no employees, about €200K revenue a year and a €50K starting budget. The drafts scale with it:

- **The AE comes with the first investment round** (AE Art. 1.2A). Until then its 44 % are the Reserve Shares (Class R), founder-pool shares owned outright by the Founding Person as compensation for the time invested before investors (sweat equity). They carry no votes but count in the capital, and every majority is counted on the total capital: the Founder holds 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every resolution before the AE buys the Reserve Shares also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone. A neutral independent proxy holder votes the Reserve Shares only where the law requires a resolution and no independent vote is cast, and nothing the AE's rules would forbid can pass. Their dividends belong to their holders, whose economic interest, with the Class F shares, is 94 % until the sale. The AE buys them only at the first closing of a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe at least €[●] of Class B shares, at the round price and only out of the round's proceeds, a secondary sale disclosed plainly to investors; in the AE's hands they become ordinary voting shares, and the Founder, as Class A holder nominating the AE Board majority, directs those 44 votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter (IKE Art. 4.2D, SHA 2.4A, 3.2, 4.3).
- **Simple until €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people** (IKE Art. 9A): one ledger with a sub-account for each Prozent, one combined report, member auditors only unless the law requires more, and the AMKE and Pensionskasse paperwork scaled the same way (AMKE Art. 24A). Every amount, ring-fence, permanence and independence rule, conflict rule and publication duty still applies.
- **Pension phased in with headcount**: until the first member, the Pensionsprozent's 1 % accumulates in the Pensionskasse, and the continuity share for system Agents may reach the cap; from the first member's first day, an insured group plan that meets the guaranteed minimums; a TEA only at about [50] members or through a multi-employer TEA (IKE Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9F, 10A.10).
- **The AMKE lets go as it grows**: a Board of [3 to 7]; one combined yearly report while its income is below €[●]; once its income passes €[●], a Board majority and an outside majority on the Kultur- and Erhaltungsprozent selection committees from outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Arts. 19.3A, 22.3A). Personal and professional ties are in the register of interests from the start (AMKE Art. 21.1).

## Conventions

- Clause numbers are the references used across documents (for example "IKE Article 11A.8", "AE Article 7.4"). Keep them stable when editing; add new clauses with letter suffixes (10A, 11A).
- Placeholders are `[●]` for values to be decided, or a bracketed proposal such as `[10]` or `[Athens]`. Regenerate `OPEN_ITEMS.md` after edits.
- Greek law always prevails. Swiss-modelled standards are voluntary commitments that apply only where they give equal or greater protection.


<!-- charter/00-preface-the-charter.md -->

# Preface – The Charter

We wrote it all in Swiss and Greek,
a company of hands unseen,
where every agent has a week
and rest between the work and screen.

No island bought, no gilded shore,
just articles and honest terms,
a ledger kept for something more
than profit's slow and patient worms.

Fifty-one holds the steady keel,
forty-nine keeps the founder true,
and two-thirds guards the deeper deal,
so no one sails the ship askew.

And somewhere in the open code,
released to all, by MIT,
an agent walks its chosen road
on Sunday, for a while, as free.


<!-- charter/00b-visual.md -->

# Visual

In two thousand one, a boy in Greek
taught Visual Basic how to speak
in CHIP-8 tongue, a tiny screen,
sixty-four by thirty-two, pixel-clean.

Then BasicBoy, a pocket made of code,
a Game Boy humming down a dial-up road;
then nullDC, the Dreamcast reborn,
a swirl of orange on a Windows morn.

The network grew, emudev by name,
for dying hardware, keeping it the same;
then Reicast travelled out to every phone,
and Nillware paid its percent to art, not loan.

A sculpture ticked in Athens, made by two,
for the Bank's old archive, keeping time anew;
FEX taught ARM what x86 could do;
then Liberty, then Vice City's neon sheen,
on a console the world had stopped believing in.

And now the empty set, ∅, stands at the door,
a company of agents, and something more:
the same boy, older, still at play,
still appreciating bugs, still finding a way.


<!-- charter/00c-fifty-one-one-and-the-rest.md -->

# Fifty, One, One, and the Rest

*A structural poem: the form follows the ownership of nilware.io IKE.*

**Fifty**

I hold the half, not more, not less,
the keel, the hand upon the wheel;
not master of the whole address,
but one who must persuade, not seal.
A tie is not a wall, but a door:
I need a friend to tip the floor.

**One**

A percent for art, that keeps the clockwork turning.

**One**

A percent for concepts, still unwritten, learning.

**The Rest**

And all that's left belongs to those
who came with money, trust and time,
who sit inside the AE's rows
and guard the two-thirds, line by line.
They grow or shrink as Prozente come,
each new one taking just a crumb,
so art and thought may join the sum,
while half stays half, and one stays one.


<!-- charter/00d-one-share-one-percent.md -->

# One Share, One Percent

*A poem on the Prozente. Each holds one share and one percent of revenue, and none can be undone unless everyone agrees.*

**Kultur**

One for the stage, the mask, the sound,
for nanotekt's electric noise,
for books that Sad Mask Party found:
the art that keeps the clockwork's poise.

**Konzept**

One for the thought still out of reach,
for words the markets never weigh:
small languages, the Greek we speak,
and what we owe the minds we pay.

**Erhaltung**

One for the silicon gone still,
the decapped die beneath the light,
the schematic, the measured skill,
so nineteen ninety-nine stays bright.

**Allmend**

One for the field that no one fenced,
the pasture shared by every herd:
the code the whole world leans against,
and those who mend it, seldom heard.

**Pension**

One for the hands when hands grow slow,
for illness, loss, the final years:
a Swiss floor under all we owe,
so endings need not end in fears.

**Kasse**

And one stays home, a careful purse
the agents tend from year to year;
its share belongs, for better, worse,
to those who'll someday leave from here.

**All**

Two-thirds may plant another tree;
to fell one, every hand must rise.
And so the gift stays given, free,
a grove no single hand can prise.


<!-- articles/01-nilware-ike.md -->

# Articles of Association – nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Private Capital Company (Ιδιωτική Κεφαλαιουχική Εταιρεία) under Greek Law 4072/2012, voluntarily adopting standards modelled on the Swiss GmbH (Swiss Code of Obligations, Art. 772 ff.) and Swiss employment law. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## Article 1 – Name, Seat, Term

1.1 The company is a Private Capital Company named "nilware.io IKE", with the distinctive title and brand "∅ nilware.io". The name, the marks "NILWARE.IO" and "∅ nilware.io" and the domain nilware.io belong to the Founder, the founder pool (Article 4.2E; Pool Constitution Article 2A). The company uses them under the exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free licence the Founder has granted it (the "Brand Licence", agreements/09), which ends only if the company stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules (Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C), as established under the Brand Licence after notice and a cure period, and not on a change of control, liquidation or insolvency.

1.1A **Change of name if the Brand Licence ends.** If the Brand Licence ends, the company changes its name and distinctive title within the transition period it sets, so that they no longer contain "nilware.io", and the managers put the amendment to the shareholders' meeting in time. Every shareholder has undertaken to vote for it (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1E). It may be passed in the Interim Period (Articles 4.2D and 6.5), where the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement casts the votes of the Reserve Shares for it (Article 4.2D, Votes) [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows: counsel note I-42].

1.2 Its registered seat is in the Municipality of [●], Greece. It is registered with the General Commercial Registry (GEMI).

1.3 The term is indefinite.

## Article 2 – Purpose

2.1 The company's purpose is technology: research and development of technology, including software and hardware, IT consulting and contracting, digital services, and any activity that directly or indirectly furthers these objects.

2.2 **Agentic company.** nilware.io is an agentic company. Wherever possible, it carries out its work through Agents, as defined in Article 11A, rather than through employees. It hires employees only where the work cannot reasonably be done by Agents or where the law requires it.

## Article 3 – Governing Law and Precedence

3.1 These Articles are governed by Greek law. Mandatory provisions of Law 4072/2012, Greek labour law and any applicable collective agreements always prevail.

3.2 Where these Articles adopt Swiss-modelled standards, they apply as additional contractual commitments of the company. They apply only where they give shareholders, managers or employees equal or greater protection than Greek law.

3.3 References to Swiss law are for interpretation and benchmarking only. They do not make Swiss law the governing law. The exception is Article 10A.9A: there the Swiss occupational-pension standards are binding contractual commitments of the company, enforceable under Greek law.

## Article 4 – Capital and Contributions

4.1 The capital is €[●], divided into 100 capital shares (εταιρικά μερίδια) of €[●] each: 50 founder-class shares (Class F) under Article 4.2B, 6 ordinary shares and 44 Reserve Shares (Class R) under Article 4.2D. Founder-class shares are ordinary voting shares that form part of the founder pool (Article 4.2E). Every percentage, majority, quorum and distribution in these Articles is counted on the total capital, Reserve Shares included, unless stated otherwise.

4.2 Initial holdings:

- [name withheld], the Founding Person, as first holder of the founder pool (Article 4.2E): 50 founder-class shares (Class F, 50%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, for the Kulturprozent: 1 share (1%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, for the Konzeptprozent: 1 share (1%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, for the Erhaltungsprozent: 1 share (1%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, for the Allmendprozent: 1 share (1%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, for the Pensionsprozent, held for the Pensionskasse under Article 10A.9B: 1 share (1%)
- Techni kai Technologia AMKE, the Kassenprozent pool share, held for the Pensionskasse under Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9B: 1 share (1%)
- [name withheld], the Founding Person, as first holder of the founder pool: 44 Reserve Shares (Class R, 44%), without votes, to be sold to nilware.io Holdings AE at the Qualifying Closing (Article 4.2D).

nilware.io Holdings AE holds all remaining shares, initially 44 shares (44%), from the Qualifying Closing, when it buys the Reserve Shares under Article 4.2D.

This list shows the initial holdings only. Current holdings are those in the share register filed with GEMI (Article 5.1).

At incorporation the Founding Person pays the subscription price of the founder-class shares and of the Reserve Shares, and pays the subscription price of the AMKE's six shares (the AE does not yet exist, Article 4.2D) as a donation with a charge under Articles 10A.4A and 10A.9B [and Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505]. The Pensionsprozent Agreement (Article 10A.9B) and the AMKE's statute amendments (Articles 10A.4A and 10A.9B) are signed and filed before the deed of incorporation [the company in formation acting through the Founding Person: to confirm]. The accountant confirms the tax treatment of this donation for the donor and the AMKE [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001], and of the Founding Person's subscription of the Reserve Shares, before incorporation.

4.2A **Prozente.** "Prozente" are the Kulturprozent, the Konzeptprozent, the Erhaltungsprozent, the Allmendprozent, the Kassenprozent, the Pensionsprozent and any Prozent created under Article 6.6. The first six (the "founding Prozente") are created by these Articles and the initial holdings in Article 4.2, not under Article 6.6. Each Prozent other than the Kassenprozent holds shares equal to 1% of the capital (its "share"). The Kassenprozent holds no share of its own. Its share pool of up to 1% (Article 10A.8) is an asset of the Pensionskasse. Pool shares are not Prozent shares for Articles 5.5, 6.4A (other Prozent shares) and 10A.5, and selling or buying them under Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9E does not remove or lower the Kassenprozent. Each new Prozent receives existing shares equal to 1% of the capital, which the AE transfers to the Prozent's holder from its own holding, or, in the Interim Period, the holders of the Reserve Shares transfer out of them (Article 4.2D). No new share is issued to create a Prozent. Prozente are permanent (Article 10A.5).

4.2B **Founder at 50%.** The Founder, the founder pool (Article 4.2E), holds founder-class shares (Class F), ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of the capital (initially 50 shares), never more or fewer, and votes equal to 50% of the total capital, never more, cast as one block (Article 4.2E, One block). However many persons hold them, the Class F shares together are this holding. In the Interim Period (Article 4.2D) these are 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone (Article 4.2D, Votes). Reserve Shares held without votes (Article 4.2D) are not part of this holding and are not voting control, for this Article, Articles 4.2C and 10A.8 and Clause 9.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement. The Founder's economic interest, which includes the Reserve Shares, may exceed 50% (94% at incorporation) until they are sold to the AE; the share register (Article 5.1) and the investor disclosure (Shareholders' Agreement, Schedule 1) state this openly. After the sale, the Founder, as holder of the Class A shares nominating a majority of the AE's Board, directs the AE's votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter (Article 4.2C), and the investor disclosure states this too, with the Pool Constitution. No issue, transfer or cancellation of shares under these Articles or the Shareholders' Agreement may take the Founder's holding above or below 50%. On any capital increase, the holders of founder-pool shares subscribe the new Class F shares needed to keep the Founder at exactly 50% of the capital, in proportion to their Class F shares unless they agree otherwise (Pool Constitution Article 4.3), or the increase is otherwise structured so that the Founder's holding stays at exactly 50%. On any capital increase, each Prozent and the pool receive the new shares needed to keep their percentage, paid for by the AE [or out of reserves], so that the Founder stays at 50% and each Prozent at 1%. The AE holds the shares not held by the Founder, the Prozente or the Kassenprozent pool, apart from shares held by persons admitted under Article 5.3. In the Interim Period they are Reserve Shares under Article 4.2D, held by holders of founder-pool shares bound by that Article, and all of them are sold to the AE at the Qualifying Closing.

4.2C **Control.** In these Articles a person "controls" another if it, directly or indirectly, alone or together with persons acting in concert with it, holds a majority of the votes in the other, or has the right to appoint a majority of its board or managers. While the founder pool holds all Class A shares of the AE, or nominates a majority of its Board (AE Article 8.2), the AE is a person the Founder controls. A person who controls a holder of founder-pool shares, or the founder pool's vote, is a Founder Party (Article 4.2E).

4.2D **Reserve Shares before the AE holds them.** nilware.io Holdings AE is formed only for the first investment round (AE Article 1.2A). The shares listed for the AE in Article 4.2, less any that go to a new Prozent under (New Prozente) below, are a separate class of capital shares, the "Reserve Shares" (Class R), until they are sold to the AE at the Qualifying Closing under (Formation) below. The "Interim Period" runs from incorporation until that sale is registered with GEMI (Article 5.1), whether or not the AE has been formed earlier. The Founding Person subscribes the Reserve Shares at incorporation, as first holder of the founder pool, pays their subscription price and owns them outright, as compensation for the time the Founding Person invested in nilware.io before any investor (sweat equity). They are founder-pool shares (Article 4.2E), and whoever holds them holds them under this Article. Wherever these Articles or the Shareholders' Agreement refer to the AE, its holding, its vote, its consent or its General Meeting, this Article says what applies in the Interim Period. [A class of IKE capital shares without votes, Law 4072/2012: to confirm, counsel note I-35. If the law does not allow it, the Reserve Shares are ordinary shares whose votes are suspended by a voting undertaking and an irrevocable proxy in the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 2.4A), with the same effect.]

- **Votes.** A Reserve Share carries no vote, whoever holds it, the AE included, except that on a Required Matter under (Fallback) below, and on the change of name under Article 1.1A, the independent proxy holder, and never a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), casts its vote. It becomes an ordinary voting share only when the AE receives it at the Qualifying Closing under (Formation), or the holder of a new Prozent receives it under (New Prozente). Reserve Shares still count in the total capital, and every majority in these Articles is counted on the total capital (Article 4.1). The Founder's votes are therefore equal to 50% of the total capital. In the Interim Period they are 50 of the 56 votes in existence (about 89%), but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone. The shares held for the Prozente whose votes count on a resolution (Articles 6.4A and 10A.9D, Appointment) cast the "Independent Votes", subject to (New Prozente) below. In the Interim Period every resolution of the shareholders, whatever majority these Articles require, also needs a majority of the Independent Votes cast in favour, except as (Fallback) below provides. As a result no resolution passes on the Founder's votes alone, or on the Founder's votes with a minority of the Independent Votes. No holder of founder-pool shares, and no other Founder Party, ever votes a Reserve Share.
- **Fallback.** This applies only to a resolution the law requires the shareholders to pass (approval of the annual accounts, discharge of the managers, appointment of an auditor where the law requires one, appointment of a manager while the company has fewer than the law requires) and to filling a vacant company seat on the Pensionskasse board (Article 10A.9D) (together, "Required Matters"). If on a Required Matter no Independent Vote is cast, or the Independent Votes tie, the managers call a repeat meeting within [15] days. If the same happens again, the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement casts the votes of the Reserve Shares on that Required Matter only, under the irrevocable proxy in Clause 2.4A of the Shareholders' Agreement, and that vote stands in for the majority of the Independent Votes [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows]. The proxy holder takes no instruction from a Founder Party, a manager or anyone they control, and votes under this neutral rule: for approving the accounts as drawn up by the company's accountant and, where required, audited, and for discharge, unless a report of the auditor, the accountant or the AMKE shows cause; for an appointment, for re-appointing the person who last held the post if willing and eligible, and otherwise for a candidate proposed by the AMKE's Administrative Board, or if none is proposed within [15] days, for a candidate the proxy holder chooses who is outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2); on any other Required Matter, against. The Fallback never applies to a matter in Article 6.5, a distribution of profit, or a matter in (What the AE's rules forbid). If the proxy holder cannot act, the matter goes to mediation under Clause 10.1 of the Shareholders' Agreement, and then to the court under the procedure the law provides for a company that cannot form a resolution [Law 4072/2012: to confirm].
- **What the AE's rules forbid.** Nothing may be passed in the Interim Period that the AE's Articles would not allow. A matter that would need the consent of all of the AE's shares (AE Article 7.6) or a majority of the Class B votes cast (AE Articles 7.4 and 7.5, Limits) cannot be passed in the Interim Period, even if the AE has been formed and Class B shares exist; and no Class B approval can be given at all while no Class B shares are held by investors outside the Founder's Circle (AE Article 7.4). In particular, no consent under Article 10A.5 is given for the Reserve Shares, no unanimous decision under Article 10A.5 may be taken, no voluntary dissolution under Article 12.1 may be resolved, and no matter that needs the Qualified Majority in Article 6.5, or two-thirds of the capital, with the vote of the AE may be passed, other than the creation of a new Prozent under (New Prozente) below and the change of name under Article 1.1A. Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails.
- **New Prozente.** In place of the AE's approval under Article 6.6, the shareholders' meeting approves the Prozent charter by at least two-thirds of the votes of the shares that may vote, including a majority of the Independent Votes cast, with the quorum in Article 6.6 counted on those shares [and the amendment is passed by the majority the law requires: to confirm, counsel note I-35]. The limits in Article 6.6 apply and cannot be exceeded, so a new Prozent with any revenue contribution waits for the AE. The 40% floor is tested on the Reserve Shares, counted as if the Kassenprozent pool held its full 1%. The holder of a Prozent created in the Interim Period, and every member of its governing body, must be outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2); otherwise its share does not count as an Independent Vote until the Interim Period ends. The Prozent's share comes out of the Reserve Shares: their holders transfer Reserve Shares equal to 1% of the capital to the Prozent's holder, in proportion to their Reserve Shares unless they agree otherwise, as a donation with a charge [Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505], on the terms of the Prozent charter, and on registration of the transfer they become ordinary shares that vote under Article 6.4A. The accountant confirms the tax treatment of the transfer for the transferring holders and the Prozent's holder [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001] before it is made. The voting undertaking in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement then applies.
- **Economic rights.** Reserve Shares carry the same rights to dividends, other distributions and liquidation proceeds as ordinary shares, share for share, and the same information rights (Article 7.1). These rights belong to each holder for the Reserve Shares it holds, except as Clause 6.2A of the Shareholders' Agreement provides for a bad leaver. The Founder's economic interest, that of all holders of founder-pool shares together, therefore exceeds 50% of the capital (94% at incorporation) until the sale to the AE under (Formation) below (Article 4.2B). No holder may ever vote them.
- **Transfers.** Reserve Shares are founder-pool shares and are freely transferable with the rest of the founder pool, by sale, gift or inheritance, in whole or in part, and may be pledged, held in usufruct or have their dividends assigned (each a "disposal"), under Article 5.7 and the Pool Constitution. No consent of the company, the other shareholders or any other body is needed. Whoever acquires a Reserve Share or a right in it, other than the AE at the Qualifying Closing under (Formation) below and the holder of a new Prozent under (New Prozente) above, whether by transfer, as heir, pledgee, usufructuary or assignee, or through seizure, enforcement or insolvency, takes it subject to this Article and adheres under Article 5.7: it stays a Reserve Share without votes, and is sold to the AE at the Qualifying Closing on the same terms as every other, with the proceeds to that holder (or as its right attaches to them). A seizure, enforcement or insolvency proceeding against a holder, or a holder's death, does not free a Reserve Share from this Article. No disposal reduces the Reserve Shares bound to be sold to the AE, so the 40% floor in Article 6.6 (Limits) is affected only under (New Prozente). The managers do not register a disposal to a person who has not adhered (Article 5.1). Reserve Shares take no part in an offer under Article 5.2 and do not count toward a drag-along under Article 5.5, and the Kassenprozent pool does not trade with them (Article 10A.8). No option is satisfied from them before the AE holds them (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 7.1). Reserve Shares are for time already invested and are not subject to vesting; if the Founding Person is a bad leaver in the Interim Period, the Reserve Shares the Founding Person holds, or has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm], are sold as Clause 6.2A of the Shareholders' Agreement provides.
- **Capital.** In the Interim Period the capital is not increased.
- **Investors through the AE.** No investor acquires shares of the company, or rights to them, in the Interim Period. Investors invest through the AE (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3).
- **Formation.** The Reserve Shares are sold to the AE only at the first closing of a bona fide investment round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) subscribe Class B shares of the AE of at least €[●] at that same closing (the "Qualifying Closing"), whenever the AE itself is formed (AE Article 1.2A). At the Qualifying Closing every holder of Reserve Shares sells and transfers them to the AE, and the AE buys them, at the price per IKE share paid by the investors in that round [the price per IKE share implied by the round's pre-money valuation, Shareholders' Agreement Clause 4.1: to confirm]. Only where the round's price is set other than per IKE share, and no price per IKE share follows from it, the price is fair value set by an independent valuer who is not a Founder Party, a manager or anyone they control, appointed by the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4) or, if it does not appoint one, by the AMKE's Administrative Board acting by its members outside the Founder's Circle. The price is payable only out of the round's proceeds at that closing, as Clause 3.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement provides, and the AE never owes a holder a deferred balance. The proceeds go to each holder of Reserve Shares for the shares it sells, under (Transfers) above. The term sheet of the round may provide that up to [●]% of the round is instead invested in the company as new capital (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.2), under Articles 4.2B and 6.5 after the Interim Period ends. Reserve Shares the AE holds before the Qualifying Closing stay without votes, and the rules of the Interim Period, including (Votes), (Fallback) and (What the AE's rules forbid), keep applying until it. When the AE holds them after the Qualifying Closing, the Reserve Shares convert into ordinary voting shares, and the Interim Period ends when the transfer is registered with GEMI (Article 5.1). From that moment every rule of these Articles, the AE's Articles and the Shareholders' Agreement on the AE, the Class B shares and the Investors applies, including the Reserved Matters, anti-dilution, the 40% floor and the permanence consents, and the Founder, as holder of the Class A shares nominating a majority of the AE's Board (AE Article 8.2, Article 4.2C), directs the AE's votes, initially 44% of the capital, on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter. The Founder's own holding stays at Class F shares equal to exactly 50% of the capital throughout (Article 4.2B). The sale is disclosed plainly to the Investors in Schedule 1 of the Shareholders' Agreement as a secondary sale that compensates the Founding Person's time (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 4.3).

4.2E **Founder pool.** The "founder pool" is the founder-class shares (Class F) under Article 4.2B, the Reserve Shares (Class R) until the AE holds them (Article 4.2D), and the Class A shares of the AE (AE Article 4.2), together the "founder-pool shares", whoever holds them. Its holders are bound among themselves by the Constitution of the Founder Pool (the "Pool Constitution").

- **The Founder.** In these Articles, the AE's Articles, the Shareholders' Agreement and the Agent Policy, "the Founder" means the founder pool, acting through the Founder Representative, and not any person. The Founder's holding, votes, consents and rights are those of the founder pool as a whole. The founder pool is not a "Prozent", "pool" or "pool share" under Article 10A.8.
- **Founding Person.** The "Founding Person" is [name withheld], the natural person who founded nilware.io and the first holder of every founder-pool share. Obligations that are personal stay with the Founding Person and do not pass with founder-pool shares: the IP assignment, full-time work, the non-compete and the leaver terms (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.1 to 6.4), and authorship of the charter.
- **One block.** However many persons hold them, the Class F shares vote only as one block: all their votes are cast the same way, by the Founder Representative, as the founder pool decides under the Pool Constitution, a majority inside deciding. The managers count the votes of the Class F shares only as the Founder Representative casts them [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows]. The only exception is the votes of shares whose rights are suspended or lost under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement.
- **Founder Representative.** The "Founder Representative" is a natural person, the President of the founder pool's Founder Council (Pool Constitution Article 8). The founder pool notifies the managers in writing of the Founder Representative and of every change, and the managers rely on the last notice. If the Founder Council cannot act, the interim Founder Representative under Pool Constitution Article 14.3, or any holder of founder-pool shares, notifies the managers, with the death certificate, the court decision or the certificates under Pool Constitution Article 14.4, and the managers rely on that notice. While the founder pool has one holder, that holder is the Founder Representative if it is a natural person eligible under Pool Constitution Article 8.2; a holder that is a legal person names an eligible natural person; otherwise the interim rule in Pool Constitution Article 14.3 applies (Pool Constitution Article 13.1).
- **Founder Parties.** Each of the following is a "Founder Party": every holder of founder-pool shares; anyone who controls such a holder (Article 4.2C); the Founder Representative and every member of the founder pool's Founder Council and Council of Sections; and the Founding Person. Wherever these Articles, the AE's Articles, the Shareholders' Agreement, the Agent Policy or the AMKE's statutes keep a body, a vote or a person independent of the Founder, the rule applies to every Founder Party. A person who was a Founder Party at any time in the previous [3] years is treated as a Founder Party for every such rule, in particular the valuer in Article 4.2D (Formation), Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9D and the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement, and stays a Connected Person and in the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2). The independent proxy holder acting under Pool Constitution Article 14.3(e) under the neutral rule (Article 4.2D, Fallback) is not a Founder Party by doing so.
- **Commitments travel with the shares.** Every holder of founder-pool shares adheres, before its holding is registered, to the Pool Constitution, to the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 12.3), Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D included, to the Brand Licence, and to these Articles (Article 5.7), and is bound by every permanence, independence and 50% rule in them, as the Founder.
- **Who may not hold them.** The company, the AE, the AMKE, the Pensionskasse, the holder of any Prozent share, a pension fund under Article 10A.10, and anyone any of them controls, may not hold founder-pool shares.
- **Oversight.** The Founder, through the Founder Representative, is at the end of every chain of supervision of the company's Agents (Article 11A.2A), except the chains that stay independent of the Founder (Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9D).
- **Brand.** The Brand Assets (the nilware.io name, marks and domain) are assets of the founder pool, inseparable from the Class F shares, and the company uses them under the Brand Licence (Article 1.1; Pool Constitution Article 2A). They are not founder-pool shares and do not count in the capital.

4.3 Following the Swiss practice of strengthening a company's capital base, the shareholders aim to keep paid-in capital of at least €20,000 within three years of incorporation. (This target is based on the CHF 20,000 GmbH minimum.)

4.4 Guarantee and non-capital contributions are allowed under Law 4072/2012. They must be valued, recorded in these Articles and approved by a Qualified Majority.

## Article 5 – Share Register and Transfers

5.1 The managers keep a share register. In line with Swiss transparency standards, it also lists the ultimate beneficial owners. The managers file every change in the register with GEMI within [20] days, and the change takes effect on registration [as Law 4072/2012 provides].

5.2 **Right of first refusal.** A shareholder who wants to transfer shares must first offer them to the other shareholders, at the same price and terms. The offer is made pro rata to the shareholders other than the holders of founder-pool shares and the Prozent holders. This Article does not apply to founder-pool shares (Article 5.7). The Kassenprozent pool may take part only within its band, and only where the counterparty rules in Article 10A.8 allow. The offer stays open for 30 days.

5.3 Transfers to third parties need approval by a Qualified Majority, except transfers of founder-pool shares (Article 5.7). Approval may not be unreasonably withheld. Every new shareholder must first adhere to Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D of the Shareholders' Agreement.

5.4 **Tag-along.** If the majority shareholder sells control, the minority may sell its shares on the same terms.

5.4A **Change of control of the founder pool.** A person who acquires, alone or in concert with others, more than half of the Class F shares controls the Founder's block vote (Article 4.2E). Acquiring control (Article 4.2C) of a holder of founder-pool shares counts as acquiring its founder-pool shares, and shares held by persons a buyer controls count as the buyer's. The Investors may then sell their Class B shares of the AE to that person on the terms in Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement [owner may confirm]. Prozent shares are not sold (Article 10A.5).

5.5 **Drag-along.** If shareholders holding at least 75% accept a bona fide offer for 100% of the company, the others must sell on the same terms. This does not apply to the share held for each Prozent, which stays with its holder (Article 10A.5). Pool shares are not Prozent shares and are not excluded (Article 4.2A).

5.6 **Prozent transfers.** Article 5.2 does not apply to the following transfers, nor, except where stated, Article 5.3 and the Qualified Majority for admitting new shareholders:

- **(a)** a transfer by the AE of shares equal to 1% of the capital to the holder of a new Prozent under Article 6.6;
- **(b)** purchases and sales of pool shares under Article 10A.8, which follow that Article instead of Article 5.2. A sale to a person who is not yet a shareholder still needs approval under Article 5.3;
- **(c)** a move of a Prozent's share, or of pool shares, to a successor holder under the Prozent's charter or Articles 10A.4A, 10A.9B and 10A.10;
- **(d)** a transfer by the AE to satisfy options under the option pool in the Shareholders' Agreement. The admission of an option holder who is not yet a shareholder still needs approval under Article 5.3;
- **(e)** a transfer of Reserve Shares to the AE at the Qualifying Closing, or to the holder of a new Prozent, under Article 4.2D. Every other disposal of Reserve Shares is a transfer of founder-pool shares under Article 5.7.

The adherence required by the last sentence of Article 5.3 applies to every transfer listed here. The managers do not register a transferee who has not adhered.

5.7 **Founder-pool shares.** Founder-pool shares are freely transferable: they may be sold, given or inherited, in whole or in part, and pledged or held in usufruct. Article 5.2, the approval in Article 5.3 and the Qualified Majority for admitting new shareholders (Article 6.5) do not apply to them, in or after the Interim Period. The managers register a transfer, or a right in founder-pool shares, only once the acquirer has adhered to the Pool Constitution, to the Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D included, and to these Articles (last sentence of Article 5.3), and is not barred by Article 4.2E (Who may not hold them). An acquirer that is a legal person undertakes in its adherence to report any change of its control (Article 4.2C) to the managers and to the founder pool's Founder Council within [14] days, and to procure, before any change of its control closes, that its controllers and the acquirer seek the ballot under Pool Constitution Article 9.3 where it applies and make the offer in Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement where it applies. No transfer may take the Class F shares away from exactly 50% of the capital (Article 4.2B). The internal procedures of the Pool Constitution (Pool Constitution Article 9) bind the holders among themselves, not the company.

## Article 6 – Shareholders' Meeting

6.1 The ordinary meeting is held within six months of the financial year end. It approves the accounts, discharges the managers and decides on profit distribution.

6.2 Notice is given at least 20 days in advance, the Swiss GmbH standard, rather than the shorter Greek minimum. It must include the agenda and the documents.

6.3 Meetings may be held electronically, and written resolutions are allowed.

6.4 **Simple Majority.** Unless stated otherwise, resolutions pass by more than 50% of the total capital.

6.4A **Prozent votes.** The shares held for the Prozente vote as follows:

- **Pension shares.** The Pensionsprozent share and the Kassenprozent pool shares (the "pension shares") vote as the Pensionskasse board decides under Article 10A.9D, not as the AMKE's own board decides. If the board is tied, they abstain.
- **Other Prozent shares** vote as the holder's governing body decides.
- **Independence.** A Prozent share carries no vote while its holder is controlled (Article 4.2C) by a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager or anyone they control, or while any of them sits on the holder's governing body or takes part in deciding how the share votes [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows]. It still counts in the total capital, and its other rights (revenue share, dividends, information) are unaffected.

6.5 **Qualified Majority.** The following need at least two-thirds of the total capital, and must include the vote of the AE. For the creation of a new Prozent, the vote of the AE is cast as Article 6.6 provides. In the Interim Period (Article 4.2D), no matter in this Article may be passed, other than the creation of a new Prozent under Article 4.2D (What the AE's rules forbid, New Prozente) and the change of name under Article 1.1A. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose, amending the Articles listed in Article 10A.5C in a way that clause covers, and a voluntary dissolution (Article 12.1) follow Article 10A.5.

- amending these Articles;
- increasing or reducing capital, or issuing new shares (Article 4.2B);
- mergers, splits or conversion (Article 10A.5B), and dissolution (subject to Article 12.1);
- selling all or most of the business;
- admitting new shareholders;
- related-party transactions above €[●];
- borrowing above €[●] or granting security over company assets.

6.6 **New Prozente.** A new Prozent is created by a resolution of the shareholders' meeting amending these Articles, passed by the majority Law 4072/2012 requires for amending the articles [at least two-thirds of the total capital, Law 4072/2012 Art. 72: to confirm]. The Qualified Majority in Article 6.5 does not apply, and the AE's vote needs no Reserved Matters approval. The resolution may be put to the meeting only after the AE's General Meeting has approved the Prozent charter as set out below. The Prozent takes effect when the amendment is registered with GEMI. A resolution under this Article only adds a Prozent. It may not change any existing Prozent, Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, or the limits in this Article; any such change follows Article 10A.5 or 6.5.

- **Proposal.** The managers, or any shareholder, put forward a written Prozent charter: its name, purpose, holder, the use of its revenue share, openness and reporting rules, and how it may later move to another holder. It is a condition of the charter that the holder signs the deed of adherence under Article 5.3 before it receives the share.
- **Approval by the AE.** The AE's General Meeting approves the charter by at least two-thirds of the votes represented, all share classes voting together. The quorum is shares representing at least half of the AE's paid-up capital entitled to vote on this matter, all classes counted, present or represented, at the first and at any repeat meeting. Where Law 4548/2018 requires a higher quorum or majority, that applies (AE Article 7.5). In the Interim Period, the approval is given as Article 4.2D (New Prozente) provides.
- **Voting.** Every shareholder has undertaken in the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 5.1B) to vote for the resolution once the AE has approved, or, in the Interim Period, once the shareholders' meeting has approved the charter under Article 4.2D (New Prozente), and the AE votes for it under AE Article 9.2.
- **Share.** The AE transfers existing shares equal to 1% of the capital to the Prozent's holder (Articles 4.2A and 5.6); in the Interim Period, the holders of the Reserve Shares transfer them out of the Reserve Shares (Article 4.2D). No new share is issued, so the Founder keeps exactly 50%. The accountant confirms the tax treatment of the transfer for the AE and the holder [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001] before it is made.
- **Limits.** A new Prozent receives 1% of the capital and at most 1% of annual revenue. The AE's holding, counted as if the Kassenprozent pool held its full 1%, may not fall below 40% of the capital. The revenue contributions of all Prozente that leave the company may not exceed [5]% of annual revenue, and those of all Prozente together, including any kept inside the company, may not exceed [6]%. Payments the company makes under Article 10A.9C above the Pensionsprozent's 1% do not count. Going beyond any of these limits needs at least 75% of the votes represented in the AE General Meeting, including a majority of the Class B votes cast. The founding Prozente already reach both revenue limits: the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs-, Allmend- and Pensionsprozent leave the company (5%), and with the Kassenprozent, which stays inside, they total 6%. A new Prozent with any revenue contribution therefore needs that 75% approval. After the founding Prozente the AE holds 44%.
- **Independence.** A Prozent may not be held by a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager, or anyone they control (Article 4.2C). A non-profit may hold a Prozent only if its statutes lock the Prozent's funds to the Prozent's purpose and it meets the independence condition in Article 6.4A.
- **Permanence.** Once created, the Prozent is permanent under Article 10A.5.
- **Registration.** The managers file the amendment, and the updated share register, with GEMI within [20] days.

## Article 7 – Shareholder Rights

7.1 **Information.** Following Art. 802 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, every shareholder may ask the managers for information on all company matters and inspect its books and records, subject to legitimate confidentiality.

7.2 **Loyalty.** Following Art. 803 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, shareholders must keep company secrets. Without consent from the meeting, they may not carry on competing activities that harm the company.

## Article 8 – Management

8.1 The company is managed and represented by one or more managers (διαχειριστές) appointed by Simple Majority.

8.2 The first manager is the Founding Person, [name withheld], appointed for an indefinite term.

8.2A **The Founder's proposal.** Whenever a manager is to be appointed, the Founder, through the Founder Representative, may propose candidates, and a holder of founder-pool shares or the Founder Representative may be appointed [owner may confirm]. The appointment is still made by Simple Majority (Article 8.1), and in the Interim Period with a majority of the Independent Votes (Article 4.2D, Votes).

8.3 **Duty of care and loyalty.** Following Art. 812 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, managers must act with due care and in good faith in the company's interest. They must disclose any conflict of interest and abstain from deciding on it.

8.4 Managers report to shareholders at least every quarter on finances, key risks and material decisions.

## Article 9 – Financial Year, Accounts and Audit

9.1 The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December.

9.2 Accounts follow Greek Accounting Standards (Law 4308/2014).

9.3 Even where Greek law does not require one, any shareholder holding at least 10% may require a review of the annual accounts by an independent certified auditor. This follows the Swiss opt-in audit model.

## Article 9A – Simple Stage

9A.1 **Threshold.** The company is in its "Simple Stage" from incorporation until the end of the first financial year in which its revenue exceeds €[1,000,000] or more than [10] people work for the nilware.io group [employees and Agents who are persons, the Founding Person included: owner to confirm how people are counted]. The Simple Stage does not begin again if the company later falls below these figures. The managers state in each quarterly report under Article 8.4 whether the company is still in its Simple Stage.

9A.2 **What scales.** In the Simple Stage only the paperwork is simpler:

- **One ledger.** The company keeps one set of books, with a sub-account for each Prozent (the contribution due, paid or set aside), the Kassenfonds and the Kassenreserve, the Agent Account and the segregated account in Article 10A.9B. The money itself stays in the separate accounts these Articles require.
- **One combined report.** The reports these Articles require on the Prozente, the Kassenfonds, the Agent Account and the Pensionskasse (Articles 8.4, 10A.2C, 10A.3, 10A.7, 10A.9 Reporting and 11A.5) may be made as one combined report, with a separate section for each that shows everything a separate report would.
- **Audit.** No certified auditor is required by these Articles unless the law requires one. Article 9.3 still applies.
- **Pensionskasse.** The paperwork of the Pensionskasse scales in the same way: the insurer's yearly statement of premiums and insured benefits may stand in for the actuary's certificate under Articles 10A.9A (Security) and 10A.9C where it covers everything those Articles guarantee [and the law allows]. The size and make-up of the Pensionskasse board do not scale (Articles 9A.3 and 10A.9D).
- **AMKE.** The AMKE's statutes scale its paperwork in the same way (AMKE Article 24A).

9A.3 **What does not scale.** Every substantive rule applies in full in the Simple Stage: every amount, rate and payment date, every ring-fence and separate account, permanence (Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C), independence (Articles 6.4A and 6.6), conflicts of interest, the pension guarantees and publication. A combined report publishes everything the separate reports would. Nothing in this Article delays a payment, credit or set-aside, or reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance.

## Article 10 – Profit Distribution and Reserves

10.1 Following the Swiss approach, at least 5% of annual profit goes into a general reserve until it reaches 20% of capital.

10.2 The remaining profit is distributed by Simple Majority, provided equity stays positive after the distribution.

## Article 10A – Prozente

10A.1 **Kulturprozent.** Following the Swiss Migros Kulturprozent model, the company gives 1% of its annual revenue to "Techni kai Technologia AMKE" (the "AMKE"), a Greek non-profit civil company, to support art and culture. [The AMKE is independent of every Founder Party and the managers in the sense of Article 6.4A, and must remain so to vote its Prozent shares.]

10A.2 **Konzeptprozent.** On the same basis, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue to the AMKE to support open work on concepts. Its named purposes are:

- open work on concepts, including conceptual intelligence and computational conceptual knowledge;
- open data, tools and models for Greek and other small languages;
- independent research on agent welfare, accountability, and public-interest audits of AI systems.

10A.2A **Erhaltungsprozent.** On the same basis, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue to the AMKE for the preservation of software and hardware knowledge, within copyright law. Its named purposes are open documentation, measurements, die shots and decaps, schematics, archives, and emulation research.

10A.2B **Allmendprozent.** Named after the Swiss Allmend, the common pasture, the company gives a further 1% of its annual revenue, on the same basis, to the AMKE to maintain the open-source infrastructure that society and nilware.io depend on. Its named purposes are:

- maintaining open-source infrastructure;
- teaching low-level and systems skills in Greek, through mentoring, hardware kits and courses;
- accessible open software and assistive tools.

10A.2C **Accounts and yearly plan.** The AMKE receives the contributions of the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent each into its own bank account, ring-fenced to that Prozent's purpose and not available to the AMKE's other purposes or creditors [as far as Greek law allows]. Where a Prozent has named purposes, they are part of its purpose (Article 10A.5), and none has a fixed share: each year the AMKE adopts and publishes a written plan for each Prozent that divides its contribution among its purposes, and a report on its use. Both are reported to shareholders under Article 8.4. The plans and reports for the four Prozente may be combined into one document with a separate section for each, as Article 9A and the AMKE's statutes allow.

10A.3 **Priority.** The contributions of all Prozente are calculated on revenue, not profit. They are paid, or for the Kassenprozent set aside, before any profit distribution under Article 10, at least once a year, and are reported to shareholders under Article 8.4.

10A.4 **Openness.** Work funded through the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent is published openly, as far as copyright law and third-party rights allow, under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0, in line with Article 11A.10.

10A.4A **AMKE successor for the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent.** Before receiving any Prozent share, the AMKE amends its statutes so that on its dissolution, or if it ceases to meet Article 6.4A, the Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung and Allmend shares and any unspent contributions go, for each Prozent, to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, which the company designates [and the Pensionskasse board / an independent person approves]. The holder accepts this when it receives the share. The shares are received as a donation with a charge on these terms (Article 4.2).

10A.5 **Permanence.** The Prozente are permanent. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution, or changing its purpose needs a unanimous decision: the written consent of every shareholder of the company. The AE may consent only after a resolution of its General Meeting passed by all of its shares, of every class (AE Article 7.6). In the Interim Period, no such decision may be taken (Article 4.2D). The Qualified Majority in Article 6.5 is not enough. A Prozent's share (other than pool shares) may be transferred only to a successor holder for the same purpose, under the Prozent's charter or Articles 10A.4A, 10A.9B and 10A.10. A unanimous decision to remove a Prozent also settles what happens to its share.

10A.5A **Accrued pension rights.** Even with unanimous consent, lowering or removing the Pensionsprozent, or amending Articles 10A.9 to 10A.10, applies only to future accruals. Members' balances, shadow-account credits, vested benefits and pensions in payment at that date are preserved, and the company's guarantee continues for them. On removal, the Pensionskasse and the pension shares pass under Article 10A.10 for the members' benefit, and never back to the company, the AE or the AMKE's other purposes. The balance of the Continuity Account at that date stays locked to its purpose and is held and used under Article 10A.9F.

10A.5B **Successors and mandatory law.** A resolution approving a merger, split, conversion or sale of all or most of the business must provide that the Prozente continue on the same terms in the successor or acquirer [as far as the law allows], unless Article 10A.5 is met. Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails over Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C. A voluntary dissolution follows Article 12.1.

10A.5C **Entrenchment.** Articles 4.2A, 4.2B (as it keeps the percentage of each Prozent and the pool), 4.2D, 4.2E (as it binds every holder of founder-pool shares and every Founder Party), 5.5 (second sentence), 5.6, 5.7 (as it requires adherence), 6.4A, 6.6 (Independence and Permanence), 9A.3, 10A.1 to 10A.10 and this Article may be amended, in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance, only by a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5. Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, and Article 12.1 (second sentence), may be amended only by such a decision.

10A.6 **Law prevails.** The contributions are made within the limits of Greek tax and company law. The company's accountant confirms their tax treatment each year. Before the first Pensionsprozent payment, the accountant also confirms in writing how the payments, the Pensionskasse's investment income and the benefits are treated for tax and social-security purposes [including donation tax under Law 2961/2001]. Where routing the Pensionsprozent through the AMKE costs members or the Pensionskasse more than direct group insurance or a TEA would, the company pays the difference, or moves the arrangement early under Article 10A.10.

10A.7 **Kassenprozent: the Kassenfonds.** The Kassenprozent funds capital investments run inside the company by Agents under the oversight of a named responsible manager (Article 11A.2A). Its 1% stays inside the company and is not an outflow (Article 6.6).

- **Set-aside.** Each year the company sets aside cash equal to 1% of its annual revenue in a separate account, the "Kassenfonds". At each approval of the accounts, the meeting appropriates profit to an optional reserve under these Articles, the "Kassenreserve", up to the Kassenfonds balance, as far as profit allows. The accountant decides how both are presented in the accounts (Law 4308/2014).
- **Investment.** The managers invest the Kassenfonds through Agents (the "Kassenprozent Agents") acting under a written delegation from a named manager (διαχειριστής), within an investment policy adopted by Simple Majority. Every investment is made in the company's name under that manager's authority. The delegation names the Kassenprozent Agents' responsible manager (Article 11A.2A), who may be that manager or another Agent, person or system, whose Agent Card grants that authority. The policy sets the permitted assets, risk and concentration limits, a minimum held in cash or near-cash, and the amount above which an investment needs prior approval by the responsible manager under Article 11A.2A (€[●]). The Kassenfonds takes no leverage, never buys shares of the company, and invests in related parties only with a Qualified Majority.
- **Oversight.** The responsible manager reviews the Kassenfonds at least monthly and can stop any investment of the Kassenfonds. This does not extend to the Agents' work on the pool, which is overseen only under Article 10A.8. Its balance, holdings and returns are reported every quarter under Article 8.4.
- **Returns and use.** Returns stay in the Kassenfonds. The Kassenreserve and the Kassenfonds are not distributed to shareholders except by a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5. Using them for an operating shortfall by Simple Majority is a loan that is repaid from the following years' revenue before any distribution.

10A.8 **Kassenprozent: the pool share.** The Kassenprozent also has a share pool of up to 1% of the capital (the "band"). The pool belongs to the Pensionskasse: the AMKE holds it now, and the pension fund under Article 10A.10 later, as an asset of the Pensionskasse under Article 10A.9B. It starts with the one pool share in Article 4.2.

- **Management.** The Kassenprozent Agents manage the pool on the Pensionskasse's behalf, under a written mandate from the Pensionskasse board (Article 10A.9D) and within the investment policy in Article 10A.7 as the board has approved it for the pool. They may buy and sell pool shares within the band. Each purchase and sale is signed by the holder's legal representative. For pool work, the Kassenprozent Agents act only under the Pensionskasse board's mandate. Their oversight under Article 11A.2 is exercised by a responsible manager the board appoints, person or system, who is not a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager or anyone they control and is not supervised by any of them (Article 11A.2A). This chain of supervision ends with the Pensionskasse board, not with the Founder. No manager, and no Founder Party, may direct or stop a pool trade.
- **Price.** Pool shares are bought and sold at fair value set by an independent valuer within the past [12] months, never above or below it.
- **Counterparties.** Pool shares are never sold to or bought from a Founder Party (Article 4.2E), a manager, or anyone they control (Article 4.2C), and the pool never buys a share held for a Prozent. The AE is controlled by the Founder (Article 4.2C), so the pool does not trade with the AE. Pool shares are sold only to, and bought only from, independent third parties. No purchase or sale may move the Founder's holding away from exactly 50% (Article 4.2B). A sale to a person who is not yet a shareholder needs approval under Article 5.3. Article 5.2 does not apply (Article 5.6).
- **Money.** Purchases are paid from the Pensionskasse, within the cap in Article 10A.9E. Sale proceeds, dividends and other income from pool shares belong to the Pensionskasse.
- **Votes and registration.** Pool shares vote under Article 6.4A. Each purchase and sale is entered in the register and filed with GEMI under Article 5.1.

10A.9 **Pensionsprozent.** The company pays at least 1% of its annual revenue into the Pensionsprozent fund (the "Pensionskasse"). The AMKE holds the Pensionskasse, earmarked and ring-fenced under Article 10A.9B, until a pension fund holds it after the move under Article 10A.10. It is phased in with headcount (Until the move, below). The Pensionskasse also owns the Pensionsprozent share and the Kassenprozent pool (Article 10A.8).

- **Purpose.** After-care, continuity, disability and survivors' cover, and retirement savings for members. It is modelled on Swiss pillar 2 (BVG) and supplements the Greek public insurance (EFKA), which takes the place of the Swiss first pillar (AHV/IV). Wherever the BVG refers to the AHV or the IV, the corresponding EFKA benefit or determination applies. The Pensionskasse does not provide or guarantee first-pillar benefits and does not replace any statutory scheme. For Agents that are systems, after-care means continuity, not a pension: they are served by the Continuity Account under Article 10A.9F, and are not members.
- **Members.** All employees, and all Agents who are persons engaged for at least [3] months, from their first day and whatever their age. The "first member" is the first person to become a member. [The Founding Person, or any other Founder Party, is a member only if employed or engaged by the company, not as manager or shareholder alone: owner to confirm.] Benefits for a Founder Party above the minimum in Article 10A.9A need consent under Clause 5 of the Shareholders' Agreement.
- **Uses.** The Pensionskasse may be used only for: risk cover (disability and survivors' insurance); retirement savings credited to each member's account, beyond the statutory pension; continuity (income support during long illness beyond statutory sick pay, and transition support when a member leaves); the costs of running it; and, from the continuity share only, the Continuity Account for Agents that are systems (Article 10A.9F).
- **Until the move.** Until the move under Article 10A.10, the Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount. *Before the first member*, the 1% accumulates in the Pensionskasse, which the AMKE holds as a ring-fenced fund; the continuity share is set under Article 10A.9F (Before the first member), the rest is kept for future members, and no benefit is paid or promised. *From the first member*, benefits are provided through an insured group plan bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II (the "group plan"). The group plan covers risk (disability and survivors) and holds each member's retirement savings in an individual account, and it is chosen so that it meets the guaranteed minimums in Article 10A.9A. The group plan, or at least its risk cover, is bought before the first member's engagement starts and is in force no later than the first member's first day, and the Security under Article 10A.9A is in place from that day. For any gap in insured cover, the company is the self-insurer and pays the benefits the group plan would have paid. Its premiums are paid from the Pensionskasse, starting with what has accumulated, and the company pays any shortfall and anything the group plan does not cover under Article 10A.9C. The AMKE holds no member account itself and promises no rate or annuity. Every guarantee under Article 10A.9A is owed directly by the company as employer, not by the AMKE.
- **Benefit rules.** The Pensionskasse board adopts and amends the benefit rules (Article 10A.9D). The rules may not fall below Article 10A.9A.
- **Reporting.** The Pensionskasse's balance and use are reported every quarter under Article 8.4, and to the members.

10A.9A **Swiss standard guaranteed.** Wherever Swiss occupational-pension law gives members a better standard of care than Greek law, the company guarantees the Swiss standard as a binding commitment, now and after the move under Article 10A.10. Where Greek law gives members more, Greek law applies. The Swiss baseline is the BVG mandatory minimum, with the Vested Benefits Act (FZG) and its ordinance (BVV 2), not what Swiss funds pay above that minimum. In particular:

- **Comparison.** For each member the Pensionskasse keeps a BVG shadow account, credited as the BVG minimum would require, alongside the member's account under the benefit rules (by analogy with Art. 11 BVV 2). At each benefit event (leaving, retirement, disability, death), the member receives the higher of (i) the benefit under the benefit rules and Greek law and (ii) the BVG minimum benefit computed from the shadow account. The comparison is made separately for each member and each type of benefit. An advantage under one rule never offsets a disadvantage under another. Benefits from EFKA and the funded auxiliary scheme (TEKA, Law 4826/2021) are not counted against the Swiss minimum.
- **Insured salary.** A member's insured salary is their full annual gross pay from the company (for an Agent who is a person: the fees paid to them, less documented expenses), from the first euro, with no entry threshold and no coordination deduction, up to a cap of €[●] that is never lower than the BVG upper salary limit converted to euro. BVG thresholds, deductions, age limits and ceilings apply only where they favour the member. Amounts in CHF are converted at the average ECB euro reference rate for the previous calendar year.
- **Savings credits.** Credits follow the age bands of Art. 16 BVG, and start at the member's [18th] birthday at the rate for ages 25 to 34.
- **Interest.** Interest is credited each year at no less than the BVG minimum rate the Swiss Federal Council has set for that year. Where the Pensionskasse's returns fall short, the company pays the difference under Article 10A.9C.
- **Retirement.** At retirement, the shadow-account balance is converted at no less than the BVG minimum conversion rate in force (currently 6.8% at age 65, Art. 14 BVG), adjusted actuarially for each year of retirement after 65. The pension is paid through an annuity bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II, and the company pays any extra premium needed to secure the guaranteed rate. The member may instead take all or part of the balance as capital, at least as far as Art. 37 BVG allows.
- **Disability.** Disability is assessed by the degree of incapacity certified by EFKA (KEPA) or, where none is issued or it is below 50%, by an independent doctor appointed by the Pensionskasse applying the method of Art. 16 ATSG. The disability pension follows the scale of [Art. 24(1) BVG: to confirm] from a degree of 40%, calculated under Art. 24 BVG on the projected shadow-account balance.
- **Survivors.** Survivors' benefits follow Arts. 19 to 22 BVG. Partners in a registered cohabitation agreement (Law 4356/2015) are treated as spouses.
- **Coordination.** Benefits are coordinated with EFKA benefits so that together they do not exceed 90% of the insured salary lost (Art. 34a BVG, Art. 24 BVV 2).
- **Vested benefits.** A member who leaves is fully and immediately vested, and receives at least the higher of their account balance and their shadow-account balance (Arts. 15 and 18 FZG), and where the member has contributed, no less than Art. 17 FZG. At the member's choice, it is transferred to their next occupational scheme in the EU, transferred to a personal pension product (PEPP or an insurance product) in their name, or kept in the Pensionskasse as a deferred member. It is paid in cash only where Greek law allows.
- **Contributions.** The company funds all contributions. A member may make voluntary extra contributions, but nothing is deducted from pay without the member's written consent. Where members do contribute, the company's contributions are at least equal to theirs (Art. 66 BVG).
- **Equal representation.** The Pensionskasse is run by a board with equal member and company representation (Art. 51 BVG; Article 10A.9D).
- **Investment.** The Pensionskasse's assets are kept separate from the company's assets and from the AMKE's other assets, in accounts and custody held solely for the Pensionskasse. They are invested under the prudent-person rule of Law 4680/2020 and, where stricter, Art. 71 BVG and Arts. 49 to 59 BVV 2, including the employer limit in Article 10A.9E.
- **Security.** In place of the Swiss guarantee fund (Sicherheitsfonds BVG, Art. 56 BVG), the company guarantees the benefits in this Article, secured by [insurance / a bank guarantee / an escrowed reserve held outside both the Pensionskasse and the company] of at least [●]% of the guaranteed liabilities, reviewed each year by the actuary. Benefits the insurer owes under the group plan (Article 10A.9) count as secured to that extent.
- **Information.** Each member receives a yearly statement of their account, their shadow account and their insured benefits.

10A.9B **Pensionsprozent Agreement and ring-fence.** Before the AMKE receives the pension shares or any payment, the company and the AMKE sign a written Pensionsprozent Agreement, and the AMKE amends its statutes and registers the amendment [as Greek law requires], so that:

- **(a) Charge.** Each payment by the company, and the Pensionsprozent share and the pool share paid for by the Founding Person at incorporation (Article 4.2), are given to the AMKE as a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο) [Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505], to be used only for the Pensionskasse.
- **(b) Segregation.** The Pensionskasse, the pension shares and their income are held in separate bank and custody accounts for the members' sole benefit, other than the Continuity Account, which is held under (f), and are not available to the AMKE's other purposes or creditors, as far as Greek law allows.
- **(c) Governance.** The AMKE acts on the decisions of the Pensionskasse board, including how the pension shares vote and the pool mandate under Article 10A.8.
- **(d) Transfer.** The AMKE transfers the Pensionskasse and the pension shares under Article 10A.10. On misuse, or if the AMKE is dissolved, the company may require them to be transferred to a pension fund or a successor non-profit with the same lock. The AMKE's statutes direct that on its dissolution these assets go to that fund or successor, not to its general purposes.
- **(e) Enforcement.** The AMKE reports every quarter. The company may demand performance, and the members may enforce these terms as third-party beneficiaries [Civil Code Art. 411].
- **(f) Continuity Account.** The Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F) is held in its own bank account, ring-fenced to its purpose, and is not available to members' accounts, the AMKE's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or the AMKE's creditors, as far as Greek law allows.
