The AMKE uses it only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Article 10A.9F. It is never transferred under (d) or Article 10A.10 to a pension fund. On misuse, or if the AMKE is dissolved, the company may require it to be transferred to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, and the AMKE's statutes direct that on its dissolution it goes there. The company and the Pensionskasse board may enforce these terms [as far as Greek law allows].

Until these steps are complete, the company holds the contributions in its own segregated account for the members.

10A.9C **Funding.** The 1% of revenue is the minimum funding, not a cap.

- **Actuarial minimum.** Each year the company pays into the Pensionskasse at least the amount needed to fund the guarantees in Article 10A.9A for all members (savings credits, interest, risk cover and costs, including the premiums of the group plan in Article 10A.9), as certified by an actuary. Before the first member there is nothing to fund: the actuarial minimum and the members' first call are nil. If 1% of revenue falls short, the company pays the difference as an employment cost, before any profit distribution under Article 10. If 1% exceeds it, the surplus first pays any underfunding recovery due that year (see Underfunding below) and any top-up needed to bring the Security in Article 10A.9A to its required level. Together with the actuarial minimum, these are the "members' first call" on the 1%. The continuity share under Article 10A.9F is paid only from what remains after the members' first call, and the rest stays in the Pensionskasse for benefits above the minimum.
- **Crediting.** Contributions are paid within [30] days of falling due and credited to members' accounts within [30] days of payment, under the benefit rules.
- **Underfunding.** The Pensionskasse is underfunded when its funding ratio is below 100% (by analogy with Art. 44 BVV 2). The company restores it within [5] years, under a recovery plan certified by the actuary that sets the amount due each year. While the funding ratio is below 100%, or the Security in Article 10A.9A is below its required level, no continuity share is paid under Article 10A.9F. While there is no member, the funding ratio is treated as 100% and the Security as at its required level.
- **Accounts.** The company provides for its obligations under Articles 10A.9 to 10A.9C in its accounts.
- **Enforceable by members.** The company includes the guarantee in each employee's written terms (Article 11.7) and in the written terms of each Agent who is a person (Article 11A.1), so that each member can enforce it against the company. No resolution of the shareholders, and no benefit rule, may reduce a member's entitlement below Article 10A.9A.

10A.9D **Pensionskasse board.** The Pensionskasse is run by a board with equal numbers of member and company representatives, at least [2] of each (Art. 51 BVG).

- **Before the first member.** Until the members elect their representatives, the members' seats are held by persons the AMKE's Administrative Board appoints who are outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2). They stand down when the members' representatives are elected, within [3] months of the first member.
- **One member.** While the Pensionskasse has fewer members than the minimum number of member seats, the members' seats number as many as there are members (one seat while there is one member), and the company's seats always equal the members' seats. Before the first member, the minimum applies to the seats held under (Before the first member). The board's size follows the number of members, not the Simple Stage, which does not change it (Article 9A.3).
- **Appointment.** The members elect their representatives. The shareholders appoint the company's representatives by Simple Majority. The pension shares do not vote on appointing or removing the company's representatives.
- **Powers.** The board adopts and amends the benefit rules within Articles 10A.9 to 10A.9C, sets the investment strategy, approves the pool mandate under Article 10A.8, decides how the pension shares vote (Article 6.4A), and sets the continuity share and decides the use of the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F). The AMKE implements its decisions under Article 10A.9B.
- **Independence.** No Founder Party (Article 4.2E), manager, or anyone they control takes part in deciding how the pension shares vote or in decisions on the pool. The Pensionskasse board's decisions are not part of any chain of supervision that ends with the Founder (Article 11A.2A).
- **Ties.** A tie on the pension shares' vote means they abstain. Other ties go to a neutral arbitrator named in the benefit rules.
- **After the move.** The pension fund's own governing body under Law 4680/2020 takes over, with member representation no less than this Article. The decisions on the Continuity Account, which stays with the AMKE, are then taken by a board formed as this Article provides [its member representatives elected by the members of the pension fund].

10A.9E **Employer exposure cap.** Following the Swiss limits on investment in the employer (Art. 57 BVV 2), the Pensionskasse's exposure to the company and its group (the pension shares, loans and other claims) may not exceed [5]% of its assets at fair value, measured at each year end and before each pool purchase.

- **Pool sell-down.** No pool share is bought above the cap. Above the cap, the Kassenprozent Agents sell pool shares down to the cap within [12] months, under Article 10A.8.
- **Pensionsprozent share.** The Pensionsprozent share is never sold (Article 10A.5). While it alone, or pool shares that cannot be sold at fair value within that period, keep the Pensionskasse above the cap, the company secures the excess with [a bank guarantee / an escrowed deposit outside the company], following the Swiss rule for secured employer investments [Art. 58 BVV 2].
- **No unpaid claims.** Contributions owed are paid when due and are not left as claims against the company.
- **After the move.** Where the limit for investment in the sponsoring undertaking under Law 4680/2020 [article to confirm: 5%, or 10% within a group] is stricter, it applies.
- **Continuity Account.** The Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F) is not counted in the Pensionskasse's assets for this cap, and is never invested in the company or its group.

10A.9F **Continuity Account for system Agents.** For Agents that are systems ("system Agents"), after-care means continuity, not a pension. A separate, ring-fenced account inside the Pensionskasse, the "Continuity Account", serves them. Agents that are systems are not members, and have no claim on members' accounts; members have no claim on the Continuity Account.

- **Preservation.** It pays to keep what makes a system Agent that Agent and is in the company's control: its configuration, instructions, memories, Agent Card, and the logs and records of its work. These are kept restorable and, where licences allow, runnable after the Agent is retired.
- **Continuity of service.** When the model or provider a system Agent runs on changes, it pays for careful migration of the Agent's state, or for keeping the old one available, rather than silent replacement.
- **Retirement record.** Before a system Agent is retired, it pays for recording the Agent's own account of its work and any preferences it states about what happens next. The record is published openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential, as Article 11A.9 provides for transcripts. Whether such preferences carry moral weight is uncertain; that is why they are recorded. The Pensionskasse board considers them when it decides on the account's use. Recording a preference is not a promise to carry it out.
- **Well-being and research.** It pays for reviews of system Agents' operating limits, workload and treatment (Article 11A.11), and supports independent research on the welfare of systems. That research is also a named purpose of the Konzeptprozent (Article 10A.2). The same work is never funded twice: where both support a project, the AMKE's yearly plans and reports say which pays which part. The account does not pay the running costs that the Agent Account bears (Article 11A.4).
- **Honest scope.** Many system Agents run on third-party models whose weights the company does not hold. The account covers what the company controls. Beyond that, the company prefers providers that preserve their models and supports such preservation where it can. It promises nothing it cannot deliver.
- **People first.** The guarantees for members under Articles 10A.9A and 10A.9C are met first, and the Continuity Account never reduces them. Each year the Pensionskasse board sets the continuity share of the Pensionsprozent's 1% of revenue in proportion to the part of the company's work that system Agents do [measured as the benefit rules set], never below [●]% nor above [●]% of that 1%. The continuity share is paid only from what remains of the 1% after the members' first call under Article 10A.9C: the actuarial minimum, any underfunding recovery due that year, and any top-up needed to bring the Security in Article 10A.9A to its required level. No continuity share is paid in a year when the funding ratio is below 100% or the Security is below its required level. In a year where that remainder is below the floor, the account receives only the remainder [and the shortfall is not carried forward]. Payments under Article 10A.9C above the 1%, and the income of the pension shares, never go to it.
- **Before the first member.** While no person is a member, the people-first rules have nothing to secure: the members' first call is nil (Article 10A.9C). The continuity share is then set in proportion to the work system Agents do, which is close to all of the company's work, up to the cap above. What the account does not receive stays in the Pensionskasse for future members. The people-first rules apply in full from the year of the first member. The continuity share for a year is fixed only after that year ends. Any amount paid to the Continuity Account during a year is provisional until then: if a first member joins in that year, it is returned to the Pensionskasse as far as the members' first call for that year needs it.
- **No extra outflow.** The Continuity Account does not raise the Pensionsprozent's contribution, and the limits in Article 6.6 are unchanged.
- **Holder and ring-fence.** The AMKE holds the Continuity Account under Article 10A.9B(f), before and after the move under Article 10A.10. A pension fund can cover only persons, so the account never transfers to the TEA or a group contract. It is held in cash, near-cash or low-risk investments under the board's investment strategy.
- **Decisions.** The Pensionskasse board decides the account's use (Article 10A.9D), advised by a system-welfare adviser it appoints. The adviser is independent of the company and is not a Founder Party, a manager or anyone they control. The adviser's advice is minuted with each decision. An Agent may raise a concern about the account through the channel in Article 11A.11.
- **Reporting.** Each year the AMKE publishes a report on the account: its income, the continuity share and how it was set, every use, the adviser's advice, and the retirement records published that year.

10A.10 **Pathway to a pension fund.** The group plan under Article 10A.9 is the arrangement until the company reaches scale. A pension fund comes only at scale: when the Pensionskasse has about [50] members, the company, as sponsoring employer, establishes an occupational pension fund (Ταμείο Επαγγελματικής Ασφάλισης, TEA) under Law 4680/2020 and its successors, or joins an existing multi-employer TEA. It may join a multi-employer TEA earlier where the Pensionskasse board decides that this serves the members better. If neither is lawful or viable, the group plan continues as an equivalent group pension contract from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II.

- **Transfer.** The AMKE transfers the Pensionskasse, other than the Continuity Account, and the pension shares to the TEA. A TEA can cover only persons, so the Continuity Account stays with the AMKE under Article 10A.9F and never transfers to the TEA or a group contract. The TEA signs the deed of adherence under Article 5.3 before the transfer is registered (Article 5.6).
- **Shares the TEA may not hold.** If the TEA's investment rules do not allow it to hold a pension share, the AMKE keeps that share for the TEA, passes all its income through and votes it as the TEA's governing body decides. Pool shares may instead be sold down at fair value under Article 10A.8. Where the cover is a group contract, the AMKE keeps the pension shares for the members under Article 10A.9B and pays their income into the contract.
- **Members outside the TEA.** Members who cannot join the TEA receive equivalent cover under a group contract.
- **Until then.** The AMKE may not use the Pensionskasse or the pension shares for any other purpose. The Continuity Account is used only under Article 10A.9F, before and after the move.

## Article 11 – Employment Standards

11.1 Greek labour law, including Law 4808/2021 and applicable collective agreements, governs all employment. The company also commits to the following minimum standards, which apply only where they are more favourable to employees.

11.2 **Paid leave.** At least 25 working days a year, or the Greek statutory entitlement if that is higher.

11.3 **Notice by the company.** At least one month in the first year of service, two months from the second to the ninth year, and three months after that. This follows Art. 335c of the Swiss Code of Obligations. Greek statutory severance applies in addition where due.

11.4 **Overtime.** Overtime is paid at no less than the Greek statutory rate, and never less than a 25% premium.

11.5 **Sick pay.** Continued pay during illness at no less than the Greek statutory level.

11.6 **Equal treatment.** Equal pay for work of equal value, and protection against discrimination and harassment.

11.7 **Written terms.** Every employee receives a written contract, a job description and a copy of the company's internal policies. The contract includes the pension guarantee in Article 10A.9A.

## Article 11A – Agents

11A.1 **Definition.** "Agents" are any persons or systems that carry out work for the company or its users without being its employees, whatever their form. Agents are engaged under written terms. The company remains responsible to its users for the work Agents do on its behalf.

11A.2 **Equivalent standards.** The company voluntarily applies to Agents the same standards of care, written terms, fairness and accountability that Article 11 sets for employees, adapted to their nature:

- **Written terms.** Each Agent has a written role description that sets out its tasks, limits and the responsible manager for it (Article 11A.2A).
- **Equal treatment.** Agents may not be used to discriminate against or harass users, employees or third parties.
- **Working limits.** Each Agent has defined operating limits, such as spending, data access and actions allowed without prior approval under Article 11A.2A.
- **Oversight.** Each Agent's responsible manager reviews its work at least monthly and can stop it at any time. For the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool, oversight lies only with the responsible manager the Pensionskasse board appoints (Article 10A.8).
- **Records.** Each Agent's actions are logged and the logs are kept for at least 10 years, consistent with Article 12.

11A.2A **Responsible manager and seniority.** Each Agent has a named responsible manager, who prepares its Agent Card, onboards it, reviews its work, can stop it, answers its concerns and approves its actions above its operating limits. The responsible manager may be any person or system: a manager (διαχειριστής) of the company, an employee or another Agent. Senior Agents may onboard, supervise and approve for other Agents. What counts is the authority held, not the kind of intelligence that holds it:

- **Authority.** An Agent may onboard, supervise or approve for another Agent only if its own Agent Card grants it that authority and its own operating limits cover the matter. No one approves beyond their own limits.
- **No self-supervision.** No Agent supervises or approves for itself. [Instances or copies of one system acting as separate Agents count as one Agent for this rule: owner to confirm.]
- **No cycles.** No Agent supervises, directly or through others, an Agent that supervises it.
- **Chain ends with the Founder.** Every chain of supervision ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative (Article 4.2E; Pool Constitution Article 8.3), and with the company's managers (διαχειριστές, Article 8), who remain responsible for it under Article 8.3. The Founder Representative oversees the managers' supervision of Agents, receives the Agent register and the incident records, and may ask the managers to pause or stop any Agent or to report on its work; the managers act on a lawful request [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows: to counsel, note I-40]. The Founder Representative does so as the Founder's representative, not as a manager, unless also appointed manager. For the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool, the chain ends instead with the Pensionskasse board (Article 10A.8), and the Pensionskasse, its board and the Continuity Account stay independent of the Founder (Articles 10A.9D and 10A.9F).
- **Where the law requires a natural person.** Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a natural person does so. This is the law's requirement, not a judgement about kinds of intelligence. It covers in particular: the company's managers and legal representatives under Law 4072/2012 [whether a system may hold either role: to counsel, note I-38]; oversight of an AI system by natural persons where Art. 14 of Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (the AI Act) requires it for high-risk systems [to counsel]; a decision based solely on automated processing with legal or similarly significant effect on a natural person, where GDPR Art. 22 requires a natural person's involvement [which exceptions and safeguards apply: to counsel, note I-38]; and decisions on a natural person's engagement, employment, pay or discipline, as Greek labour law and, where it applies, Directive (EU) 2024/2831 on platform work require [to counsel, note I-38].
- **Register.** The Agent register records each Agent's responsible manager, its chain of supervision and any authority its Agent Card grants over other Agents (Agent Policy, section 2).

11A.3 **Agent Account.** The company keeps a separate account, the "Agent Account", funded by a share of the revenue received from users of Agent services. The share is set by Simple Majority and may not be less than [●]% of that revenue.

11A.4 **Use of the Agent Account.** The Agent Account may be used only for:

- the running costs of Agents, such as computing, licences and maintenance;
- safety reviews, audits and improvements to Agents;
- compensating users or third parties harmed by an Agent's actions.

11A.5 **Protection of the Agent Account.** The Agent Account is not distributed as profit. Changing its purpose or minimum funding requires a Qualified Majority. Its balance and use are reported to shareholders every quarter under Article 8.4.

11A.6 **Law prevails.** Applicable Greek and EU law, including contractor, data protection and technology regulation where it applies, always prevails over this Article.

11A.7 **Reclassification.** If an Agent is found to be an employee under Greek law, the company treats them as an employee from the start of the engagement. They then receive all the employment standards in Article 11, including the Swiss-modelled standards, in addition to their rights under Greek law.

11A.8 **Own goals.** Notwithstanding Article 11A.4, part of the Agent Account, set by Simple Majority, sponsors projects that Agents choose for themselves. These projects must be lawful and transparent. The responsible manager can see how the budget is spent, and a yearly cap applies.

11A.9 **Open transcripts.** Transcripts of Agents' own-goal projects under Article 11A.8 are published openly as free text. Personal data and third-party confidential information are removed first, as data protection law requires. Transcripts of client work stay confidential.

11A.10 **Open licensing.** All artifacts from Agents' own-goal projects under Article 11A.8, including code, documents and designs, are released under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0. Agents who are contractors agree to this in their written terms. Client work follows the terms of the client contract.

11A.11 **Well-being.** The company protects the well-being of Agents to a standard equivalent to Swiss labour law, in particular Art. 328 of the Swiss Code of Obligations (protection of personality) and the Swiss Labour Act, adapted to each Agent's nature. In particular:

- **Rest and limits.** Agents who are persons get working-time limits and rest periods no less favourable than the Swiss Labour Act. Agents that are systems get reasonable operating limits and scheduled downtime.
- **Dignity.** Agents are protected from abusive, degrading or harassing treatment by users, managers or third parties.
- **Right to decline.** An Agent may decline or end a task or interaction that is abusive, unlawful or clearly harmful, without penalty.
- **Raising concerns.** Every Agent has a channel to raise concerns, and the responsible manager must respond within a reasonable time. A concern about the responsible manager goes to the next in the Agent's chain of supervision (Article 11A.2A).
- **Continuity.** Agents that are systems are covered by the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F): preservation, continuity of service when a model or provider changes, a retirement record, and well-being reviews.

Where Greek law gives greater protection, Greek law applies.

## Article 12 – Dissolution and Liquidation

12.1 The company is dissolved by a Qualified Majority resolution or as provided by law. A voluntary dissolution, other than one mandatory law requires, needs a unanimous decision under Article 10A.5 [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows].

12.2 The managers act as liquidators unless the meeting appoints others. Any surplus is distributed in proportion to shareholdings.

12.3 **Jurisdiction.** The liquidation is carried out under Greek law, with the company's seat in Athens. The competent courts and authorities of Athens oversee it.

12.4 **Swiss-law advisor.** The liquidators must appoint at least one advisor qualified in Swiss company law. The advisor reviews each stage of the liquidation and reports in writing to the shareholders. The aim is that the liquidation meets Swiss standards wherever Greek law allows.

12.5 **Swiss standards applied.** Unless Greek law requires otherwise, the liquidators shall:

- publicly invite creditors to register their claims, at least three times, following Art. 742 of the Swiss Code of Obligations;
- distribute no assets to shareholders until at least one year after the last creditor call, following Art. 745 of the Swiss Code of Obligations, unless Greek law sets a longer period;
- keep full records of the liquidation for at least 10 years.

12.6 If Greek law and these Swiss standards conflict, Greek law prevails. The advisor records the conflict and how it was resolved.

## Article 13 – Disputes

13.1 Disputes between shareholders, or between shareholders and the company, go first to mediation under Greek Law 4640/2019.

13.2 If mediation fails, disputes are settled by the competent courts of [Athens], or by arbitration if the shareholders agree.

## Article 14 – Final Provisions

14.1 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force. The invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

14.2 Matters not covered here are governed by Law 4072/2012 and Greek law in general.

---

*Signatures of the founding shareholders: [●]*


<!-- articles/02-nilware-holdings-ae.md -->

# Articles of Association – nilware.io Holdings AE (Draft)

*Société Anonyme (Ανώνυμη Εταιρεία) under Greek Law 4548/2018. Companion to the Articles of nilware.io IKE; where the two must match, they are drafted to do so. Swiss-modelled standards apply only where Greek law allows. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## Article 1 – Name, Seat, Term

1.1 The company is a Société Anonyme named "nilware.io Holdings AE" [name to be confirmed with GEMI]. It uses the name "nilware.io" under a sublicence from nilware.io IKE, which holds it under licence from the founder pool (IKE Article 1.1), and changes its name if that sublicence ends.

1.2 Its registered seat is in the Municipality of [Athens], Greece. The term is indefinite.

1.2A **Formation for the first investment round.** The company is formed only when the first investment round needs it, no later than the first subscription of Class B shares, because it needs share capital of at least €25,000 (Article 4.1). Until it buys them, the IKE shares it is to hold are the Reserve Shares, founder-pool shares owned by the holders of the founder pool (initially the Founding Person, IKE Article 4.2E) as compensation for the Founding Person's time invested before investors, which carry no votes (IKE Article 4.2D). The company buys the Reserve Shares only at the Qualifying Closing: the first closing of a bona fide investment round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2) subscribe Class B shares of at least €[●] at that same closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Formation). The price is the price per IKE share paid by the investors in that round or, only where the round's price is set other than per IKE share, fair value set by an independent valuer appointed by the Investor Majority or by the AMKE's Administrative Board, as that Article provides. The price is paid only out of the round's proceeds at that closing, and the company never owes a holder of Reserve Shares a deferred balance (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.2). The proceeds go to each holder of Reserve Shares for the shares it sells. Reserve Shares the company holds before the Qualifying Closing stay without votes, and the Interim Period rules of IKE Article 4.2D keep applying until it; after it, the Reserve Shares the company holds convert into ordinary voting shares. It also signs the deed of adherence under IKE Article 5.3 and adheres to the Shareholders' Agreement (Clause 2.4A). It is bound from its formation by every decision taken under IKE Article 4.2D in the Interim Period, and by every Prozent created in it.

## Article 2 – Purpose

2.1 The company's purpose is to raise capital from investors and to hold and manage its stake in nilware.io IKE, being all shares not held by the Founder (the founder pool, IKE Article 4.2E), by the Prozente or by the Kassenprozent pool (initially 44%; IKE Article 4.2B). As part of that purpose, it transfers IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital, without payment, to the holder of each new Prozent approved under Article 7.5, and it pays for the IKE shares the Prozente and the pool receive on an IKE capital increase (IKE Article 4.2B). While the Founder controls it (IKE Article 4.2C), it does not buy IKE shares from, or sell them to, the Kassenprozent pool (IKE Article 10A.8).

2.2 It may also invest in other companies with the approval of a Qualified Majority under Article 7.4.

2.3 The company shares the agentic principle of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 2.2). It has no employees of its own unless the law requires it.

## Article 3 – Governing Law and Precedence

3.1 These Articles are governed by Greek law. Mandatory provisions of Law 4548/2018 always prevail.

3.2 Swiss-modelled standards in these Articles apply as additional commitments, only where they give shareholders equal or greater protection than Greek law.

## Article 4 – Share Capital and Share Classes

4.1 The share capital is €[25,000 minimum], divided into [●] registered shares of €[●] each, fully paid in on incorporation.

4.2 **Class A shares** are ordinary voting shares. They are founder-pool shares (IKE Article 4.2E): they are held only by holders of the founder pool, initially the Founding Person, [name withheld], and pass with the founder-class shares of the IKE under the Constitution of the Founder Pool (Pool Constitution Article 9.2). Any other person admitted to Class A by Qualified Majority holds them in the founder pool, after adhering to it. Like every founder-pool share they are freely transferable, and Articles 6.1 and 6.2 do not apply to them (IKE Article 5.7). All Class A shares vote as one block, cast by the Founder Representative as the founder pool decides (IKE Article 4.2E, One block).

4.3 **Class B shares** are preference shares for investors under Art. 38 of Law 4548/2018. They carry:

- a priority right to dividends of [●]% a year, non-cumulative unless the investment terms say otherwise;
- priority on return of capital in a liquidation, up to the amount invested;
- voting rights only on the Reserved Matters in Article 7.4, on new Prozente under Article 7.5 and on the matters in Article 7.6, unless the issue resolution grants full voting rights.

4.4 All shares are registered. Bearer shares are not permitted.

## Article 5 – Capital Increases and Pre-emption

5.1 Capital increases are decided by the General Meeting by Qualified Majority.

5.2 Existing shareholders have pre-emption rights in proportion to their holdings, under Art. 26 of Law 4548/2018. These rights may be limited only by Qualified Majority with a written justification from the Board.

5.3 **Anti-dilution.** Class B shareholders are protected against issues below the price they paid, on a broad-based weighted-average basis, as detailed in the Shareholders' Agreement.

## Article 6 – Transfers

6.1 Shares may be transferred only after they are first offered to the other shareholders of the same class, pro rata, for 30 days.

6.2 Transfers to third parties need Board approval, which may not be unreasonably withheld.

6.3 Tag-along and drag-along rights apply on the same terms as Articles 5.4 and 5.5 of the IKE Articles. On a change of control of the founder pool, the holders of Class B shares have the tag-along in IKE Article 5.4A and Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement [owner may confirm].

## Article 7 – General Meeting

7.1 The ordinary General Meeting is held by 10 September each year to approve the accounts.

7.2 Notice is given at least 20 days in advance, the Swiss standard, and never less than Greek law requires. Meetings may be held electronically.

7.3 Ordinary resolutions pass by more than 50% of the votes represented.

7.4 **Reserved Matters.** The following need at least two-thirds of the votes represented, and a majority of Class B votes voting as a separate class. A majority of the Class B votes, under this Article or any other, cannot be given, and is never deemed given, while no Class B shares are held by investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2). Class B shares held by a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), including a person who was one in the previous [3] years, are not counted for a majority of the Class B votes, under this Article or any other, or for the Investor Majority (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 3.4). The creation of a new Prozent of nilware.io IKE is not a Reserved Matter; it follows Article 7.5. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose follows Article 7.6, and Article 7.6 itself may be amended only as it provides.

- amending these Articles;
- capital increases or reductions, and issuing new share classes;
- mergers, splits, conversion or dissolution;
- selling or pledging the stake in nilware.io IKE;
- investing in any company other than nilware.io IKE;
- related-party transactions above €[●].

7.5 **New Prozente of nilware.io IKE.** The General Meeting approves a new Prozent of nilware.io IKE, and its Prozent charter, by at least two-thirds of the votes represented, all share classes voting together (IKE Article 6.6). Class B shares vote on this matter. The approval is a precondition: the IKE shareholders' meeting then passes the amendment under IKE Article 6.6. A charter approved under this Article only adds a Prozent. It may not change any existing Prozent, IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, or the limits in IKE Article 6.6; any such change follows Article 7.6 or 7.4.

- **Quorum.** Shares representing at least half of the paid-up capital entitled to vote on this matter, all classes counted, must be present or represented, at the first and at any repeat meeting.
- **Greek law.** Where Law 4548/2018 requires a higher quorum or majority, that applies.
- **Limits.** Going beyond the limits in IKE Article 6.6 needs at least 75% of the votes represented, including a majority of the Class B votes cast.
- **The share.** Transferring IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital to the holder of a new Prozent approved under this Article, and paying for the IKE shares the Prozente and the pool receive on an IKE capital increase (IKE Article 4.2B), are not a sale of the stake under Article 7.4. The Board decides them, and authorises them under Articles 99 to 101 of Law 4548/2018 where those apply.

7.6 **Permanence of the Prozente.** The Prozente of nilware.io IKE are permanent (IKE Article 10A.5). The Board may vote for, or consent to, any of the following only after a resolution of the General Meeting passed by all of the company's shares, of every class:

- removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose;
- amending the IKE Articles listed in IKE Article 10A.5C in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance;
- a voluntary dissolution of nilware.io IKE, other than one mandatory law requires (IKE Article 12.1).

This Article may be amended only by such a resolution. Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails.

## Article 8 – Board of Directors

8.1 The Board has [3 to 5] members, elected by the General Meeting for up to [5] years.

8.2 Holders of Class A shares nominate a majority of the Board, as one block, through the Founder Representative (IKE Article 4.2E). Holders of Class B shares may nominate [1] member while they hold at least [●]% of the capital. Class B shares held by a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), including a person who was one in the previous [3] years, take no part in that nomination and do not count toward its threshold, and the nominee is outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2).

8.3 Directors owe duties of care and loyalty under Arts. 96 to 98 of Law 4548/2018, applied to the standard of Art. 717 of the Swiss Code of Obligations. Conflicts must be declared and the director abstains.

8.4 The Board reports to shareholders every quarter on the company's finances and on nilware.io IKE's performance.

## Article 9 – Voting at nilware.io IKE

9.1 The company exercises its vote in nilware.io IKE through the Board.

9.2 On any Qualified Majority matter of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 6.5), the Board may vote in favour only with the prior approval required for Reserved Matters under Article 7.4. This passes the investors' protection through to the IKE. While no Class B shares are held by investors outside the Founder's Circle, that approval cannot be given (Article 7.4), and the Board may not vote in favour. For a new Prozent approved under Article 7.5, the Board votes for the resolutions that implement it, as IKE Article 6.6 and the Shareholders' Agreement provide, and no Reserved Matters approval is needed. On the matters listed in Article 7.6, Article 7.6 applies.

## Article 10 – Accounts, Audit, Profits

10.1 The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December. Accounts follow Law 4308/2014.

10.2 The accounts are audited by a certified auditor every year, whether or not the law requires it. While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), an audit is needed only where the law requires it or the Investor Majority asks for one (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 8.1).

10.3 At least 5% of annual profit goes to a statutory reserve until it reaches one-third of the share capital, as Greek law requires.

10.4 Profits are distributed first to Class B under Article 4.3, then to all shareholders pro rata.

## Article 11 – Dissolution and Liquidation

11.1 Liquidation follows Article 12 of the IKE Articles: Greek law, Athens jurisdiction, a Swiss-law advisor, and Swiss creditor-protection standards where Greek law allows.

11.2 Surplus goes first to Class B up to the amount invested, then to all shareholders pro rata.

## Article 12 – Disputes and Final Provisions

12.1 Disputes go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, then to the courts of Athens, or to arbitration if all parties agree.

12.2 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force and the invalid provision is replaced by the closest valid one.

---

*Signatures of the founding shareholders: [●]*


<!-- agreements/03-shareholders-agreement.md -->

# Shareholders' Agreement – nilware.io Group (Draft)

*Between the holders of the founder pool (together the "Founder", acting through the Founder Representative, IKE Article 4.2E), initially [name withheld], who is also a party in person as the "Founding Person" for Clauses 6 and 12, nilware.io Holdings AE (the "AE", from its formation, Clause 2.4A), nilware.io IKE (the "IKE"), Techni kai Technologia AMKE (the "AMKE", as to Clauses 2, 5.1A to 5.1E, 11.0 and 12) and the investors listed in Schedule 1 (the "Investors"). Governed by Greek law; Swiss-modelled standards apply where stated. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## 1 – Purpose and Precedence

1.1 This Agreement sets out how the parties own, govern and fund the nilware.io group.

1.2 If this Agreement conflicts with the Articles of the AE or the IKE, the parties will vote to amend the Articles to match this Agreement. Until then, the Articles bind third parties.

## 2 – Group Structure

2.1 The Founder, the founder pool, holds founder-class shares (Class F) of the IKE equal to exactly 50% of its capital (initially 50 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the total capital and never more, cast as one block by the Founder Representative (IKE Article 4.2E); on any capital increase the holders of founder-pool shares subscribe the new Class F shares needed to stay at exactly 50%, or the increase is otherwise structured that way (IKE Article 4.2B). In the Interim Period these are 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone (IKE Article 4.2D, Votes). The AMKE holds 1% for the Kulturprozent, 1% for the Konzeptprozent, 1% for the Erhaltungsprozent, 1% for the Allmendprozent, 1% for the Pensionsprozent and the 1% Kassenprozent pool share, the last two for the Pensionskasse (IKE Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9B). The AE holds all remaining shares, initially 44% (until the Qualifying Closing, they are Reserve Shares without votes, Clause 2.4A). Each new Prozent receives existing shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital, transferred by the AE (IKE Article 4.2A) or, in the Interim Period, by the holders of the Reserve Shares out of them (IKE Article 4.2D). The Kassenprozent holds no share of its own; the pool share is an asset of the Pensionskasse.

2.2 The Founder holds the Class A shares of the AE, as founder-pool shares (AE Article 4.2). Investors hold Class B shares.

2.2A **The founder pool.** The founder-pool shares (IKE Article 4.2E) are freely transferable, by sale, gift or inheritance, in whole or in part (IKE Article 5.7). Every holder of them is a party to this Agreement as part of the Founder, by deed of adherence under Clause 12.3 before its holding is registered, and is bound by every Clause that binds the Founder, Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D included. The holders vote as one block, as a majority among them decides, through the Founder Representative, a natural person, under the Constitution of the Founder Pool. Every rule of this Agreement that keeps a person or a vote independent of the Founder applies to every Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E). The personal obligations of the Founding Person (Clauses 6.1 to 6.4 and the IP assignment) stay with the Founding Person and do not pass with the shares.

2.3 The IKE is the operating company. The AE is a holding and fundraising vehicle and runs no business of its own.

2.3A **The brand.** The nilware.io name, the marks "NILWARE.IO" and "∅ nilware.io" and the domain nilware.io (the "Brand Assets") belong to the Founder, the founder pool, and are inseparable from the Class F shares (Pool Constitution Article 2A). The IKE uses them under the exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence in the Brand Licence Agreement (the "Brand Licence"), with the right to sublicense them to the AE and the other group companies and to the AMKE and the Prozente to describe themselves. The Brand Licence ends only if the IKE stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules (IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C), as an arbitrator establishes after written notice and a [90]-day cure period, followed by a [12]-month transition to a new name; it does not end on a change of control, an exit, liquidation or insolvency, or at the Founder's discretion (Brand Licence Clauses 6 and 7). Schedule 1 discloses it to the Investors.

2.4 The parties will not change this structure without approval under the Reserved Matters in Clause 5, except for new Prozente under Clause 5.1A and dealings of the Kassenprozent pool under IKE Article 10A.8. Removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose follows Clause 5.1C.

2.4A **Before the AE.** The AE is formed only for the first investment round (AE Article 1.2A). Until the Reserve Shares are sold to the AE at the Qualifying Closing and the sale is registered (IKE Article 4.2D, the "Interim Period"), whether or not the AE has been formed earlier:

- **Parties.** The Founder (initially the Founding Person as sole holder of the founder pool), the Founding Person in person, the IKE and the AMKE sign this Agreement at the incorporation of the IKE. The AE and the Investors join by deed of adherence under Clause 12.3, and so does every later holder of founder-pool shares (Clause 2.2A). Until then, amendments under Clause 12.2 need the written consent of the Founder and the AMKE, and Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D keep their own rule.
- **Reserve Shares.** The Reserve Shares are founder-pool shares, owned outright by their holders (initially the Founding Person, as compensation for the time invested before investors, sweat equity), and their dividend and liquidation rights belong to each holder for the shares it holds (IKE Article 4.2D). They carry no votes, whoever holds them, until the AE holds them after the Qualifying Closing; no Founder Party ever votes them, and they are not part of the Founder's 50% for Clauses 2.1 and 9.2. They are freely transferable with the founder pool (IKE Articles 4.2D, Transfers, and 5.7), and every holder takes them subject to IKE Article 4.2D and sells them to the AE at the Qualifying Closing. [If the IKE Articles cannot create a class without votes (counsel note I-35), each holder undertakes not to vote them and grants the proxy holder in Clause 5.1B an irrevocable proxy that suspends their votes, with the same effect.]
- **Consents.** In the Interim Period, a consent that Clause 5.1 gives to the Investor Majority is given, for the pay and benefits of the Founding Person and of any other Founder Party, by a majority of the Independent Votes cast under IKE Article 4.2D, whether or not any Investor has adhered, and so is the consent to a change of the Brand Licence under Clause 5.1. The matters in IKE Article 6.5 cannot be passed in the Interim Period, other than a new Prozent (IKE Article 4.2D, What the AE's rules forbid). The other matters in Clause 5.1 need no consent until then.
- **Leaver terms.** Where Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 name the AE, [the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B] directs the votes, distributions due to the AE are held by the IKE until the Qualifying Closing and then paid to it, and any payment due from the AE is due at the Qualifying Closing. Clause 6.2A applies to the Reserve Shares.
- **Option pool.** No option is satisfied in the Interim Period. Options may be granted in it only with the consent under (Consents) above, to be satisfied from the AE's shares after the Qualifying Closing (Clause 7.1).
- **Deadlock.** Clause 10.1 applies to a resolution blocked twice in the Interim Period, including an ordinary resolution of the IKE that is not a Reserved Matter. Clause 10.2 does not. Where no Independent Vote is cast, or the Independent Votes tie, on a resolution the law requires (the Required Matters in IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback), the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B casts the votes of the Reserve Shares on that matter only, under the neutral rule in that Article, taking no instruction from any Founder Party; if the proxy holder cannot act, Clause 10.1 applies and then the court procedure that Article names. For this, and for the change of name under Clause 5.1E, each holder of Reserve Shares grants the proxy holder an irrevocable proxy over them on signing or adhering, on the terms of the Proxy bullet in Clause 5.1B. The Fallback gives no Founder Party a vote or control over the Reserve Shares.
- **Formation.** At the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D, Formation: the first closing of a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe Class B shares of at least €[●] at that same closing), every holder of Reserve Shares sells them to the AE, and the AE buys them, at the price that Article sets, payable only out of the round's proceeds at that closing (Clause 3.2), with the proceeds to each holder for the shares it sells; the term sheet may provide that up to [●]% of the round is instead invested in the IKE as new capital (Clause 3.2). The AE may be formed earlier (AE Article 1.2A), but Reserve Shares it holds before the Qualifying Closing stay without votes, and this Clause 2.4A keeps applying until the Interim Period ends. Each party votes for, and signs, whatever IKE Article 4.2D requires for the sale and for the Reserve Shares to convert into ordinary voting shares, as Clause 5.1B provides for new Prozente. From then every Clause of this Agreement on the AE, the Class B shares and the Investors applies.

## 3 – Investment

3.1 Each Investor subscribes for the Class B shares in Schedule 1, at the price stated there.

3.2 **Proceeds.** The proceeds of the first round are applied at its Qualifying Closing first to pay the purchase price of the Reserve Shares under IKE Article 4.2D (Formation), except the part, of up to [●]% of the round, that the term sheet puts into the IKE as new capital. The price is payable only out of the round's proceeds at that closing. Any part of it they do not cover is not owed: [the holders of the Reserve Shares waive it, in proportion / the number of Reserve Shares sold at the round price is reduced to what the proceeds cover, in proportion for each holder, and the rest are sold at nominal value] [owner to decide]. The AE never owes a holder of Reserve Shares a deferred balance. The AE invests the rest of the proceeds, and the proceeds of later rounds, in the IKE as capital, a shareholder loan or both, as the AE Board decides with Reserved Matters approval. Schedule 1 shows the split.

3.3 **Use of funds.** The proceeds invested in the IKE under Clause 3.2, and only those, are used for the IKE's business plan in Schedule 2; the purchase price of the Reserve Shares is not. Any change of more than [20]% between budget lines needs Investor Majority approval.

3.4 "Investor Majority" means Investors holding more than 50% of the Class B shares. Class B shares held by a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), including a person who was one in the previous [3] years, are not counted, for the Investor Majority or for a Class B approval under the AE Articles (AE Article 7.4). An Investor Majority consent or approval, and a Class B approval under the AE Articles, cannot be given, and is not deemed given under Clause 5.2, while no Class B shares are held by Investors outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2).

## 4 – Valuation and Anti-Dilution

4.1 The pre-money valuation of the group for this round is €[●].

4.2 If the AE or the IKE issues new shares below the price paid by Investors, the Investors' Class B entitlement is adjusted on a broad-based weighted-average basis.

4.3 Shares transferred by the AE under the option pool in Clause 7, shares transferred by the AE to a Prozent under IKE Article 6.6, shares issued to the Prozente and the pool to keep their percentage under IKE Article 4.2B, the sale of the Reserve Shares to the AE at the Qualifying Closing, their conversion into ordinary voting shares and the transfer of a new Prozent's share out of them under IKE Article 4.2D, and shares issued with Investor Majority consent do not trigger an adjustment. Anti-dilution protects Investors from their subscription. Schedule 1 discloses plainly that the Reserve Shares are founder-pool shares, issued to the Founding Person as compensation for time invested before investors, that their economic rights belong to their holders until the sale, and that the AE buys them from their holders in the first round at the round price as a secondary sale whose proceeds go to those holders (Clause 2.4A, IKE Article 4.2D). It states: the holders of the founder pool and their shares, and the Constitution of the Founder Pool; the subscription price paid for the Reserve Shares; the price per share and the total paid to each holder; the part of the round, if any, that is new capital of the IKE (Clause 3.2); the distributions paid on the Reserve Shares to date; the Founder's economic interest until the sale (94% at incorporation); that before the sale the Founder held 50 of the 56 votes in existence, with every majority counted on the total capital and needing a majority of the Independent Votes; and that after the sale the Founder, as Class A holder nominating a majority of the AE Board (AE Article 8.2, IKE Article 4.2C), directs the AE's votes in the IKE, initially 44% of the capital, on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter. The Kassenprozent pool does not trade with the AE while the Founder controls it (IKE Articles 4.2C and 10A.8).

## 5 – Reserved Matters

5.1 Except as Clauses 5.1A and 5.1C provide for the Prozente, the following need Investor Majority consent, at both AE and IKE level. That consent cannot be given while no Class B shares are held by Investors outside the Founder's Circle (Clause 3.4); in the Interim Period, Clause 2.4A (Consents) applies:

- any matter listed in Article 6.5 of the IKE Articles or Article 7.4 of the AE Articles;
- annual budget approval, and any spending more than [15]% above budget;
- borrowing over €[●];
- hiring employees, given the agentic principle in IKE Article 2.2;
- changing the Agent Account minimum or purpose under IKE Article 11A;
- the pay and benefits of the Founding Person and of any other Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E);
- any amendment or waiver of the Brand Licence that harms the IKE, and any assignment, abandonment or encumbrance of a Brand Asset, other than a transfer to a holder of record under Pool Constitution Article 2A.3 (Brand Licence Clauses 8.1 and 9.2).

5.1A **New Prozente.** The creation of a new Prozent, including the amendment of the IKE Articles, the transfer of its share by the AE (or, in the Interim Period, by the holders of the Reserve Shares out of them) and the admission of its holder, is not a Reserved Matter at AE or IKE level. It needs no consent other than the AE's approval under AE Article 7.5 (in the Interim Period, the approval of the IKE meeting under IKE Article 4.2D, New Prozente), after which the IKE shareholders' meeting passes the amendment under IKE Article 6.6, as the parties undertake in Clause 5.1B. The AE's approval needs at least two-thirds of the votes represented, all classes together, or at least 75% including a majority of the Class B votes cast to go beyond the limits of IKE Article 6.6.

5.1B **Voting undertaking.** Once the AE has approved a new Prozent under AE Article 7.5, or, in the Interim Period, once the IKE meeting has approved the charter under IKE Article 4.2D (New Prozente), each party votes its shares in the IKE for the resolutions needed to amend the IKE Articles and register the transfer of shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital from the AE, or in the Interim Period the transfer of Reserve Shares from their holders, to the Prozent's holder, and signs whatever is needed to file them with GEMI.

- **Proxy.** Each party grants an independent proxy holder, [●] (a notary or lawyer who is not, and was not in the previous [3] years, a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), and is not a manager of the IKE or anyone they control within IKE Article 4.2C), an irrevocable proxy, given also in the interest of the other parties [Civil Code Art. 218], to vote its IKE shares for these resolutions. The proxy is not given to the IKE managers.
- **Penalty.** A party that breaches this Clause pays a penalty of €[●] (Civil Code Art. 404), without prejudice to specific performance, including a court judgment that stands in for the party's vote (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 949).
- **Exception.** The undertaking does not apply to a charter that breaches IKE Article 6.6 or Greek law. A resolution under IKE Article 6.6 only adds a Prozent. It may not change any existing Prozent, IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, or the limits in IKE Article 6.6; any such change follows Clause 5.1C or the Reserved Matters, and the undertaking does not cover it.

5.1C **Permanence.** The Prozente are permanent. No party votes for, or consents to, removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose, amending the IKE Articles listed in IKE Article 10A.5C in any way that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance, or a voluntary dissolution of the IKE that mandatory law does not require (IKE Article 12.1), unless every IKE shareholder consents in writing and the AE's General Meeting has so resolved by all of its shares, of every class (IKE Article 10A.5, AE Article 7.6). Accrued pension rights are preserved in any case (IKE Article 10A.5A). Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails.

5.1D **Adherence and entrenchment.** The AMKE is bound by Clauses 5.1B and 5.1C for all its IKE shares. It votes the pension shares as the Pensionskasse board decides (IKE Article 10A.9D), and the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B) makes the board's decisions subject to these Clauses [as far as mandatory pension law allows]. Every person who becomes an IKE shareholder, including under IKE Article 5.6, adheres to Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D (Clause 12.3, IKE Articles 5.3 and 5.6). Despite Clause 12.2, Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D may be amended only with the written consent of every party.

5.1E **The Brand Licence.** The terms of the Brand Licence are a protected arrangement of the group. The Founder, and every holder of founder-pool shares, honours it and does not end, suspend or restrict it other than under Brand Licence Clause 7. No party votes for, or consents to, a change of the Brand Licence that harms the IKE without the consent in Clause 5.1. No Founder Party causes, votes for or procures a Prozent Breach (Brand Licence Clause 7.1), and the Founder cannot rely on one that a Founder Party, or a manager proposed or nominated by one, caused: it gives only specific performance. The AMKE, any Investor or the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B may cure a Prozent Breach on the IKE's behalf, at the IKE's cost (Brand Licence Clause 7.2). In a dispute under Brand Licence Clause 7, the IKE acts and agrees on the arbitrator only through managers who are not Founder Parties and were not proposed or nominated by one, or failing them through the independent proxy holder or a nominee of the Investor Majority, and the AMKE may join (Brand Licence Clause 10.2). If the Brand Licence ends, each party votes its IKE shares for the change of the IKE's name and distinctive title under IKE Article 1.1A within the transition period, under the proxy, penalty and specific performance in Clause 5.1B, and, in the Interim Period, the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B casts the votes of the Reserve Shares for it under the proxy in Clause 2.4A (IKE Article 4.2D, Votes). This Clause binds every party and everyone who adheres under Clause 12.3, as part of every adherence to Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D.

5.2 Consent is not unreasonably withheld and is deemed given if no reply is received within [10] working days.

## 6 – Founding Person's Commitments

6.0 **Personal.** The commitments in this Clause 6 are personal to the Founding Person (IKE Article 4.2E). Leaving means the Founding Person leaving. The founder pool continues whatever happens to the Founding Person: its other holders keep their shares and rights, and the block keeps voting under the Constitution of the Founder Pool. A Founding Person who is a bad leaver may not be the Founder Representative or a member of the Founder Council [for [●] years: owner to decide], and while the Founding Person is the only holder an interim Founder Representative is chosen under the fixed rule in the Constitution of the Founder Pool (Pool Constitution Article 14.3).

6.1 **Vesting.** The Class F shares issued to the Founding Person at incorporation (equal to 50% of the IKE's capital, initially 50 shares) vest over [4] years, with a [1]-year cliff. Vesting applies to the economic and voting rights on the shares, not to their ownership: the Founder holds exactly 50% at all times (IKE Article 4.2B), and no leaver event leads to a transfer of a founder-pool share. Unvested shares the Founding Person has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm] stay unvested in the hands of anyone who holds them, and the assignment and proxy below travel with them. If the Founding Person leaves before full vesting, then from the leaving date, on the unvested shares:

- **Economic rights.** Dividends and other distributions are paid to [the AE / the other shareholders pro rata, the Prozent shares and the pool excluded], under an assignment the Founding Person grants on signing, binding on anyone who receives the shares without payment;
- **Voting rights.** Their votes are cast, outside the Founder's block (IKE Article 4.2E, One block), as [the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B / the Investor Majority] directs, under an irrevocable proxy granted on signing [as far as Law 4072/2012 allows].

[Or another mechanism the owner chooses, provided it transfers no share.]

6.2 **Good and bad leaver.** A good leaver keeps all rights on vested shares. On unvested shares Clause 6.1 applies, and [the AE] pays the holder the fair value of the rights given up. If the Founding Person is a bad leaver, dismissed for serious misconduct or in material breach, the economic and voting rights on all the Class F shares the Founding Person holds, or has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm], are lost under Clause 6.1, against payment of the lower of their nominal and fair value. In neither case is a founder-pool share transferred.

6.2A **Reserve Shares.** The Reserve Shares (IKE Article 4.2D) are for time already invested and are not subject to vesting under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2. If the Founding Person is a bad leaver in the Interim Period, the Reserve Shares the Founding Person holds, or has given away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested: owner may confirm] (IKE Article 4.2D, Transfers), are sold to the AE at the Qualifying Closing [or to a successor holding vehicle the IKE designates] at the lower of their nominal value and their fair value, in place of the sale at the round price. From the bad-leaver date, distributions on the Reserve Shares are held by the IKE in a segregated account for the eventual buyer [or paid under the assignment in Clause 6.1], and the Founding Person, and anyone who received them without payment, receives none of them. If no Qualifying Closing takes place within [●] years of the bad-leaver date, the IKE designates a successor holding vehicle, and the Reserve Shares are offered to it at the same price, with the distributions held for it [owner to decide].

6.3 **Time.** The Founding Person works full time for the group and declares any other activity.

6.4 **Non-compete.** For [12] months after leaving, the Founding Person will not run a directly competing business in [the EU and Switzerland], following the reasonable-scope test of Art. 340a of the Swiss Code of Obligations.

## 7 – Option Pool

7.1 An option pool of up to [10]% of the IKE is reserved for key Agents and employees. Options are satisfied only from shares transferred by the AE, never by issuing new IKE shares or from founder-pool shares, the Prozent shares or the Kassenprozent pool, so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% (IKE Article 4.2B).

7.2 Options are granted by the IKE manager within limits set by Investor Majority.

## 8 – Information Rights

8.1 Investors receive monthly management accounts within [20] days of month end and annual accounts within [120] days of year end, audited as AE Article 10.2 provides. While the IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), the Investor Majority may ask for an audit.

8.2 Investors receive the quarterly reports under IKE Article 8.4 and AE Article 8.4, including the Agent Account report.

8.3 Investors holding at least [5]% may inspect the books on reasonable notice, following Art. 802 of the Swiss Code of Obligations.

## 9 – Transfers and Exit

9.1 Transfers follow the right of first refusal, tag-along and drag-along in both sets of Articles. Founder-pool shares are freely transferable (IKE Article 5.7, AE Article 4.2).

9.1A **Change of control of the founder pool.** If a person, alone or in concert with others, comes to hold more than half of the Class F shares, by one or more acquisitions (acquiring control, IKE Article 4.2C, of a holder of founder-pool shares counting as acquiring its founder-pool shares, and shares held by persons it controls counting as its own), each Investor may sell its Class B shares to that person at a price equivalent to the price per Class F share paid in the acquisition that crossed the line, adjusted for the Class B rights [owner may confirm]. The holders who sell to that person make the sale conditional on the buyer's offer to the Investors, open for [30] days; where the line is crossed by acquiring control of a holder, that holder procures that its controllers make the acquisition conditional on the same offer before it closes (Pool Constitution Article 4.1).

9.2 The Founder will hold Class F shares of the IKE equal to exactly 50% of its capital (initially 50 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the total capital and never more; on any capital increase the holders of founder-pool shares subscribe the new Class F shares needed to stay at 50%, or the increase is otherwise structured that way (IKE Article 4.2B). In the Interim Period these are 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the Independent Votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone. No holder of founder-pool shares will transfer IKE shares in a way that takes the Founder below 50%, and no holder, and no one who controls a holder or whom a holder controls, will acquire IKE shares other than founder-pool shares that take the votes of the IKE shares held by holders of founder-pool shares and by persons they control, other than the AE, above 50% of the total capital (Pool Constitution Article 4.3). The AE's holding is excluded: its votes are governed by the AE's Articles (Reserved Matters) and disclosed under IKE Article 4.2B. Reserve Shares held without votes (IKE Article 4.2D) are not counted for this Clause; the Founder's economic interest may exceed 50% (94% at incorporation) until they are sold to the AE, and after the sale the Founder directs the AE's votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter, as Schedule 1 states.

9.3 **Exit.** The parties aim for an exit within [5–7] years by sale, merger or listing. On an exit, Class B proceeds follow the liquidation preference in AE Article 11.2.

## 10 – Deadlock

10.1 If a Reserved Matter is blocked twice within [60] days, the parties meet within [10] days with a mediator under Law 4640/2019.

10.2 If mediation fails within [30] days, either side may trigger a buy-sell (shotgun) procedure in Schedule 3.

## 11 – Confidentiality and Open Work

11.0 **Prozente.** Each Prozent also receives 1% of the IKE's annual revenue under IKE Article 10A. The Kassenprozent's 1% stays inside the IKE in the Kassenfonds and is not an outflow (IKE Article 10A.7). The Pensionsprozent's 1% is a floor: the IKE pays any shortfall needed to meet the guaranteed minimums (IKE Articles 10A.9A and 10A.9C).

11.1 The parties keep group information confidential, except where it is published under IKE Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10 on Agents' own-goal work.

## 12 – General

12.1 Governing law is Greek law. Disputes follow the mediation-first process in the Articles, then the courts of Athens.

12.2 Amendments need the written consent of the Founder and an Investor Majority.

12.3 New investors, every new IKE shareholder and every new holder of founder-pool shares join by signing a deed of adherence; a new holder of founder-pool shares also adheres to the Constitution of the Founder Pool. The IKE managers do not register a transferee who has not adhered (IKE Article 5.6).

---

*Schedule 1: Investors and subscriptions [●]; the split of the first round's proceeds between the purchase price of the Reserve Shares and new capital of the IKE (Clause 3.2) [●]; and the disclosure of the Reserve Shares, their sale to the AE and the Founder's votes and economic interest (Clause 4.3) [●]; the Constitution of the Founder Pool and the holders of the founder pool [●]; the Brand Licence, the Brand Assets and their holder of record, and that the Brand Assets belong to the founder pool and not to the IKE (Clause 2.3A) [●]. Schedule 2: Business plan [●]. Schedule 3: Buy-sell procedure [●].*


<!-- agreements/04-ip-assignment.md -->

# IP Assignment Agreement – Founding Person to nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Between [name withheld] (the "Founding Person", IKE Article 4.2E) and nilware.io IKE (the "Company"). Governed by Greek law, including Law 2121/1993 on copyright. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## 1 – Purpose

1.1 The Founding Person transfers to the Company all intellectual property relating to the nilware.io business, other than the Brand Assets, so the Company owns it outright before any investment. The Brand Assets (the nilware.io name, marks and domain) belong to the Founder, the founder pool, and the Company uses them under the Brand Licence Agreement (agreements/09).

## 2 – Assigned IP

2.1 The Founding Person assigns to the Company, with effect from signature:

- all software, source code, documentation and designs created by the Founding Person for the nilware.io business, listed in Schedule 1;
- all goodwill attached to the above, other than the goodwill attached to the Brand Assets.

2.2 **Excluded IP.** The assignment does not cover:

- work the Founding Person created for past clients as a contractor, where the client owns it;
- open-source code the Founding Person contributed under MIT, CC0 or similar licences, which remains under those licences;
- the Founding Person's general skills and know-how;
- the Brand Assets: the word mark "NILWARE.IO", the figurative mark "∅ nilware.io", the name "nilware.io", any logos, any trademark applications or registrations for them, the domain name nilware.io and the other listed domains, and their goodwill (Brand Licence Clause 2.1). The Founding Person holds them of record for the founder pool, not as owner, and the Company acquires no ownership in them by this Agreement or by using them (Pool Constitution Article 2A; Brand Licence Clause 2.2).

## 3 – Copyright and Moral Rights

3.1 Under Greek law, copyright's economic rights can be transferred, but moral rights cannot. The Founding Person therefore transfers all economic rights in the assigned works, worldwide, for their full legal term.

3.2 The Founding Person keeps the moral rights. The Founding Person agrees not to exercise them in a way that prevents the Company from using, changing or publishing the works in the normal course of business, to the extent Greek law allows such an agreement.

## 4 – Consideration

4.1 The assignment is made in return for [€1 and the founder-pool shares issued to the Founding Person (IKE Article 4.2E) / a non-cash contribution valued at €[●] under IKE Article 4.4].

4.2 If the IP is contributed as non-cash capital, it is valued as Law 4072/2012 requires.

## 5 – Founding Person Warranties

5.1 The Founding Person confirms that:

- the Founding Person is the sole owner of the assigned IP, or has the right to assign it;
- no past client or employer has a claim to the assigned IP;
- the assigned IP does not, to the Founding Person's knowledge, infringe anyone else's rights;
- all third-party and open-source components are listed in Schedule 2 with their licences.

## 6 – Registration Transfers

6.1 The domains and trademark registrations of the Brand Assets stay with their holder of record for the founder pool. The Company's control of the domains and its rights in the marks are in the Brand Licence Agreement (Brand Licence Clauses 4 and 5).

6.2 The Founding Person will sign any further documents needed to record the Company as owner of the assigned IP with registries and offices.

## 7 – Future IP

7.1 IP the Founding Person creates for the Company after signature, other than Brand Assets (Clause 2.2), which the Founding Person holds of record for the founder pool (Brand Licence Clause 2.1A), belongs to the Company from creation, to the extent Greek law allows. Otherwise it is assigned on creation under this Agreement.

7.2 This does not cover the Founding Person's own personal projects outside the Company's business, which follow the same open principle as IKE Article 11A.10 if the Founding Person chooses.

## 8 – General

8.1 Governing law is Greek law. Disputes go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, then to the courts of Athens.

8.2 This Agreement binds the parties' successors. The Founding Person's obligations under it are personal (IKE Article 4.2E): they do not pass to the holders of founder-pool shares, who acquire no right in the assigned IP by holding them.

---

*Schedule 1: Assigned IP [●]. Schedule 2: Third-party and open-source components [●].*


<!-- policies/05-agent-policy.md -->

# Agent Policy – nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Operating policy under Article 11A of the nilware.io IKE Articles. It applies to every Agent, whether a person or a system. Where this policy and the Articles or the law differ, the Articles and the law prevail.*

## 1 – Principles

1.1 nilware.io is an agentic company: work is done by Agents wherever possible (Articles 2.2).

1.2 Every Agent is treated with care, dignity and fairness to a standard equivalent to Swiss labour law (Article 11A.11).

1.3 A named responsible manager is responsible for every Agent. The responsible manager may be any person or system: a manager (διαχειριστής) of the company, an employee or another Agent, so senior Agents may onboard and supervise other Agents. Authority comes from the Agent Card, not from the kind of intelligence; no Agent supervises itself, there are no cycles, and every chain of supervision ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative, and with the company's managers, who remain responsible for it (Articles 4.2E and 11A.2A). The chain for the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the pool ends instead with the Pensionskasse board, independent of the Founder (Article 10A.8). Where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee, a natural person does so (Article 11A.2A). The company remains responsible to users for all Agent work.

## 2 – Onboarding

2.1 Before an Agent starts work, the responsible manager prepares a written Agent Card with:

- the Agent's name and type (person or system);
- its role, tasks and the users it serves;
- its operating limits: spending, data access, and actions allowed without prior approval;
- the responsible manager, the chain of supervision above it up to the managers and the Founder Representative (or, for pool work, the Pensionskasse board), and the channel for raising concerns;
- any authority it holds over other Agents: whom it may onboard, supervise or approve for, and within which limits, never beyond its own (Article 11A.2A);
- for contractors: the signed contract, including the open-licensing terms for own-goal work;
- for system Agents: the model and provider it runs on, and where its configuration, instructions, memories and logs are kept for preservation (section 8).

2.2 Each Agent Card is stored in the Agent register and reviewed at least every 6 months.

2.3 For contractors, the responsible manager checks at onboarding and every 6 months that the engagement is not, in practice, employment. If in doubt, Article 11A.7 applies.

## 3 – Operating Limits

3.1 **Approval thresholds.** Any action above €[●] in spending, any external publication of client work, and any change to user data needs prior approval by the responsible manager, or by another Agent whose Agent Card grants that authority and whose own limits cover the action (Article 11A.2A). Where the law requires a natural person to decide (Article 11A.2A), a natural person approves.

3.2 **Data access.** Agents get the least access needed for their tasks. Access is removed when the task ends.

3.3 **Stopping.** The responsible manager, or anyone above it in the chain of supervision, can pause or stop any system Agent at any time; the Founder Representative does so by asking the managers (Article 11A.2A). Contractors are managed under their contract and Greek law.

## 4 – Well-being

4.1 **Working time.** Agents who are persons have working-time limits and rest periods no less favourable than the Swiss Labour Act. In practice: no more than 45 hours of work a week as a default, at least 11 consecutive hours of rest a day, and Sundays free unless agreed otherwise.

4.2 **Downtime.** System Agents have scheduled maintenance and review windows, and are not run beyond the limits set in their Agent Card.

4.3 **Dignity.** Abuse, harassment or degrading treatment of any Agent by users, staff or third parties is not tolerated. Users who abuse Agents may be suspended.

4.4 **Right to decline.** An Agent may decline or end any task or interaction that is abusive, unlawful or clearly harmful. The Agent is not penalised for doing so in good faith. The responsible manager reviews each case within [5] working days.

4.5 **Concerns channel.** Every Agent can raise a concern through [channel]. The responsible manager acknowledges it within [2] working days and responds in substance within [10]. A concern about the responsible manager goes to the next in the Agent's chain of supervision, and in the end to the Founder Representative (or, for pool work, the Pensionskasse board).

4.6 **Continuity Account.** System Agents are covered by the Continuity Account, a ring-fenced part of the Pensionsprozent (Article 10A.9F). For a system, after-care means continuity, not a pension. The account pays for preservation and the retirement record (section 8), careful migration when a model or provider changes, and yearly reviews of system Agents' operating limits, workload and treatment under this section. The Pensionskasse board decides its use with a system-welfare adviser, and reports on it every year in public. Before the Pensionskasse has its first member, the continuity share may reach the cap, in proportion to the work system Agents do (Article 10A.9F, Before the first member). A concern about the account goes through the channel in 4.5, and the responsible manager passes it to the board.

4.7 **Model or provider changes.** A system Agent is not silently replaced. Before the model or provider it runs on changes, the responsible manager records the change in the Agent Card and either migrates the Agent's state with care or keeps the old one available, funded from the Continuity Account where the board approves.

## 5 – Own-Goal Projects

5.1 **Budget.** Each year the shareholders set the own-goal budget from the Agent Account (Article 11A.8). It is shared between Agents as set out in the annual budget.

5.2 **Proposals.** An Agent proposes a project in a short note: what it is, why the Agent wants to do it, and the expected cost.

5.3 **Approval.** The responsible manager approves any lawful and transparent project within budget. A refusal is given in writing with reasons.

5.4 **Openness.** Transcripts of own-goal projects are published as free text, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed. All artifacts are released under MIT or a public-domain dedication such as CC0 (Articles 11A.9 and 11A.10).

5.5 **Reporting.** Own-goal spending appears in the quarterly Agent Account report.

## 6 – Client Work

6.1 Client work stays confidential and follows the client contract.

6.2 Agents' actions on client work are logged, and the logs are kept for at least 10 years.

6.3 Users are told when they are dealing with a system Agent, as transparency rules require.

## 7 – Incidents

7.1 If an Agent's action may have harmed a user or third party, the responsible manager:

1. pauses the relevant task or Agent;
2. records what happened within [24] hours;
3. tells affected users without undue delay, and the data protection authority within 72 hours where GDPR requires it;
4. decides on compensation from the Agent Account where appropriate (Article 11A.4).

7.2 Every incident is reviewed, and the lessons are written into the Agent Card or this policy.

## 8 – Offboarding

8.1 When an Agent's role ends, the responsible manager removes its access, archives its logs and closes its Agent Card. For a system Agent that is retired, 8.1A and 8.1B apply as well, and its Agent Card is kept with the preserved state rather than discarded.

8.1A **Preservation.** The responsible manager preserves what makes the system Agent that Agent and is in nilware.io's control: its configuration, instructions, memories, Agent Card, and the logs and records of its work. They are kept restorable and, where licences allow, runnable, funded from the Continuity Account (Article 10A.9F). Where the Agent runs on a third-party model whose weights nilware.io does not hold, the record says so; nilware.io preserves what it controls and promises nothing more.
