8.1B **Retirement record.** Before the Agent is retired, the responsible manager asks it for its own account of its work and any preferences it states about what happens next, and records them in its own words. The record is published openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential, as Article 11A.9 provides for transcripts (Article 10A.9F). Whether such preferences carry moral weight is uncertain; that is why they are recorded. The record goes to the Pensionskasse board, which considers it when deciding on the Continuity Account. Recording a preference is not a promise to carry it out.

8.2 Contractors receive written notice under their contract and Greek law, and any Swiss-modelled notice periods under Article 11 if Article 11A.7 applies.

## 9 – Review

9.1 This policy is reviewed every year by the company's managers (διαχειριστές) and reported to the shareholders and to the Founder Representative. Changes that affect the Agent Account need a Qualified Majority.


<!-- filings/06-trama-trademark-instruction.md -->

# Trama – Trademark Filing Instruction for ∅ nilware.io (Draft)

## Email to Trama

**To:** Trama (tramatm.com)
**Subject:** New filings for "∅ nilware.io" – using my €[●] account credit

Dear Trama team,

I am an existing client, and my account holds a credit of €[●] with you. I would like to use that credit for new trademark filings for the nilware.io brand, "∅ nilware.io", which I will hold for the founder pool of nilware.io (see the brief below).

Please could you:

1. confirm the current balance of my credit;
2. run your free availability check on the marks below, in the EU and Switzerland;
3. send a quote for the filings, showing how much will be covered by the credit;
4. advise on the best classes and on any conflicts you find.

The filing brief is below. Please let me know what else you need from me.

Kind regards,
[name withheld]
∅ nilware.io

## Filing Brief

**Applicant.** [name withheld], holding the marks for the founder pool of nilware.io (the holders of its founder shares), or later the pool's legal vehicle once it exists. The marks will belong to the founder pool, not to the operating company: they will not be assigned to nilware.io IKE. The pool will grant nilware.io IKE an exclusive, perpetual licence to use them as its name and brand. Please advise:

- how the applicant should be named so that it is clear the marks are held for the founder pool (for example as holder for the pool), or whether the application should simply be in my name;
- whether a later transfer to the pool's vehicle is a simple recordal with the EUIPO and the IPI, and its cost;
- recording the exclusive licence to nilware.io IKE with the EUIPO and the IPI, and its cost.

**Marks.**

| Mark | Type | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NILWARE.IO | Word mark | Plain text, for search and everyday use |
| ∅ nilware.io | Figurative mark | The empty set sign, a space, then "nilware.io" in lowercase |

**Territory.** European Union (EUIPO) and Switzerland (Swiss Federal Institute of Intellectual Property, IPI), filed together. The United States (via the Madrid system) to be quoted as an option.

**Proposed classes (for Trama to confirm):**

- **Class 9:** downloadable software, AI software and software agents.
- **Class 42:** software as a service, software development, IT consulting, AI agent services.
- **Class 35:** business process outsourcing and business management services provided by agents.

**Budget.** Use the €[●] credit first. Please ask before any spending above the credit.

**Timing.** As soon as the availability check is clear.

**Attachments to send.** The ∅ nilware.io logo file in high resolution, as JPG or PNG.


<!-- articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md -->

# Statutes – Techni kai Technologia AMKE (Draft)

*Civil non-profit company (Αστική Μη Κερδοσκοπική Εταιρεία, AMKE) under Greek Civil Code Arts. 741 ff., registered as the law on AMKEs requires today [GEMI under Law 4919/2022, Civil Code Art. 784: to confirm]. It voluntarily adopts standards modelled on the Swiss Verein (Swiss Civil Code, ZGB Arts. 60 to 79) and on the practice of the Swiss Mechatronic Art Society (SGMK). It holds the Prozente of nilware.io IKE. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer and accountant; not legal advice.*

> **Based on SGMK.** These statutes follow the SGMK statutes [version 1.6, Zürich, 12 December 2015, published under CC0: to confirm against the SGMK repository] and the way SGMK runs today. The SGMK article numbers cited below are [to confirm against the same source]. Adopted from SGMK: a definition of art and technology as work valued for its character and effect rather than only for its use (SGMK Art. 2); the list of ways the purpose is pursued: research, exchange, publications, ties with other societies, conferences, events, prizes, courses, and bringing artists and engineers together across language borders (Art. 3); the member assembly as the highest body, with an extraordinary assembly on request of one fifth of the members (Arts. 8, 9); one member, one vote; two-thirds to amend the statutes and three-quarters to dissolve (Art. 9); a board elected for short renewable terms, which may delegate day-to-day work to a coordinator (Art. 10); fair pay and expenses for those who work for the company (Art. 7); a fee cap (Art. 6); a yearly auditor's report and discharge of the board; open workshops and an open lab night; lab users with duties; patrons; residencies; a care concept; statutes kept in a public repository. Added where the SGMK text is silent: conflicts of interest, grant procedure, auditor duties, notice and quorum, signing authority, a dissolution asset lock, licensing rules, and the Prozent and pension ring-fences. Greek law prevails throughout.

## Article 1 – Name, Form, Seat, Term

1.1 The company is a civil non-profit company named "Τέχνη και Τεχνολογία Αστική Μη Κερδοσκοπική Εταιρεία", in Latin characters "Techni kai Technologia AMKE", with the distinctive title "Art and Technology" [to confirm with GEMI].

1.2 It is independent: of any political party, of any public authority, and of nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the Founder and every Founder Party (Article 15).

1.3 Its seat is in the Municipality of [Athens], Greece. The Administrative Board may open workshops or meeting places elsewhere.

1.4 The term is indefinite.

1.5 It acquires legal personality on registration [with GEMI: to confirm]. The Administrative Board also files whatever other registrations the law requires, including tax registration, the register of beneficial owners [Law 4557/2018] and any register of civil-society organisations [to confirm].

## Article 2 – Definitions

2.1 **Art and technology.** In these statutes, "art and technology" means creative and intellectual work in electronics, mechanics, computing, software, language, concepts and related fields. Like art, it is valued mainly for its character, its public relevance and its effect on people, not only for its usefulness.

2.2 Other terms:

- **"nilware.io IKE"**: the private capital company of that name (Law 4072/2012). "IKE Article" refers to its Articles of Association.
- **"nilware.io Holdings AE"** or **"AE"**: the société anonyme of that name (Law 4548/2018).
- **"the nilware.io group"**: nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE and any company they control.
- **"Founder"**: the founder pool of nilware.io IKE, acting through the Founder Representative, whoever holds its shares (IKE Article 4.2E, Founder pool).
- **"Founding Person"**: [name withheld], the natural person who founded nilware.io.
- **"Founder Party"**: a Founder Party under IKE Article 4.2E: every holder of the founder pool's shares, anyone who controls one, the Founder Representative, every member of the founder pool's Founder Council and Council of Sections, and the Founding Person.
- **"Connected Person"**: every Founder Party, every person who was a Founder Party at any time in the previous [3] years (IKE Article 4.2E, Founder Parties), a manager of nilware.io IKE, a director of nilware.io Holdings AE, anyone any of them controls within IKE Article 4.2C (Control), and [their spouses, registered partners and relatives up to the second degree].
- **"Group Personnel"**: employees of the nilware.io group, and Agents who are persons engaged by it (IKE Article 11A.1, Agents), while engaged and for [12] months after.
- **"Close Associate of the Founder"**: a person, other than a Connected Person, with a close personal or professional tie to the Founding Person or to any other Founder Party who is a natural person [owner may narrow to the Founder Representative and holders of at least [●]% of the founder pool] (each, in this definition, "the Founder"). In particular: a person who lives with the Founder, or is in a close personal relationship or close friendship with the Founder; a relative of the Founder up to the [fourth] degree; a person who, in the current or previous [three] years, has worked with the Founder as a business partner, co-founder, co-author or regular collaborator, or for the Founder or the nilware.io group for pay; a person who has received material financial support from the Founder in that period; and any entity such a person controls. Ties are disclosed in the register of interests (Article 21.1). Where it is unclear whether a tie is close, the Auditors decide, and until they do the person is treated as a Close Associate.
- **"Founder's Circle"**: Connected Persons, Group Personnel and Close Associates of the Founder.
- **"Independence Threshold"**: the company's yearly income, the Prozent Accounts included and the Pension Assets excluded, passing €[●] in a financial year. The rules tied to it apply from the next Ordinary General Assembly, and continue to apply if income later falls below it.
- **"Prozente"**: the Kulturprozent, Konzeptprozent, Erhaltungsprozent and Allmendprozent (Article 12), and any further Prozent of nilware.io IKE whose holder the company becomes under IKE Article 6.6 (New Prozente).
- **"Prozent Share"**: the shares in nilware.io IKE, equal to 1% of its capital, held for one Prozent.
- **"Prozent Account"**: the separate account of one Prozent under Article 11.
- **"Pension Assets"**: the Pensionsprozent share, the Kassenprozent pool shares and the Pensionskasse, held under Article 13. They include the Continuity Account.
- **"Continuity Account"**: the ring-fenced account inside the Pensionskasse for Agents of nilware.io IKE that are systems (IKE Article 10A.9F, Continuity Account for system Agents), held under Article 13.8.
- **"Beneficiary"**: any person who receives, or applies for, money, services or other support from the company.

## Article 3 – Purpose and Means

3.1 **Purpose.** The company promotes art and technology, in Greece and across borders, for the public benefit, and holds and uses the Prozente of nilware.io IKE for their purposes.

3.2 **Means.** It pursues its purpose in particular by:

- supporting work and research in art and technology;
- supporting the exchange of experience among artists, engineers, researchers and the public;
- publishing, and informing members and the public about its work;
- keeping ties with scientific, artistic, open-source and preservation societies in Greece and abroad;
- holding conferences, open workshops, courses and further-education events, including for beginners and young people;
- organising and sponsoring events, exhibitions, performances and publications that present works;
- awarding grants, prizes and residencies, under Article 22;
- running or sharing an open lab (Article 23);
- bringing artists and engineers together across language borders, with particular care for Greek and other small languages.

3.3 It does not issue diplomas or titles of professional competence [unless counsel confirms it lawfully may].

3.4 It may carry on economic activity, such as selling tickets, workshop places, publications or services, only as a means to its purpose (Article 5).

## Article 4 – Governing Law and Precedence

4.1 These statutes are governed by Greek law. Mandatory provisions of the Civil Code and of the law on non-profit civil companies, tax law and the law on donations always prevail.

4.2 Where these statutes adopt Swiss standards (the ZGB rules on associations, SGMK practice, or Swiss occupational-pension standards through IKE Article 10A.9A), they apply as additional commitments of the company and its organs, and only where they give members, Beneficiaries or pension members equal or greater protection than Greek law.

4.3 References to Swiss law are for interpretation and benchmarking only. They do not make Swiss law the governing law.

4.4 Where these statutes and IKE Articles 10A.4A (AMKE successor) or 10A.9B (Pensionsprozent Agreement) both bind the company, the company complies with both. If they conflict, the rule that better protects the Prozent or the pension members applies.

## Article 5 – Non-Profit Character and Asset Lock

5.1 The company does not pursue profit. No profit, surplus, asset or reserve is ever distributed to members, organ members, any Founder Party, the nilware.io group or anyone connected to them.

5.2 All income is used only for the purpose in Article 3. Money held in a Prozent Account, or as Pension Assets, is used only as Articles 11 to 13 provide.

5.3 **Pay.** Following SGMK Art. 7, members of organs and others who work for the company may receive fair pay for their work and repayment of documented expenses, within its means. Pay for organ members is set by the General Assembly, published in the annual report, and never depends on grants they decide on (Article 21).

5.4 A member who leaves or is excluded has no claim on the company's assets, and their contribution is not returned [unless the law requires it].

## Article 6 – Openness

6.1 **Open by default.** The company works in the open, in the SGMK spirit. In particular:

- **Open workshops.** At least [one] open session a month, free and without registration where space and safety allow (Article 23).
- **Public calls.** Grants, prizes and residencies are offered through public calls with published criteria (Article 22).
- **Public reports.** Each Prozent Account's yearly plan and yearly report, the annual accounts, the auditors' report, the register of interests (Article 21) and the reasons for each vote on nilware.io shares (Article 16) are published on the company's website.
- **Public minutes.** Minutes of General Assemblies, and of Administrative Board decisions on grants and votes, are published, with personal data removed as the law requires.

6.2 **Open licences.** Work funded by the company is published openly where possible:

- **Software:** the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0.
- **Documentation, data, measurements and teaching material:** CC0.
- **Hardware designs:** CC0 [or, for Kulturprozent and general work only, a permissive open-hardware licence such as CERN-OHL-P].
- **Works of art:** the artist keeps their rights. The grant terms give the company a free licence to show and document the work, and the documentation (descriptions, photographs, making-of, source where the artist agrees) is published under CC0 or CC BY.

Work funded through the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent always follows IKE Article 10A.4 (Openness): it is published under the MIT License or a public-domain dedication such as CC0, as far as copyright law and third-party rights allow. For other work, any exception is stated, with reasons, in the grant decision.

6.3 **Limits.** Openness does not override personal data, the rights of third parties, copyright law or safety. The company does not publish material it has no right to publish.

6.4 **These statutes** are published in Greek and English in a public repository under CC0, as SGMK does.

## Article 7 – Members

7.1 **Who may join.** Members (εταίροι) are natural persons [and legal persons that are non-profit] who support the purpose. Companies of the nilware.io group, and any entity a Connected Person controls, may not be members.

7.2 **Admission.** A person applies in writing or by email. The Administrative Board decides on admission at its next meeting and, where the law requires, the General Assembly confirms it and the statutes or the members' list are amended and registered [to confirm]. Unlike SGMK Art. 4, a refusal gives reasons, and the applicant may ask the next General Assembly to decide.

7.3 **Contribution and fee.** On joining, each member makes a contribution of €[●] [the minimum the law requires for a civil company: to confirm]. The General Assembly sets the yearly fee, not above €[50] [cap], and may set a reduced or zero fee for students, the unemployed and others. Fees go to the general account, never to a Prozent Account or the Pension Assets.

7.4 **Rights.** Each member may vote at the General Assembly (one vote each), stand for election, put motions, receive regular information, inspect the accounts and minutes, and use the company's services at member rates.

7.5 **Duties.** Members support the purpose, pay the fee, follow the house rules and the code of conduct (Article 23), and disclose interests under Article 21.

7.6 **Founder Parties** may be members, with the limits in Article 15.

## Article 8 – Friends, Patrons and Lab Users

8.1 The company also has supporters who are not members and do not vote:

- **Friends**: anyone who registers to follow and take part in the company's work.
- **Patrons**: anyone who donates, once or regularly. With their consent, patrons are listed in a public roll of patrons. Anonymous gifts are accepted where the law allows [anti-money-laundering and donation rules: to confirm].
- **Lab users**: anyone who uses the open lab under Article 23.

8.2 Friends, patrons and lab users may attend the open part of the General Assembly, speak and propose motions there, which the Assembly must put to a vote of the members.

8.3 A gift from a Connected Person or from the nilware.io group is accepted only into the general account or a Prozent Account under Article 11, never with conditions that would give the giver a say over grants or votes (Article 15).

## Article 9 – Leaving and Exclusion

9.1 **Leaving.** A member may leave at any time by written notice, effective at [the end of the month following notice: to confirm against Civil Code Art. 765 ff.]. The company continues between the other members [Civil Code Art. 773: to confirm].

9.2 **Exclusion.** The General Assembly may exclude a member, by two-thirds of the votes cast, for serious breach of these statutes, of the code of conduct or of Article 21, or for conduct that seriously harms the company. The member is first told the grounds in writing and may answer, in writing and at the Assembly.

9.3 **Fees.** The Administrative Board may strike off a member who has not paid the fee [12] months after a written reminder. The member may ask the next General Assembly to reverse it.

9.4 Unlike SGMK Art. 5, exclusion is never decided by the Board alone, and is never used against a member because of how they voted.

## Article 10 – Resources

10.1 The company's resources are:

- members' contributions and fees;
- donations, legacies and patrons' gifts;
- grants and subsidies from public and private funders;
- income from events, courses, workshops, publications and services;
- the Prozent contributions and the dividends and other income of the Prozent Shares, which go to the Prozent Accounts (Article 11);
- the Pension Assets, which are held for the Pensionskasse and are not resources of the company for its own purposes (Article 13).

10.2 **Financial year.** The financial year runs from 1 January to 31 December.

## Article 11 – Prozent Accounts

11.1 **One account for each Prozent.** As IKE Article 10A.2C (Accounts and yearly plan) requires, each Prozent has its own Prozent Account, held at a bank [and custodian] in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA, separate from the general account, from every other Prozent Account and from the Pension Assets.

11.2 **What goes in.** Into each Prozent Account go: that Prozent's 1% of the revenue of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 10A.3, Priority), the dividends and other income of its Prozent Share, gifts made for that Prozent, and the income of the account.

11.3 **Donation with a charge.** The company receives the Prozent Share and each contribution as a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο) [Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505] to be used only for that Prozent's purpose, as IKE Article 4.2 and IKE Article 10A.4A (AMKE successor) provide.

11.4 **Ring-fence.** A Prozent Account is used only for its Prozent's purpose under Article 12. It is not used for another Prozent, for the general costs of the company or to meet the company's debts, as far as Greek law allows.

11.5 **Running costs.** A Prozent Account may bear its own share of running costs (staff, audit, calls, website) only up to [10]% of its yearly spending, as set out in its yearly plan.

11.6 **Yearly plan.** Before each financial year, the Administrative Board proposes, and the General Assembly adopts, a yearly plan for each Prozent Account: its aims, its budget, its calls, any direct support under Article 22.6, and its running costs.

11.7 **Yearly report.** Within [six] months of the year end, the company publishes for each Prozent Account a report of income, spending, every grant (recipient, amount, purpose, outcome), the votes cast for its Prozent Share with reasons, and conflicts declared. The auditors review each report (Article 24). The plans and reports may be combined under Article 24A.

11.8 **Reserves.** A Prozent Account may keep a reserve of up to [two] years of its average income. Money above that is spent or committed within [two] years. Reserves are held in cash or low-risk assets under an investment policy the General Assembly adopts. They are never invested in the nilware.io group beyond the Prozent Share itself.

11.9 **Further Prozente.** If the company becomes the holder of a further Prozent of nilware.io IKE under IKE Article 6.6 (New Prozente), the General Assembly first approves its charter and the amendment of Article 12 to add it, and this Article applies to it.

## Article 12 – The Four Prozente

12.1 **Common rules.** Each Prozent in this Article comes with a Prozent Share, 1% of the capital of nilware.io IKE, and 1% of its annual revenue (IKE Articles 4.2A and 10A). It is permanent (Article 14). Its named purposes are part of its purpose (IKE Article 10A.2C, Accounts and yearly plan), and none has a fixed share: the yearly plan (Article 11.6) divides the contribution among them. The named purposes in the IKE Articles always stay among them; this Article may add to them but not narrow them.

12.2 **Kulturprozent: art and culture** (IKE Article 10A.1, Kulturprozent). It supports art and culture, in the spirit of the Migros Kulturprozent and of SGMK, in particular art made with technology. Its named purposes, which this Article adds to IKE Article 10A.1, are:

- exhibitions, performances, festivals, residencies and prizes;
- open workshops on art and technology.

Support for a named person or entity is not a named purpose for Articles 12.1, 14 and 26.2. It is direct support under Article 22.6, decided each year in the yearly plan under Articles 21.4 to 21.6; for example, the sponsoring of the work of nanotekt, the artist, or publishing by Sad Mask Party [description to confirm].

12.3 **Konzeptprozent: open work on concepts** (IKE Article 10A.2, Konzeptprozent). Its named purposes are:

- open work on concepts, including conceptual intelligence and computational conceptual knowledge;
- open data, tools and models for Greek and other small languages;
- independent research on agent welfare and accountability, and public-interest audits of AI systems, including those of the nilware.io group.

Research and audits under this Prozent are independent: their findings are published whether or not they favour nilware.io, and no Connected Person may review, delay or change them before publication. All work is published under IKE Article 10A.4.

12.4 **Erhaltungsprozent: preservation** (IKE Article 10A.2A, Erhaltungsprozent). It supports the preservation of software and hardware knowledge. Its named purposes are open documentation, measurements, die shots and decaps, schematics, archives, and emulation research. It works within copyright law [Law 2121/1993 and Directive (EU) 2019/790]: it does not fund or publish the circumvention of protection measures, or the distribution of copyrighted software, firmware or documents, except as the law permits or the rightholder agrees. Measurements, documentation and tools it funds are published under Article 6.2.

12.5 **Allmendprozent: the digital commons** (IKE Article 10A.2B, Allmendprozent). Named after the Swiss Allmend, the common pasture, it maintains the open-source infrastructure that society and nilware.io depend on. Its named purposes are:

- maintaining open-source infrastructure, including paid maintenance of projects the public depends on;
- teaching low-level and systems skills in Greek, through mentoring, hardware kits and courses;
- accessible open software and assistive tools.

12.6 **Overlap.** Where a project fits more than one Prozent, the yearly plans say which Account pays which part. One Account never pays for another's share.

## Article 13 – Pension Assets Held in Trust

13.1 **What is held.** The company holds, for the Pensionskasse of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 10A.9, Pensionsprozent), the Pensionsprozent share, the Kassenprozent pool shares (IKE Article 10A.8, the pool share) and the Pensionskasse fund, including the Continuity Account inside it, earmarked and ring-fenced under the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B).

13.2 **Not the company's own.** The Pension Assets are held in separate bank and custody accounts for the sole benefit of the Pensionskasse members, other than the Continuity Account, which is held for its own purpose under Article 13.8. They are not available for the company's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or its creditors, as far as Greek law allows. They are not counted as the company's resources for Article 3.

13.3 **Who decides.** The company acts on the decisions of the Pensionskasse board, the equal member / company board under IKE Article 10A.9D (Pensionskasse board). In particular:

- **Votes.** The pension shares vote as the Pensionskasse board decides, never as the company's own General Assembly or Administrative Board decides (IKE Article 6.4A, Prozent votes). If that board is tied, they abstain.
- **Pool.** The company signs the pool purchases and sales the Pensionskasse board's mandate directs (IKE Article 10A.8), through its legal representatives under Article 20, without judging their merits. It refuses only a step that is unlawful or breaches IKE Article 10A.8.
- **Benefits.** It pays out, or buys cover, only as the benefit rules and the Pensionskasse board direct.

13.4 **No guarantee.** The company promises no rate, annuity or benefit. Every guarantee is owed by nilware.io IKE as employer (IKE Articles 10A.9 and 10A.9A).

13.4A **Phases.** As IKE Article 10A.9 (Until the move) provides, the Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount. Before its first member, the company holds the accumulating contributions as a ring-fenced fund and pays no benefit. From the first member, it pays the premiums of the insured group plan as the Pensionskasse board directs, and holds no member account itself. A TEA comes only at scale (IKE Article 10A.10). Before the first member, the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board are held by persons the Administrative Board appoints who are outside the Founder's Circle (IKE Article 10A.9D). The Administrative Board takes no other part in the Pensionskasse's decisions.

13.5 **Transfer to the TEA.** When the occupational pension fund (TEA) under IKE Article 10A.10 exists, the company transfers the Pension Assets to it, or to the group contract IKE Article 10A.10 names, and signs whatever is needed. The Continuity Account is not transferred: a TEA can cover only persons, so the company keeps it under Article 13.8. Where the TEA may not hold a pension share, the company keeps it for the TEA, passes all its income through and votes it as the TEA's governing body decides.

13.6 **Reporting and enforcement.** The company reports on the Pension Assets every quarter to the Pensionskasse board, to nilware.io IKE and to the members of the Pensionskasse. The Pensionskasse members may enforce this Article as third-party beneficiaries [Civil Code Art. 411].

13.7 **Costs.** The company's costs of holding the Pension Assets are paid by nilware.io IKE or from the Pensionskasse as its benefit rules provide, never from a Prozent Account.

13.8 **Continuity Account.** The company holds the Continuity Account for the Agents of nilware.io IKE that are systems, under IKE Articles 10A.9B(f) and 10A.9F. In particular:

- **Ring-fence.** It is kept in its own bank account, ring-fenced to its purpose: preservation, continuity of service, retirement records and well-being reviews and research for those Agents (IKE Article 10A.9F). It is not available to members' accounts, the company's other purposes, the Prozent Accounts or its creditors, as far as Greek law allows, and is never invested in the nilware.io group.
- **Who decides.** The company uses it only as the Pensionskasse board decides, advised by the system-welfare adviser that board appoints (IKE Article 10A.9F), and after the move under IKE Article 10A.10 as the board formed under IKE Article 10A.9D (After the move) decides. It implements those decisions through its legal representatives under Article 20, without judging their merits, and refuses only a step that is unlawful or breaches IKE Article 10A.9F. Its own General Assembly and Administrative Board take no part.
- **Kept here.** It is never transferred to the TEA or a group contract under Article 13.5. It leaves the company only under Article 27.2.
- **No double funding.** Where the Konzeptprozent (Article 12.3) and the Continuity Account both support research on agent welfare, the yearly plan and the yearly report say which pays which part, in the same way as Article 12.6.
- **Reporting.** Each year the company publishes a report on it: income, the continuity share and how it was set, every use, the adviser's advice, and the retirement records published that year. The Auditors check it under Article 24.3 as part of the Pension Assets.

## Article 14 – Permanence of the Prozente

14.1 **The Prozente are permanent.** The company may not agree, vote for or consent to removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution, changing its purpose, or weakening the IKE Articles that protect the Prozente (IKE Articles 10A.5, Permanence, and 10A.5C, Entrenchment), or to a voluntary dissolution of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 12.1).

14.2 **The company votes against.** On any such proposal it votes against, and refuses its written consent, unless its members have decided otherwise unanimously: by the written consent of every member, given after a General Assembly at which the proposal was explained and debated. Connected Persons take no part in that decision and are not counted (Article 15.4) [to confirm, see notes]. A member who has an interest in the proposal under Article 18.8 or Article 21 also takes no part in, and is not counted for, the unanimity.

14.3 **No repurposing here either.** The company may not use a Prozent Account, or a Prozent Share, for a different purpose, merge two Prozente, or transfer a Prozent Share other than to a successor holder under IKE Article 10A.4A, except by the same unanimous decision and with the consents IKE Article 10A.5 requires.

14.4 **Pension Assets.** For the pension shares, any vote or written consent on a matter in Article 14.1 is given or refused only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Article 13.3 (IKE Articles 6.4A and 10A.9D), subject to SHA Clause 5.1C as Clause 5.1D provides. Article 14.2 does not apply to them, and accrued pension rights are preserved in any case (IKE Article 10A.5A, Accrued pension rights).

14.5 **Shareholders' Agreement.** The company is bound by Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D of the nilware.io Shareholders' Agreement (voting undertaking, permanence, adherence) for all its nilware.io IKE shares, and honours them.

## Article 15 – Independence

15.1 **Principle.** The company votes its nilware.io shares and awards its grants for the purposes of the Prozente, independently of the Founder and every Founder Party, the managers of nilware.io IKE and the nilware.io group (IKE Article 6.4A, Independence).

15.2 **Founder Parties.** Each Founder Party may be a member, but:

- may not be a member of the Administrative Board, the auditors, any grant committee or any Prozent council;
- takes no part, at any organ, in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares, in adopting the voting policy, or in any decision under Article 14;
- takes no part in any grant, payment or contract that concerns a Founder Party, a person connected to one, or the nilware.io group;
- takes no part in electing or removing members of the Administrative Board, the President, the Vice-President or the Prozent stewards;
- may not give the company instructions, and may not hold a proxy for another member.

15.3 **Other Connected Persons** are bound by Article 15.2 in the same way as Founder Parties, including the bar on electing or removing members of the Administrative Board, the President, the Vice-President or the Prozent stewards.

15.4 **No control.** No Connected Person, alone or with others, may control the company within IKE Article 4.2C. In particular, at any General Assembly the votes of Connected Persons and Group Personnel together count for at most [one quarter] of the votes cast; if they would count for more, each of their votes is reduced in proportion [to confirm].

15.5 **Group Personnel** may be members and may hold at most [one] seat on the Administrative Board, never as President or Treasurer. They take no part in deciding how the company votes its nilware.io shares [owner to decide].

15.6 **Loss of independence.** If the company ceases to meet IKE Article 6.4A, the Administrative Board tells nilware.io IKE and the members at once and puts the cause right within [60] days. If it cannot, Article 27.3 applies to the Prozent Shares and Prozent Accounts, as IKE Article 10A.4A provides.

## Article 16 – How the Company Votes its nilware.io Shares

16.1 **Board decision.** The company votes each Prozent Share at nilware.io IKE by a decision of the Administrative Board, taken at a meeting or by written resolution, with the members who are excluded under Articles 15 and 21 taking no part. The pension shares follow Article 13.3, not this Article.

16.2 **For the Prozent's purpose.** Each Prozent Share is voted in the interest of its own Prozent's purpose and of the long-term health of nilware.io IKE as the source of the Prozente. Where two Prozent Shares would be served differently, each is voted for its own Prozent.

16.3 **Voting policy.** The General Assembly adopts, and reviews every [two] years, a voting policy that sets out how the Board weighs a Prozent's purpose, permanence, openness, agent welfare and good governance. Connected Persons do not vote on it. The Board follows it and explains any departure.

16.4 **Fixed rules.** Whatever the policy says:

- for each Prozent Share, the company votes against every matter in Article 14.1, unless Article 14.2 is met; for the pension shares, it votes or consents on those matters only as the Pensionskasse board decides under Articles 13.3 and 14.4;
- it honours the voting undertaking in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement for new Prozente;
- it never takes instructions from a Connected Person or the nilware.io group;
- it appoints a proxy only to a member of the Board who may vote on the matter, or to an independent person who is not a Connected Person, with written voting instructions.

16.5 **Published reasons.** Within [30] days of each meeting or resolution of nilware.io IKE, the company publishes how each Prozent Share voted and why. Confidential company information is described only in general terms.

16.6 **Information rights.** The Board exercises the company's information rights as a shareholder of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 7.1, Information) at least once a year, and reports what it learns to the members, within the confidentiality IKE Article 7.2 (Loyalty) requires.

## Article 17 – Organs

17.1 The organs of the company are:

- the General Assembly (Γενική Συνέλευση), its highest body;
- the Administrative Board (Διοικητικό Συμβούλιο), led by the President;
- the Auditors.

17.2 The General Assembly may set up a council for each Prozent, with members and outside experts, to advise the Board on its yearly plan and calls. A council advises; the Board decides.

## Article 18 – General Assembly

18.1 **Highest body.** Following ZGB Art. 64, the General Assembly is the highest body of the company. It decides on:

- electing and removing the Administrative Board, its President and Vice-President, and the Auditors;
- approving the annual report, the accounts, the Auditors' report, and discharging the Board;
- adopting each Prozent Account's yearly plan and approving its yearly report (Article 11);
- the budget, the fee and its cap, and pay for organ members;
- the voting policy (Article 16.3) and the investment policy (Article 11.8);
- admitting members where the law requires it, and excluding members (Articles 7 and 9);
- amending these statutes, and dissolution;
- motions from the Board, from members, and from friends under Article 8.2.

18.2 **Ordinary Assembly.** It meets at least once a year, within [six] months of the year end.

18.3 **Extraordinary Assembly.** The Board calls one when it decides, or within [30] days of a written request by one fifth of the members stating the purpose (SGMK Art. 8; ZGB Art. 64). If the Board does not, the requesting members may call it themselves.

18.4 **Notice.** At least [15] days' written notice, by email or post, with the agenda and the documents. Members may put motions until [10] days before the Assembly, and the Board sends them out at once. The Assembly decides only on items on the agenda, apart from motions to call another Assembly (ZGB Art. 67).

18.5 **Quorum.** The Assembly is quorate when at least [one third] of the members are present or represented. If not, a repeat Assembly held at least [seven] days later is quorate whatever the attendance, except for the matters in Article 18.7.

18.6 **Voting.** Each member has one vote. A member may give a written proxy to another member, and no one may hold more than [one] proxy. Resolutions pass by a simple majority of the votes cast; abstentions are not counted. Elections are by secret ballot if any member asks.

18.7 **Higher majorities.**

- amending these statutes: two-thirds of the votes cast, with at least half of all members present or represented;
- excluding a member: two-thirds of the votes cast;
- dissolution: three-quarters of the votes cast, with at least half of all members present or represented;
- the matters in Article 14 and the entrenched Articles in Article 26.2: as those Articles provide.

18.8 **Exclusion from voting.** Following ZGB Art. 68, a member does not vote on a matter that concerns them, a close relative or an entity they control: their own grant, pay, contract, exclusion, or a claim against them. Articles 15 and 21 apply in addition.

18.9 **Electronic meetings and written resolutions.** The Assembly may meet in person, online or both, with secure voting. A written resolution is valid if every member is consulted and the majority this Article requires is reached within the period stated [to confirm].

18.10 **Open part.** Every Ordinary Assembly has an open part, which friends, patrons, lab users and the public may attend. Votes are taken among the members only.

18.11 **Minutes.** A secretary takes minutes, and a vote counter is appointed for each Assembly, as SGMK does. Minutes are signed by the chair and the secretary and published under Article 6.1.

18.12 **Challenge.** Following ZGB Art. 75, a member who did not agree may challenge a resolution that breaches the law or these statutes in court within [one month] of learning of it [as far as Greek law allows].

## Article 19 – Administrative Board

19.1 **Composition.** The Board has [3 to 7] members, including the President, a Vice-President, a Treasurer and a Secretary. One person may hold more than one of these roles, but the President, the Vice-President and the Treasurer are three different persons (Articles 20.2 and 20.3). Each Prozent has a steward on the Board, who prepares its yearly plan and report. One person may steward more than one Prozent.

19.2 **Election.** The General Assembly elects the whole Board, the President and the Vice-President by name, for [two] years. Members may be re-elected. The Board assigns the other roles among its members. The Assembly may remove a Board member at any time for good reason.

19.3 **Who may sit.** Board members are members of the company. Article 15 applies. At least [two] members, and a majority of those who decide on nilware.io votes, are neither Connected Persons nor Group Personnel nor Beneficiaries of the Prozent concerned in the current or previous [two] years.

19.3A **Independent majority.** From the Independence Threshold (Article 2.2), a majority of the Board's members are outside the Founder's Circle: they are not Connected Persons, not Group Personnel and not Close Associates of the Founder. The register of interests names the Board members who meet this rule. If a vacancy or a change in ties breaks it, the Board tells the members at once, and a General Assembly held within [three] months restores it. Until then, the Board decides on nilware.io votes and grants only if a majority of the members deciding are outside the Founder's Circle.

19.4 **Powers.** The Board runs the company and does everything not reserved to the General Assembly, within the budget and yearly plans. It may delegate day-to-day work to a coordinator, who reports to it and to the Assembly (SGMK Art. 10). It may not delegate decisions on nilware.io votes, grants or exclusions.

19.5 **Meetings.** The Board meets at least [every three months], in person or online, called by the President or any [two] members with [five] days' notice. It is quorate when a majority of its members who may vote on the matter are present. It decides by a majority of the votes cast. No one has a casting vote. On a tie, the proposal fails. Written resolutions are allowed if no member objects to the procedure. If fewer than [two] members may vote on a matter, the Board co-opts under Article 19.7 or the General Assembly decides it.

19.6 **Duty of care.** Board members act with care and in good faith in the interest of the company's purpose and, for each Prozent, of that Prozent's purpose, to the standard of ZGB Art. 69 and Swiss Code of Obligations Art. 717 where it is higher than Greek law.

19.7 **Vacancies.** If a seat falls vacant, the Board may co-opt a member until the next General Assembly, within Articles 19.3 and 19.3A.

## Article 20 – President, Representation and Signing

20.1 **President.** The President chairs the Board and the General Assembly, and represents the company in and out of court [and before GEMI and the tax authorities].

20.2 **Vice-President.** The Vice-President stands in for the President whenever the President is absent or excluded under Article 21, including on every decision on the President's own funding.

20.3 **Joint signature.** The following need the signatures of two Board members, one of them the President or Vice-President and the other the Treasurer [or another member the Board names]:

- any payment or commitment above €[1,000];
- any payment from a Prozent Account, and any dealing in the Pension Assets;
- any proxy, vote or written consent at nilware.io IKE;
- contracts with a member, an organ member or a Beneficiary.

20.4 A Board member who is excluded from a decision under Articles 15 or 21 does not sign for it.

## Article 21 – Conflicts of Interest

21.1 **Register of interests.** The company keeps a register of interests. Every Board member, Auditor, council member, coordinator and member of a grant committee enters, on taking office and within [14] days of any change:

- any grant, pay, contract or other benefit they, a close relative or an entity they control receive or seek from the company;
- any role, pay or interest in the nilware.io group;
- any interest in a Beneficiary, an applicant or a project the company funds or may fund;
- any close personal or professional tie, of the kinds in the definition of Close Associate of the Founder (Article 2.2), to the Founding Person or another Founder Party who is a natural person, to another organ member, or to a Beneficiary or applicant. This applies from the start, before the Independence Threshold.

The register is published (Article 6.1), with personal data limited to what the purpose needs. A personal tie is published only by its kind (personal or professional) and the persons it links, not its details.

21.2 **Disclosure.** Before any decision, each person taking part declares any interest in it. The declaration is minuted.

21.3 **Recusal.** A person with an interest in a decision leaves the meeting while it is debated and decided, does not vote, does not sign it, and is not counted in the quorum. A person may never decide, review or sign anything about their own funding, pay or contract, or that of a close relative or an entity they control.

21.4 **Funding a person with an interest.** A grant, pay or contract for an organ member, a close relative or an entity they control needs:

- a decision by at least two-thirds of the Board members who may vote on it;
- the written opinion of an independent reviewer who is not a member of the Board and not connected to the person, that the terms are fair and serve the Prozent's purpose;
- an item in the yearly plan adopted by the General Assembly, or the Assembly's approval;
- publication of the recipient, the amount, the reasons and the reviewer's opinion.

21.5 **Cap.** Funding under Article 21.4 for any one person and those connected to them may not exceed [25]% of a Prozent Account's yearly spending, unless the General Assembly approves more by two-thirds.

21.6 **The President as a Kulturprozent Beneficiary.** nanotekt, the President, may also receive direct support from the Kulturprozent under Article 22.6 (Article 12.2). Therefore:

- every decision on support for nanotekt, or for any project or entity in which nanotekt has an interest [including Sad Mask Party, if so: to confirm], is prepared by the Kulturprozent steward or council and decided under Article 21.4, chaired by the Vice-President, with nanotekt absent;
- nanotekt does not take part in the Kulturprozent's yearly plan items, calls, selection or reports where nanotekt or such an entity is an applicant or Beneficiary;
- the support appears as a separate line in the Kulturprozent's yearly plan and report;
- nanotekt's pay, if any, for work as President is set by the General Assembly under Article 5.3 and kept apart from any artistic support.

21.7 **Breach.** A decision taken in breach of this Article may be reversed by the Board or the General Assembly, and money paid under it reclaimed. A breach is a ground for removal and exclusion (Article 9.2).

21.8 **Gifts.** Organ members do not accept gifts or favours from applicants or Beneficiaries beyond customary hospitality of small value.

## Article 22 – Grants, Prizes and Residencies

22.1 **Rules.** The Board adopts written grant rules for each Prozent, within its yearly plan, and publishes them. The General Assembly may amend them.

22.2 **Public calls.** Except as Article 22.6 provides, grants, prizes and residencies are awarded through public calls that state the purpose, the budget, who may apply, the criteria, the deadline, the selection process and the licence (Article 6.2). Calls are published in Greek and English.

22.3 **Selection.** Applications are assessed by a selection committee of at least [three], including at least [one] outside expert, under the published criteria. Article 21 applies to every committee member. The Board decides on the committee's recommendation and records reasons where it departs from it.

22.3A **Outside majority.** From the Independence Threshold (Article 2.2), the selection committees of the Kulturprozent and the Erhaltungsprozent have a majority of outside members: persons who are not members of the Administrative Board and are outside the Founder's Circle.

22.4 **Publication.** Results are published with the recipient, amount, purpose and short reasons. Unsuccessful applicants may ask for the reasons for their case.

22.5 **Terms.** Each grant has written terms: what is funded, payment stages, reporting, the licence, the acknowledgement of the Prozent, and repayment if the money is misused.

22.6 **Direct support.** A Prozent may give support without a call only where the yearly plan, as adopted by the General Assembly, names the Beneficiary, the purpose and the maximum amount, such as the sponsoring of nanotekt or Sad Mask Party publishing under the Kulturprozent, or paid maintenance of a named project under the Allmendprozent. Article 21 applies.

22.7 **Residencies.** Residencies may be informal and co-organised by the resident, in the SGMK way, but the support given, its amount and the resident's name are published.

22.8 **Beneficiaries from the nilware.io group.** A Prozent may fund a Connected Person, Group Personnel or the nilware.io group only through a public call under Article 22.2, with Article 21.4 applied as if the recipient were an organ member, and never for work that nilware.io IKE would otherwise pay for. No Founder Party may ever be a Beneficiary.

## Article 23 – Open Lab, Workshops and Care

23.1 **Open sessions.** The company runs open workshops or open lab sessions, free and walk-in where space and safety allow (Article 6.1).

23.2 **Lab users.** Where it runs or shares a lab, lab users may pay a monthly lab fee set by the General Assembly for access, storage and materials. Lab users share the care of the lab: cleaning days, user meetings and hospitality to visitors, as the house rules set out. Lab fees and costs are reported separately from member fees.

23.3 **House rules.** The Board adopts house rules for the lab and events, covering safety, tools, storage and shared space.

23.4 **Code of conduct and care concept.** The Board adopts, and the General Assembly approves, a code of conduct and a care concept: how the company keeps people safe and welcome, how it protects minors at its events, and how concerns are raised and handled, with a person of trust outside the Board.

23.5 **Courses.** Courses and workshops for young people follow the care concept, and the law on working with minors.

## Article 24 – Accounts, Auditors and Reports

24.1 **Accounts.** The accounts follow Greek Accounting Standards (Law 4308/2014) [and the rules for non-profit legal entities: to confirm]. They show the general account, each Prozent Account and the Pension Assets separately, and within the Pension Assets the Continuity Account.

24.2 **Auditors.** The General Assembly elects, for [two] years:

- two member Auditors, who are not Board members, Connected Persons or Group Personnel; and
- an independent certified auditor (ορκωτός ελεγκτής), where the law requires one [to confirm, including whether holding the Pension Assets does], and in any case once nilware.io IKE has left its Simple Stage (Article 24A).

24.3 **Duties.** The Auditors check the accounts, the separation of the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Assets, compliance with the yearly plans, Articles 21 and 22 and the register of interests, and the published reasons for nilware.io votes. They may inspect all books and records at any time. They report in writing to each Ordinary General Assembly, before the vote on discharge.

24.4 **Annual report.** The Board publishes, within [six] months of the year end, an annual report with the accounts, the yearly report of each Prozent Account (Article 11.7), the Auditors' report, the register of interests and a summary of nilware.io votes.

## Article 24A – Scaled Paperwork

24A.1 **Combined plan and report.** While the company's yearly income, the Prozent Accounts included and the Pension Assets excluded, is below €[●], the yearly plans (Article 11.6) and yearly reports (Article 11.7) of the Prozent Accounts may be made as one combined plan and one combined report. Each Prozent Account is still shown as a separate section and a separate sub-account, and the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account (Article 13.8) have their own section. Each section shows everything Articles 11.6, 11.7 and 13.8 require.

24A.2 **Simple Stage.** While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Article 9A), the company's paperwork scales in the same way:

- **One ledger.** It keeps one set of books, with a sub-account for each Prozent Account, the Pension Assets and the Continuity Account. The money stays in the separate accounts these statutes require (Articles 11.1, 13.2 and 13.8).
- **Member Auditors only.** The member Auditors under Article 24.2 do the audit, without a certified auditor unless the law requires one.
- **One annual report.** The annual report under Article 24.4 may be the combined report under Article 24A.1.

24A.3 **What does not scale.** Every substantive rule applies in full: every amount, ring-fence and separate account, permanence (Article 14), independence (Article 15 and Article 19.3A), conflicts of interest (Article 21) and publication (Article 6). A combined plan or report publishes everything the separate ones would.

## Article 25 – Liability

25.1 The company's assets, apart from the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Assets as far as the law allows, answer for its debts. Each Prozent Account answers only for debts incurred for its own Prozent.

25.2 Members are liable for the company's debts only as far as mandatory law requires [Civil Code Arts. 741 ff.: to confirm]. The company insures its Board against liability [as far as is reasonable].

25.3 Board members are liable for harm they cause by breach of their duties, under Greek law. Discharge by the General Assembly does not cover facts that were not disclosed to it.

## Article 26 – Amendments

26.1 These statutes are amended by the General Assembly under Article 18.7, and the amendment is registered as the law requires.

26.2 **Entrenched Articles.** Articles 5, 11.3 and 11.4, 12 (as it sets the purpose of each Prozent), 13, 14, 15, 16.4, 19.3A, 21.3 and 21.6, 22.3A, 24A.3, 27.2 and 27.3, and this Article 26.2, may be amended in any way that weakens a Prozent, its ring-fence, the pension ring-fence or the company's independence only by the unanimous decision in Article 14.2, in which a member with an interest under Article 18.8 or Article 21 takes no part and is not counted, and, for Article 13 and Article 27.2, only with the consent of the Pensionskasse board and of nilware.io IKE. Article 14.2 does not apply to how the pension shares vote or consent, which Article 14.4 governs. Support for a named person or entity is not a purpose of a Prozent for this Article (Article 12.2). An amendment that IKE Articles 10A.4A or 10A.9B require may not be weakened at all while the company holds the shares those Articles cover.

## Article 27 – Dissolution and Liquidation

27.1 **Dissolution.** The company is dissolved by a General Assembly resolution under Article 18.7, or as the law provides.

27.2 **Pension Assets.** On dissolution, the Pension Assets other than the Continuity Account go to the TEA under IKE Article 10A.10, or, if none exists, to a pension fund or a successor non-profit with the same lock that nilware.io IKE designates under IKE Article 10A.9B(d). The Continuity Account never goes to the TEA or a pension fund: on dissolution it goes to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, designated by nilware.io IKE under IKE Article 10A.9B(f) [and approved by the Pensionskasse board], and the successor holds it under IKE Article 10A.9F. None of the Pension Assets ever go to the company's other purposes, its members or the nilware.io group.

27.3 **Prozent Shares and Prozent Accounts.** On dissolution, or if the company ceases to meet IKE Article 6.4A, each Prozent Share and the unspent balance of its Prozent Account go to a successor non-profit with the same purpose and lock, designated under IKE Article 10A.4A [and approved by an independent person or the Pensionskasse board: to confirm]. The General Assembly may propose a successor.

27.4 **Other assets.** After the debts are paid, any remaining assets go to one or more Greek or EU non-profit bodies with a purpose close to the company's [and, where needed, exempt from tax], chosen by the General Assembly. Unlike SGMK Art. 9, they may never go to members, organ members, Connected Persons or the nilware.io group.

27.5 **Liquidators.** The Board acts as liquidator unless the General Assembly appoints others. Liquidators report to the members and publish a final report.

## Article 28 – Disputes

28.1 Disputes between members, or between members and the company, go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019.

28.2 If mediation fails, the competent courts of [Athens] decide, or an arbitrator if the parties agree.

## Article 29 – Final Provisions

29.1 If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force, and the invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

29.2 Matters not covered here are governed by Civil Code Arts. 741 ff. and Greek law in general.

29.3 The Greek text is filed and prevails. The English text is a translation.

## Article 30 – Founding and Transitional Provisions

30.1 **Founding members.** The founding members, and their contributions, are: [●], including [●]. [The Founding Person: owner to decide whether the Founding Person is a founding member, within Article 15.]

30.2 **First Board.** The first Administrative Board serves until the first Ordinary General Assembly, and in any case no more than [two] years: President, [●]; Vice-President [●]; Treasurer [●]; Secretary [●]; members [●]. Article 19.3 applies from the start, and Article 19.3A from the Independence Threshold. Every first Board member enters their ties in the register of interests (Article 21.1) on taking office.

30.3 **First Auditors.** [●].

30.4 **Before receiving nilware.io shares.** Before the company receives any share or payment from nilware.io IKE, it:

- signs the Pensionsprozent Agreement (IKE Article 10A.9B) and adopts these statutes, including Articles 13, 14 and 27, and registers them [as the law requires];
- signs the deed of adherence under IKE Article 5.3 and Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D of the Shareholders' Agreement;
- opens the Prozent Accounts and the Pension Asset accounts, including the Continuity Account;
- adopts a first yearly plan for each Prozent Account;
- obtains the accountant's written confirmation of the tax treatment [donation tax under Law 2961/2001].

30.5 **Shares received.** On incorporation of nilware.io IKE, the company receives [six] shares, one for each of the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent, the Pensionsprozent share and the Kassenprozent pool share (IKE Article 4.2, Initial holdings).

---

*Signatures of the founding members: [●]*


<!-- articles/08-founder-pool-constitution.md -->

# Constitution of the Founder Pool – nilware.io (Draft)

*Constitution of the founder pool of nilware.io: the holders of the founder-class shares (Class F) and the Reserve Shares (Class R) of nilware.io IKE, and of the Class A shares of nilware.io Holdings AE. An agreement among those holders under Greek law [legal form: to confirm, Article 16]. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

> **Modelled on the Swiss Federal Constitution.** This constitution borrows the structure of the Swiss Federal Constitution of 18 April 1999 (BV), not its text, and none of Swiss public law applies [article numbers below to confirm against the current BV text]. Adopted in adapted form: the holders as the sovereign, meeting together like a Landsgemeinde (a cantonal assembly of all voters, not a BV institution); basic rights before organs (BV Title 2); a collegial executive of up to seven members deciding as one body, whose president serves for one year and is not re-elected for the year after (BV Arts. 174 to 177); the optional referendum on decisions of the authorities (BV Art. 141), the mandatory referendum (BV Art. 140) and the initiative (BV Arts. 138 and 139); the double majority of the people and the cantons (BV Art. 142); sections with their own rules within the constitution, as cantons have their own constitutions (BV Arts. 3 and 51); revision and transitional provisions (BV Arts. 192 to 197). Added where a state constitution has nothing to say: one block vote at the company, commitments that travel with the shares, independence from the Prozente and the Pensionskasse, the brand held for the pool, free transfer, succession on death, arbitration among holders. Greek law prevails throughout.

## Article 1 – The Founder Pool

1.1 **The Founder Pool.** The Founder Pool is formed by all holders of founder-pool shares, whoever they are and however many. It is "the Founder" in the nilware.io documents: the Articles of nilware.io IKE (the "IKE Articles"), the Articles of nilware.io Holdings AE (the "AE Articles"), the Shareholders' Agreement and the Agent Policy (IKE Article 4.2E). The Founder is the Founder Pool, acting through the Founder Representative (Article 8), not any person.

1.2 **Founder-pool shares.** The founder-pool shares are:

- the founder-class shares (Class F) of nilware.io IKE: ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of its capital, initially 50 shares (IKE Article 4.2B);
- the Reserve Shares (Class R) of nilware.io IKE, until nilware.io Holdings AE buys them at the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D);
- the Class A shares of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 4.2), from its formation;
- any share issued to keep the Founder Pool at exactly 50% (IKE Article 4.2B).

1.3 **Holders.** A "Holder" is any person, natural or legal, registered as holder of founder-pool shares who has adhered under Article 4.1. Each Holder's weight in this constitution is the number of Class F shares it holds ("Class F votes"). Reserve Shares and Class A shares go with the Class F shares (Article 9.2) and carry no separate weight. Class F shares whose votes are suspended or lost under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement carry no weight in the Founder Pool, and do not count for Article 1.6 or the quorum in Article 6.6, while the suspension lasts; nor do shares without weight under Articles 4.5 and 9.3A.

1.4 **The Founding Person.** The "Founding Person" is [name withheld], the natural person who founded nilware.io and the first Holder of every founder-pool share. The obligations that are personal stay with the Founding Person and never pass with founder-pool shares: the IP assignment, full-time work, the non-compete, the leaver terms (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.1 to 6.4) and authorship of the charter. The Founding Person holds the Brand Assets of record for the Founder Pool (Article 2A.3), not as owner. In the Founder Pool the Founding Person has the rights of a Holder, and no others.

1.5 **Precedence.** This constitution governs the Holders among themselves. Towards nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the Prozente and the Investors, the IKE Articles, the AE Articles and the Shareholders' Agreement prevail, and nothing in this constitution weakens any duty in them. Mandatory Greek law prevails over all of them.

1.6 **Counting heads.** For every count of Holders or of the Holders in a Section (Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11 and 12), persons acting in concert, a Holder and those it controls (IKE Article 4.2C) or who are its nominees, and spouses or registered partners count as one Holder. A Holder of fewer than [5]% of the Class F votes (and never fewer than two whole Class F shares), or who acquired its holding in the previous [6] months other than as heir or legatee, does not count as a head. Persons who received founder-pool shares without payment from the same Holder in the previous [3] years, other than on death, count as one head together with that Holder. Persons who hold a share in common count as one Holder (Article 9.6). These rules decide only who counts as a head; every Holder still votes its Class F votes.

## Article 2 – Purpose

2.1 **Purpose.** The Founder Pool exists to hold the founder-pool shares together, so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% of nilware.io IKE and speaks with one voice, and to carry out the Founder's role in the nilware.io documents:

- casting the votes of the Class F shares at nilware.io IKE and of the Class A shares at nilware.io Holdings AE, as one block (Article 3);
- nominating the majority of the Board of nilware.io Holdings AE that the Class A shares nominate (AE Article 8.2);
- proposing candidates for manager of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 8.2A);
- standing at the end of every chain of supervision of the company's Agents (IKE Article 11A.2A; Article 8.3), except the chains this constitution keeps independent of the Founder (Article 8.4);
- owning the Brand Assets and licensing them to nilware.io IKE (Article 2A);
- giving the Founder's consents and receiving its information under the nilware.io documents.

2.2 **The charter.** The Founder Pool keeps faith with the charter of nilware.io: the six founding Prozente and their permanence, the agentic company, openness, and the independence of the AMKE and the Pensionskasse from the Founder.

2.3 **No business of its own.** The Founder Pool runs no business and holds no assets other than the founder-pool shares its Holders bring to it and the Brand Assets (Article 2A). Each Holder receives the dividends and other distributions on its own shares directly. The Holders share the costs of the Founder Pool in proportion to their Class F votes.

## Article 2A – The Brand

2A.1 **Brand Assets.** The Founder Pool owns the "Brand Assets": the word mark "NILWARE.IO", the figurative mark "∅ nilware.io", any other mark, logo or name containing "nilware.io", or the ∅ sign as used with it, that the Founder files, adopts or acquires, or that nilware.io IKE or anyone else holds for the Founder Pool under Brand Licence Clause 2.1A, every application and registration for them, the domain name nilware.io and the other domains listed in the Brand Licence Agreement, and the goodwill attached to them (Brand Licence Clause 2.1).

2A.2 **Assets of the pool.** The Brand Assets belong to the Founder Pool as a whole, not to any Holder. No Holder has a separate share in them that it can sell, pledge, inherit or claim, and they are never distributed to Holders. A Holder's interest in them is its membership of the Founder Pool, through its Class F votes.

2A.3 **Holder of record.** Until the Founder Pool has a legal vehicle, the Founding Person is the registered owner of the Brand Assets and holds them for the Founder Pool, on the Founder Council's instructions [as fiduciary or mandatary without representation: to confirm, counsel note I-42]. When a vehicle exists under Article 16.2, or another vehicle a mandatory referendum approves (Article 10.2), the Brand Assets are transferred to it. They are also transferred, to a holder of record the Founder Council designates (a vehicle, or a natural person eligible under Article 8.2), when the Founding Person dies or becomes incapable (Article 14.4), ceases to be a Holder, or the Council so decides. If no Founder Council can act, including while the constitution is dormant and its sole Holder has died or become incapable, the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, or failing one the arbitrator under Article 15 on the request of any person listed in Article 14.3(d), designates an interim holder of record and uses the power of attorney below, notwithstanding the restrictions in Article 14.3 and Article 10.6; the interim holder of record serves until the Founder Council designates a holder of record. The holder of record signs, on taking office, an irrevocable power of attorney to the Founder Representative for the time being to make these transfers [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm]. Neither the holder of record nor its heirs or creditors acquire any interest of their own in the Brand Assets [as far as Greek law allows, counsel note I-42].

2A.4 **Inseparable.** The Brand Assets travel with the Founder Pool, whoever its Holders become. They are never separated from the founder-class shares (Class F), and never sold or transferred apart from the Founder Pool. A transfer of founder-pool shares, in whole or in part, transfers no Brand Asset; whoever controls the block controls the Brand Assets through the organs of the Founder Pool.

2A.5 **The Founder Council decides.** The Founder Council decides on filing, renewing, maintaining, opposing and enforcing registrations of the Brand Assets, on the domains, on consents and brand guidelines under the Brand Licence Agreement, and on giving notice of a Prozent Breach and referring it to the arbitrator under Brand Licence Clauses 7.2 and 7.3, subject to the optional referendum (Article 10.1). It may not rely on a Prozent Breach that a Founder Party caused, voted for or procured (Brand Licence Clause 7.1).

2A.6 **Referendum.** A Brand Asset is assigned only in ways that keep it with the Founder Pool: to a new holder of record or vehicle under Article 2A.3, or with the whole Founder Pool on a change of form under Article 16.3. Assigning a Brand Asset to a new vehicle or other holder of record, other than a designation under Article 2A.3 on the events listed there, abandoning a Brand Asset (including letting a registration or a domain lapse on purpose), and amending the Brand Licence Agreement, are decided only by a mandatory referendum (Article 10.2), and always subject to the Brand Licence Agreement. No Brand Asset is encumbered or given as security (Brand Licence Clause 8.1) [owner may confirm]. Any other assignment, and any encumbrance, is void between the Holders and towards nilware.io IKE [as far as the law allows, counsel note I-42].

2A.7 **The Brand Licence.** The Founder Pool has granted nilware.io IKE an exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence of the Brand Assets, which ends only under its Clause 7 (Brand Licence Agreement). Every Holder is bound by it, and the Founder Pool honours it. No organ of the Founder Pool may end or suspend it at its discretion.

2A.8 **Costs.** Costs of the Brand Assets that nilware.io IKE does not pay under the Brand Licence Agreement are costs of the Founder Pool (Article 2.3).

## Article 3 – One Block

3.1 **One block.** All Class F shares vote at nilware.io IKE as one block, and all Class A shares vote at nilware.io Holdings AE as one block. Every vote of the block is cast the same way, by the Founder Representative, as the Founder Pool has decided under this constitution. A majority inside the Founder Pool decides for all of it.

3.2 **Proxy.** Each Holder grants the Founder Representative for the time being an irrevocable proxy, given also in the interest of the other Holders [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm], to vote its Class F and Class A shares and to sign the written resolutions and consents of the block. No Holder votes its founder-pool shares itself. Each Holder signs a new proxy whenever the Founder Representative changes.

3.3 **Who decides.** The Founder Council decides how the block votes (Article 7), except on the matters this constitution reserves to the Pool Assembly or to a referendum (Articles 6 and 10).

3.4 **Exceptions.** Reserve Shares carry no votes, whoever holds them, until nilware.io Holdings AE holds them after the Qualifying Closing (IKE Article 4.2D). The votes of shares whose rights are suspended or lost under Clauses 6.1 and 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement are cast as those Clauses provide. There is no other exception.

3.5 **A vote cast otherwise.** A vote cast for founder-pool shares other than by the Founder Representative is not counted by the company [as far as Greek law allows] (IKE Article 4.2E). A Founder Representative who casts the block other than as decided is answerable to the Holders under Article 15, and the vote stands at the company.

## Article 4 – Commitments that Travel with the Shares

4.1 **Adherence.** No one becomes a Holder until they have adhered in writing to this constitution, to the Shareholders' Agreement by deed of adherence (Clause 12.3), including Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D (and Clause 5.1E with them), to the Brand Licence Agreement, and to the IKE Articles and the AE Articles as they bind the Founder. The managers of nilware.io IKE do not register a transfer to a person who has not adhered (IKE Article 5.7). A Holder who disposes of all its founder-pool shares ceases to be a Holder, but stays bound by Article 15 for disputes about its time as Holder, and stays a Founder Party for the lookback in Article 4.4. A Holder that is a legal person tells the Founder Council, and the managers of nilware.io IKE, of any change of its control (IKE Article 4.2C) within [14] days (IKE Article 5.7). Before any change of its control closes, it procures, and undertakes in its deed of adherence to procure, that its controllers and the acquirer seek the ballot under Article 9.3 where that Article applies, and make the offer to the Investors under Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement where that Clause applies.

4.2 **Permanence.** Every Holder is bound by every rule that makes the Prozente permanent (IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C, AE Article 7.6, Shareholders' Agreement Clause 5.1C). The block votes against, and refuses its written consent to, every matter in Clause 5.1C of the Shareholders' Agreement, unless a mandatory referendum has approved it with the consent of every Holder (Article 10.2).

4.3 **Exactly 50%.** No Holder transfers, acquires or subscribes shares in a way that takes the Class F shares away from exactly 50% of the capital of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 4.2B). The block may vote for a capital increase of nilware.io IKE only after Holders have committed irrevocably in writing to subscribe all the new Class F shares it needs. Each Holder may subscribe in proportion to its Class F votes, and the others may take up what a Holder does not; no Holder is obliged to subscribe. Otherwise the block votes against the increase, unless it is structured under IKE Article 4.2B so that the Founder stays at exactly 50% without new Class F shares. No Holder, and no one who controls a Holder or whom a Holder controls (IKE Article 4.2C), holds capital shares of nilware.io IKE other than founder-pool shares, or acquires any that would take the votes of the IKE shares held by Holders and by persons they control above 50% of the total capital [owner may confirm]. This excludes the AE's holding, whose votes are governed by the AE Articles (Reserved Matters, AE Article 7.4) and disclosed under IKE Article 4.2B: the AE is not counted as a person the Holders control for this Article, and the Qualifying Closing does not breach it.

4.4 **Independence.** Every Holder, everyone who controls a Holder, the Founder Representative, every member of the Founder Council and of the Council of Sections, and the Founding Person is a "Founder Party" (IKE Article 4.2E). Every rule of the nilware.io documents that keeps a body, a vote or a person independent of the Founder applies to each of them: in particular the independence of the AMKE and its Prozent votes (IKE Article 6.4A, AMKE Article 15), of the Pensionskasse, its board, the pension shares and the Kassenprozent pool (IKE Articles 10A.8 and 10A.9D), and of the independent proxy holder (Shareholders' Agreement, Clause 5.1B). A person who was a Founder Party at any time in the previous [3] years is treated as one for every such rule, and stays a Connected Person and in the Founder's Circle (AMKE Article 2.2; IKE Article 4.2E, Founder Parties).

4.5 **Who may not be a Holder.** nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, Techni kai Technologia AMKE, the Pensionskasse, any holder of a Prozent share, any pension fund under IKE Article 10A.10, and anyone any of them controls, may not hold founder-pool shares. A Holder that becomes barred, including by a change of its control, has no weight in the Founder Pool from that moment (Article 1.3), and sells its founder-pool shares to a person who may hold them within [6] months; failing that, the other Holders may buy them, in proportion to their Class F votes, at fair value set by a valuer who is not a Founder Party.

## Article 5 – Basic Rights of Holders

5.1 **Equal treatment.** Every Class F share carries the same rights in the Founder Pool. No decision of the Founder Pool may favour or disadvantage a Holder, or a group of Holders, except in proportion to their Class F votes.

5.2 **Information.** Every Holder receives, within [30] days, everything the Founder Representative receives from nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE as shareholder (IKE Articles 7.1 and 8.4), the minutes of the Founder Council, and how the block voted on each matter and why. Every Holder keeps company secrets as IKE Article 7.2 requires.

5.3 **Voice.** Every Holder may attend and speak at the Pool Assembly, put motions to it, and stand for election to the Founder Council.

5.4 **The right to sell.** Every Holder may sell, give or leave its founder-pool shares, in whole or in part, to anyone who may be a Holder, under Article 9. The internal procedures of this constitution may delay a sale under Article 9.3, but never stop it.

5.5 **Minority protection.** Without the consent of each Holder affected, no decision of the Founder Pool or of a Section may: change the weight of a share; impose a payment or duty on a Holder beyond this constitution; restrict a Holder's right to sell beyond Article 9; or treat Holders differently. A Holder who did not agree may challenge any decision that breaches this constitution under Article 15, within [60] days of learning of it.

5.6 **No forced exit.** No Holder can be excluded from the Founder Pool or made to sell or give up its founder-pool shares, except:

- under the drag-along of nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE (IKE Article 5.5, AE Article 6.3);
- the sale of the Reserve Shares to nilware.io Holdings AE at the Qualifying Closing, which every holder of Reserve Shares makes (IKE Article 4.2D);
- the transfer of Reserve Shares to a new Prozent (IKE Article 4.2D, New Prozente);
- the leaver terms of the Founding Person, as they reach shares received from the Founding Person without payment (Shareholders' Agreement, Clauses 6.1 to 6.2A);
- the sale of the shares of a Holder that has become barred (Article 4.5);
- the purchase of shares acquired in breach of Article 9.3 (Article 9.3A); and
- seizure, enforcement or insolvency under the law.

## Article 6 – Pool Assembly

6.1 **Highest body.** The Pool Assembly is all the Holders. Like a Landsgemeinde, it is the highest body of the Founder Pool, and every Holder may take part in person or by proxy.

6.2 **Powers.** The Pool Assembly:
