- elects and removes the members of the Founder Council, and each year elects its President and Vice-President from among them (Articles 7 and 8);
- decides the matters of the mandatory referendum (Article 10.2), by ballot of all Holders;
- decides on initiatives and optional referendums (Articles 10.1 and 10.4);
- recognises Sections and approves their rules (Article 12);
- receives the yearly report of the Founder Council and discharges it;
- amends this constitution (Article 11).

6.3 **Ordinary Assembly.** The Pool Assembly meets at least once a year, at least [15] days before the ordinary shareholders' meeting of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 6.1).

6.4 **Extraordinary Assembly.** The Founder Council calls an extraordinary Assembly when it decides, or within [20] days of a written request by Holders of [10]% of the Class F votes or by [3] Holders (counted under Article 1.6). If the Council does not, the requesting Holders may call it themselves.

6.5 **Notice and meetings.** At least [15] days' written notice, by email, with the agenda and the documents. The Assembly may meet in person, online or both, and may decide by written or electronic ballot. It decides only on items on the agenda.

6.6 **Votes.** Each Class F share has one vote. Unless this constitution says otherwise, the Assembly decides by a majority of the Class F votes cast, and a tie is a rejection (on the block's votes, Article 10.6). The Assembly is quorate when Holders of at least half of the Class F votes take part; a repeat Assembly at least [7] days later is quorate whatever the attendance. A ballot under Article 10 has no quorum.

6.7 **Proxies.** A Holder may give a written proxy to another Holder or to a lawyer. No one holds proxies for more than [one third] of the Class F votes.

6.8 **Minutes.** Minutes are kept of every Assembly and sent to every Holder.

## Article 7 – Founder Council

7.1 **Collegial executive.** The Founder Council is the executive of the Founder Pool. It has 1 to 7 members, as the Pool Assembly decides, elected by the Assembly for [four] years and re-eligible. If the Assembly has not decided the number of seats, the Council has [three] members, or as many as there are Holders if fewer. Its members are natural persons; they need not be Holders [owner may confirm]. A person barred under Article 8.2 may not be a member of the Founder Council. Where there are Sections, the Assembly takes care that they are fairly represented. In an election, the candidates with the most Class F votes are elected; a tie between candidates for the last seat, or for President, is decided by lot.

7.2 **Deciding as a body.** The Founder Council decides as one body, by a majority of its members voting. No member decides alone for the Council. A member who disagrees may have the disagreement minuted, but represents the Council's decision outside it. On a tie, the President's vote decides [owner may confirm].

7.3 **Powers.** The Founder Council decides everything not reserved to the Pool Assembly or to a referendum, in particular:

- how the block votes at nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE, other than on the matters in Article 10.2;
- the Class A nominations to the Board of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 8.2), after consulting the Council of Sections (Article 12.4);
- the candidates the Founder proposes for manager of nilware.io IKE (IKE Article 8.2A);
- the policy within which the Founder Representative oversees the chains of supervision of Agents (Article 8.3);
- the Brand Assets, within Article 2A.6 (Article 2A.5);
- preparing the Pool Assembly and the ballots, and carrying out their decisions.

7.4 **Timing.** The Founder Council decides how the block votes on a matter within [3] days of the Founder Representative receiving the notice of the meeting or the proposed written resolution, and publishes the decision to the Holders at once. With the IKE's 20 days' notice (IKE Article 6.2), that leaves time for a demand within [5] days and a ballot of [7] days that ends before the meeting (Articles 10.1 and 10.5). On a matter in Article 10.2, the Council puts its proposal to the ballot on the same timetable.

7.5 **Duties.** Members of the Founder Council act with care and in good faith, in the interest of the Founder Pool as a whole and of the long-term health of nilware.io, within the charter (Article 2.2). They disclose any conflict of interest and abstain on it, as IKE Article 8.3 requires of managers. They report to the Pool Assembly every year.

7.6 **Removal and vacancies.** The Pool Assembly may remove a Council member at any time. A vacant seat is filled at the next Assembly; until then the Council continues with the remaining members.

7.7 **Failed election.** If no Founder Council, or no President, has been elected within [90] days of the date on which the Assembly had to elect one (Articles 6.2, 13.3 and 14.3), the arbitrator under Article 15, on the request of any Holder, appoints an interim Founder Council of one or more members, or an interim Founder Representative, eligible under Article 8.2, who serve until the Assembly elects. Until the arbitrator appoints, the Founder Representative in office, or the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, continues.

## Article 8 – Founder Representative

8.1 **President and Representative.** The President of the Founder Council is the "Founder Representative". The President is elected by the Pool Assembly for one year from among the Council members, and may not be President for the following year, unless the Council has only one member or no other member is willing [owner may confirm]. The Vice-President stands in whenever the President is absent or excluded by a conflict, and, as the Swiss practice is, is the natural candidate for President the next year.

8.2 **A natural person.** The Founder Representative is always a natural person, of full legal capacity, who is not barred under Article 4.5 and is not a bad leaver under Clause 6.2 of the Shareholders' Agreement [for [●] years after the bad-leaver date: owner to decide].

8.3 **Role.** The Founder Representative:

- casts every vote of the block, and signs its written resolutions and consents, as the Founder Pool has decided (Article 3);
- represents the Founder towards nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the AMKE, the Investors and third parties;
- is the end of every chain of supervision of the company's Agents (IKE Article 11A.2A; Agent Policy, section 1.3). Within the policy the Founder Council sets, the Founder Representative oversees how the managers and the responsible managers supervise Agents, receives the Agent register and the incident records, and may ask the managers to pause or stop any Agent or to report on any Agent's work. The Founder Representative oversees as the Founder's representative, not as a manager, unless also appointed manager; the managers remain responsible for supervision under IKE Article 8.3.

8.4 **What stays independent of the Founder.** The oversight in Article 8.3 never reaches the Kassenprozent Agents' work on the Kassenprozent pool, the Pensionskasse, its board and the pension shares, the Continuity Account, or the AMKE and its Prozent votes. Those chains end with the Pensionskasse board or the AMKE's own organs (IKE Articles 6.4A, 10A.8, 10A.9D and 10A.9F), and no Founder Party takes part in them (Article 4.4).

8.5 **Notice to the company.** The Founder Council notifies nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE in writing of the Founder Representative and of every change, with the new proxies under Article 3.2. If the Council cannot act, the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, or any Holder, notifies the company, with the death certificate, the court decision or the certificates under Article 14.4. The company relies on the last notice it has received.

## Article 9 – Transfers

9.1 **Free transfer.** Founder-pool shares are freely transferable: they may be sold, given or inherited, in whole or in part, and pledged or held in usufruct. No consent of nilware.io IKE, of nilware.io Holdings AE or of the other Holders is needed (IKE Article 5.7). The conditions in Articles 9.2 to 9.5 apply.

9.2 **Conditions.** The acquirer adheres under Article 4.1 and is not barred under Article 4.5. A Holder who transfers part of its holding transfers Class F, Class R and Class A shares as nearly in proportion as it holds them as whole shares allow, rounding [to the nearest whole share], so that a transfer of any number of Class F shares can be made [owner may confirm, or allow the classes to be transferred separately; a Holder with no Class F shares is then not counted as a Holder for Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11 and 12]. The proportion is worked out on everything the Holder has transferred to the same acquirer, and to persons acting in concert with it, in the previous [12] months, so that splitting a transfer into smaller ones never leaves Class R or Class A shares behind. [A finer division of the capital: counsel notes I-10 and I-40 (k).] The transferring Holder tells the Founder Council within [14] days.

9.3 **Large sales.** A transfer by any title other than inheritance or legacy (a "sale" in this Article, its acquirer the "buyer") that would give one buyer, alone or with persons acting in concert with it, more than [●]% of the Class F shares, counting what it acquired in the previous [12] months, needs a mandatory referendum (Article 10.2). Acquiring control (IKE Article 4.2C) of a Holder counts as acquiring its founder-pool shares, and shares held by persons a buyer controls count as the buyer's. The seller, the buyer, and persons acting in concert with or controlled by either, take no part in the ballot, and their Class F votes are not counted among the votes cast. If the referendum rejects it, the other Holders may buy the shares offered, at the same price and on the same terms, or at fair value where no price per share is paid, in proportion to their Class F votes, within [60] days. Shares they do not take may then be sold to the buyer as offered. If no Holder may vote in the ballot, Article 9.3 does not apply.

9.3A **Breach of Article 9.3.** Founder-pool shares acquired in breach of Article 9.3, directly or by acquiring control of a Holder, carry no weight in the Founder Pool (no Class F votes in the Assembly, ballots or elections, and no head under Article 1.6) until a ballot under Article 9.3 approves the acquisition. If the ballot rejects it, the other Holders may buy those shares from the buyer or the Holder concerned, in proportion to their Class F votes, at the price paid, or at fair value set by a valuer who is not a Founder Party where no price per share was paid, within [60] days.

9.4 **Change of control.** A buyer who acquires a majority of the Class F shares controls the block. Acquiring control of Holders counts as acquiring their founder-pool shares, as in Article 9.3. The Investors' tag-along on such a sale is in Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement and IKE Article 5.4A [owner may confirm].

9.5 **Pledges, usufruct and enforcement.** A pledgee, usufructuary or assignee of dividends adheres under Article 4.1 for the rights it receives, and the votes always stay with the block. A buyer in enforcement or insolvency takes the shares subject to this constitution and becomes a Holder on adherence.

9.6 **Shares and parts of shares.** Founder-pool shares are whole capital shares. Persons who hold one share in common, including heirs of an undivided estate, count as one Holder for Articles 6.4, 10.1, 11, 12 and 13 until the share is divided (Article 1.6), and act through one common representative [as Law 4072/2012 requires: to confirm]. While an undivided estate is the only Holder, its common representative, if eligible under Article 8.2, is the Founder Representative (Article 13.1). [Whether the capital should be divided into more shares, so that the Founder Pool can be split finely: counsel notes I-10 and I-40 (k).]

9.7 **Register.** The Founder Council keeps a register of Holders and of their Class F, Class R and Class A shares, matching the share registers of nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE.

## Article 10 – Referendum and Initiative

10.1 **Optional referendum.** Holders of [5]% of the Class F votes, or [3] Holders (counted under Article 1.6), may demand that any decision of the Founder Council be put to a ballot of all Holders. The demand suspends the decision. It is made:

- for a decision on how the block votes at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, within [5] days of its publication (Article 7.4); the ballot then runs for [7] days and ends before the meeting or the deadline for the written resolution;
- for any other decision, within [30] days of its publication.

If a ballot on a block vote still cannot end before the vote must be cast, the Founder Representative asks for the matter to be adjourned where the company's rules allow. Otherwise the block does not vote as the Council decided: it abstains, and votes against any matter that changes the IKE Articles or the AE Articles [owner may confirm]. If the ballot ends in time and rejects the Council's decision on a block vote (Article 10.3), the block likewise abstains, and votes against any matter that changes the IKE Articles or the AE Articles. On a Required Matter the block always votes under Article 10.7, whatever the ballot. On a matter in Article 10.2, Article 10.6 applies.

10.2 **Mandatory referendum.** The following are decided by a ballot of all Holders, and only after the Founder Council has consulted the Council of Sections (Article 12.4):

- amending this constitution (Article 11);
- the block's vote on any matter of nilware.io IKE that needs the Qualified Majority (IKE Article 6.5) or two-thirds of the capital, including a new Prozent (IKE Article 6.6), and the block's vote on the Reserved Matters of nilware.io Holdings AE (AE Article 7.4);
- the block's consent to any matter in Clause 5.1C of the Shareholders' Agreement, which needs the consent of every Holder (Article 4.2);
- electing the Founder Council (Article 7.1);
- a sale under Article 9.3;
- assigning a Brand Asset to a new vehicle or other holder of record (other than a designation under Article 2A.3 on the events listed there), abandoning a Brand Asset, and amending the Brand Licence Agreement (Articles 2A.3 and 2A.6);
- changing the legal form of the Founder Pool, or ending it (Article 16).

10.3 **Majority.** A ballot is decided by a majority of the Class F votes cast, except where Articles 4.2 and 11 require more, and except elections, which follow Article 7.1. A tie is a rejection. In an optional referendum on a decision of the Founder Council on how the block votes (Article 10.1), the decision stands unless a majority of the Class F votes cast is against it, so a tie leaves it in force [owner may confirm].

10.4 **Initiative.** Holders of [10]% of the Class F votes may propose in writing an amendment of this constitution, or a decision on how the block votes or what it proposes at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, within the nilware.io documents. The Founder Council puts the initiative to a ballot within [90] days, and may put a counter-proposal beside it. An initiative may not propose anything the IKE Articles, the AE Articles or the Shareholders' Agreement forbid.

10.5 **Ballots.** Ballots are held in writing or electronically, over at least [10] days, or over [7] days for a ballot on how the block votes on a matter at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, which ends before the meeting or the deadline for the written resolution (Article 7.4). Each ballot comes with the Founder Council's explanation and, where they exist, the opinions of the Council of Sections and of the initiators. The result is published to every Holder.

10.6 **When no ballot approves.** Where a matter in Article 10.2 on which the block is to vote or consent has not been approved by ballot, because the ballot rejected it, tied, could not be held or end in time, or the matter arises in an interim period under Article 14.3, the block votes against it and refuses its written consent. Where the law requires the shareholders to pass a resolution on the matter (the Required Matters, IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback), the block votes under Article 10.7 instead. Any other matter in Article 10.2 is not decided until a ballot approves it.

10.7 **Required Matters.** On a Required Matter (IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback) on which the block would otherwise abstain under Article 10.1, 10.6 or 14.3, in or after the Interim Period, the Founder Representative, or the interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3, casts the block under the neutral rule in that Article, whatever the ballot, so that the company can always pass them.

## Article 11 – Amendments and Double Majority

11.1 **Double majority.** This constitution is amended only by a mandatory referendum passed by both:

- a majority of the Class F votes cast; and
- a majority of the Holders voting (counted under Article 1.6), or, while there are Sections, a majority of all the Sections, each Section counted for the side that has the majority of its own Class F votes cast in the ballot. A Section whose ballot ties, or whose Holders cast no vote, counts against the amendment.

11.2 **Entrenched articles.** Articles 1.4, 1.5, 2A.2, 2A.4, 2A.7, 3, 4, 5, 8.2, 8.4, this Article 11 and Article 13.2 may be amended only by [two-thirds] of the Class F votes cast together with a majority of the Holders voting and of every Section.

11.3 **Limits.** No amendment may weaken a commitment that the IKE Articles, the AE Articles or the Shareholders' Agreement place on the Founder or on any Holder. Such an amendment is void between the Holders.

## Article 12 – Sections

12.1 **Sections.** Holders may form Sections, as cantons are formed within the Swiss Confederation: in particular a family branch (a Holder who inherited or received shares, and their own heirs and donees), or a group of Holders who bought together. A Section needs at least [2] Holders (counted under Article 1.6) and at least [10]% of the Class F votes. A group that meets these criteria has the right to be recognised: the Pool Assembly records it, and if it refuses, the group may appeal to the arbitrator under Article 15. Each Holder belongs to at most one Section. Holders who belong to no Section together form the Open Section, which counts as one Section for Article 11 only if it meets the same minimum size [owner may confirm]. For every ballot, a Holder belongs to the Section it belonged to [6] months before the ballot opened, or to the Open Section if it belonged to none then.

12.2 **Own rules.** Each Section adopts its own rules for its internal affairs, within this constitution, and the Pool Assembly approves them if they comply with it; a refusal may be appealed to the arbitrator under Article 15. A Section's rules may not restrict a Holder's right to sell beyond Article 9, impose a forced exit, or bind a Holder's ballot.

12.3 **Double majority.** Sections count for the double majority under Article 11.1 by the result of the ballot among their own Holders, not by any internal procedure.

12.4 **Council of Sections.** Each Section names one delegate to the Council of Sections. The Founder Council consults it, in writing or at a meeting, before every matter in Article 10.2 and every Class A nomination to the Board of nilware.io Holdings AE. Its opinion is advisory, and is minuted and sent to every Holder with the ballot.

12.5 **When Sections exist.** Sections and the Council of Sections exist only while the Founder Pool has at least [3] Holders.

## Article 13 – Transitional Rule: One Holder

13.1 **Dormant.** While all founder-pool shares are held by one Holder, this constitution is dormant: that Holder is the Pool Assembly and the Founder Council, and, if a natural person eligible under Article 8.2, the Founder Representative. A Holder that is a legal person names a natural person eligible under Article 8.2 as Founder Representative. If a natural-person Holder is not eligible, or a legal-person Holder has no eligible person named, Article 14.3 applies. The sole Holder signs the standing proxy in Article 14.7. No Assembly, ballot, Section or Council of Sections is held. While dormant, the sole Holder's written decision stands in for every ballot, the Assembly and the Council, including under Articles 4.2 and 10.2; Article 9.3 does not apply to a transfer by the sole Holder, and Clause 9.1A of the Shareholders' Agreement does. An undivided estate counts as one Holder for this Article 13 (Article 9.6); its common representative, if eligible under Article 8.2, is the Founder Representative. At incorporation the Founding Person is the one Holder.

13.2 **What applies while dormant.** Articles 1, 2, 2A, 3.1, 3.4, 4, 7.7, 8.2 to 8.5, 9, 14, 15 and 17 apply in full while the constitution is dormant: one block, the Brand Assets and the Brand Licence, the commitments that travel with the shares, independence, exactly 50%, adherence, a natural person as Founder Representative, succession, failed elections, disputes and the final provisions.

13.3 **Switching on.** The constitution switches on when a transfer, gift or inheritance gives founder-pool shares to a second Holder and is registered. The Founder Representative then in office continues as interim Founder Representative and sole member of the Founder Council, and calls the first Pool Assembly within [60] days to elect the Council. If no Council is elected within [90] days of the switch, Article 7.7 applies.

13.4 **Back to one Holder.** If all founder-pool shares come again to one Holder, the constitution becomes dormant again.

## Article 14 – Succession on Death or Incapacity

14.1 **Shares pass by will or law.** On a Holder's death, its founder-pool shares pass by will or by law. Each heir or legatee becomes a Holder on adherence under Article 4.1. Until the estate is divided, the heirs count as one Holder and act through one common representative (Article 9.6).

14.2 **Letter of wishes.** Each Holder who is a natural person may lodge with the Founder Council, or while dormant with [a notary], a letter of wishes naming the person it wishes to succeed it as Founder Representative. The letter does not bind the Pool Assembly, but the interim rule in Article 14.3 follows it. A letter written by a person who is not eligible under Article 8.2 when the interim rule applies is disregarded, and so is the naming of anyone who is a Close Associate of that person (AMKE Article 2.2).

14.3 **Interim Founder Representative.** If the Founder Representative dies, becomes incapable, resigns, ceases to be eligible under Article 8.2 or is removed, or a sole Holder that is a legal person no longer has an eligible natural person named, and the Assembly has not yet elected a successor:

Where the Founder Council still has a member able to act, the Vice-President, or failing one a member the Council chooses, acts as President and Founder Representative with full powers, and the Assembly elects a new President within [90] days; the list and the restrictions below do not apply. Where no Founder Council member remains able to act, or while the constitution is dormant, the interim Founder Representative is, in this order, the first of these who is willing and eligible under Article 8.2:

- (a) the Vice-President of the Founder Council;
- (b) the person named in the letter of wishes of the Founder Representative who has gone, unless it is disregarded under Article 14.2;
- (c) the natural person who holds, or represents the estate or Holder that holds, the most Class F votes; if two or more hold the same number, the one chosen by lot by the managers of nilware.io IKE in the presence of a notary, or if none of them is willing, (d);
- (d) a natural person appointed by the arbitrator under Article 15 on the request of any Holder, heir, legatee, executor or administrator of an estate that holds founder-pool shares, of the managers of nilware.io IKE, or of Techni kai Technologia AMKE, each of whom may ask for the appointment, and for the arbitrator under Article 15.2, for this purpose;
- (e) until someone is appointed under (d), the independent proxy holder in Clause 5.1B of the Shareholders' Agreement, who acts as interim Founder Representative for the Required Matters only (IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback), under the neutral rule in that Article; on every other matter the block abstains. The independent proxy holder acting under this (e) under the neutral rule is not a Founder Party by doing so (IKE Article 4.2E, Founder Parties).

The interim Founder Representative serves until the Pool Assembly elects a President (and, where none remains, a Founder Council), which it does within [90] days of the event, or within [90] days of the heirs' adherence while an estate is undivided; if it does not, Article 7.7 applies. While the constitution is dormant, the interim ends when a single Holder has adhered, or an undivided estate has named an eligible common representative (Article 13.1), who becomes Founder Representative under Article 13.1 if eligible under Article 8.2. If no heir has adhered within [12] months of the death, the arbitrator under Article 15, on any request under (d), appoints an interim Founder Council with the powers of Article 7.3, which serves until heirs holding a majority of the Class F votes have adhered. In the meantime the interim Founder Representative under the list above votes the block only on matters that cannot wait, such as the Required Matters (IKE Article 4.2D, Fallback), and in line with the last decisions of the Founder Council [owner may confirm]. No matter in Article 10.2 is decided in the meantime: the block votes on it as Article 10.6 provides, against, or under the neutral rule on a Required Matter (Article 10.7).

14.4 **Incapacity.** The incapacity of a Holder, of the Founder Representative or of a member of the Founder Council is established by a court decision placing the person under judicial support [Civil Code Arts. 1666 ff.: to confirm] or by [two] certificates of doctors who are not Founder Parties and have no tie to one. The person concerned may challenge a finding by certificates before the arbitrator under Article 15, who may order interim measures (Article 15.4). A Holder's legal representative exercises its rights as Holder, but is Founder Representative only if elected.

14.5 **Forced heirship.** Greek law reserves a share of every estate to the closest relatives (νόμιμη μοίρα) [Civil Code Arts. 1825 ff.: to confirm]. Nothing in this constitution or in a letter of wishes overrides it. A forced heir who receives founder-pool shares becomes a Holder on adherence like any other heir [whether the reserved share can be satisfied in founder-pool shares or in money, and how the commitments bind a forced heir: to counsel, note I-40].

14.6 **The Founding Person.** On the death or incapacity of the Founding Person, the personal obligations in Article 1.4 end as the Shareholders' Agreement provides; the founder-pool shares pass under this Article, the Brand Assets pass to a new holder of record under Article 2A.3 and not to the Founding Person's heirs, designated, if no Founder Council can act, by the interim Founder Representative or the arbitrator under that Article; nilware.io IKE may keep them in force meanwhile (Brand Licence Clause 5.1A); and the Founder Pool continues.

14.7 **Standing proxy.** While the constitution is dormant, the sole Holder signs a standing proxy to whoever is interim Founder Representative under Article 14.3 from time to time, to vote its Class F and Class A shares as Article 14.3 allows. The proxy is irrevocable, is given also in the interest of nilware.io IKE and of the Prozente, and survives the Holder's death and incapacity and binds its heirs until a single Holder has adhered or the Assembly elects a Founder Council [Civil Code Art. 218 and the Civil Code on the end of a mandate: to confirm, note I-40 (a)]. A sole Holder that is a legal person signs the same proxy, which also covers the loss of its named natural person.

## Article 15 – Disputes

15.1 **Mediation, then an arbitrator.** Disputes among Holders, or between a Holder and an organ of the Founder Pool, about this constitution, the block's votes or the Holders' rights go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, and then to a sole arbitrator under Code of Civil Procedure Arts. 867 ff. [to confirm], seated in [Athens]. They never go first to the courts, and nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE are not parties to them.

15.2 **The arbitrator.** The parties agree on the arbitrator. If they do not within [30] days, [the Chair of the Athens Bar Association] appoints one. The arbitrator is not a Founder Party and has no tie to one.

15.3 **Effect.** An award binds the Holders and the organs of the Founder Pool. It does not change a vote already cast at nilware.io IKE or nilware.io Holdings AE, but may direct how the block votes from then on.

15.4 **Interim measures.** The arbitrator, or the court where the law requires, may order interim measures [as far as Greek law allows].

## Article 16 – Legal Form

16.1 **Agreement among Holders.** The Founder Pool is a pooling and voting agreement among the Holders, each of whom owns its founder-pool shares directly and grants the proxy in Article 3.2. It has no legal personality of its own [to confirm, note I-40].

16.2 **Alternative.** If counsel advises it, the Holders may instead bring their founder-pool shares into a civil partnership (αστική εταιρεία) [Civil Code Arts. 741 ff.: to confirm] that holds them, whose partners are the Holders. This constitution would then be its statute, the Founder Representative its administrator, the partnership the holder of record of the Brand Assets (Article 2A.3), and a transfer of founder-pool shares a transfer of a partnership share. A change of form is a matter for the mandatory referendum (Article 10.2), and may not weaken anything in Article 4.

16.3 **The brand on a change of form or an end.** A change of form, or an end of the Founder Pool, keeps the Brand Assets together, with the Brand Licence Agreement, in the hands of the holders of the founder-class shares as a whole or of their vehicle. They are never divided among Holders [to confirm, counsel note I-42].

## Article 17 – Final Provisions

17.1 **Publication.** This constitution, and each amendment, is sent to nilware.io IKE and nilware.io Holdings AE and disclosed to the Investors (Shareholders' Agreement, Schedule 1).

17.2 **Invalidity.** If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force, and the invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

17.3 **Governing law.** This constitution is governed by Greek law. Matters not covered here are governed by the Civil Code and Greek law in general.

---

*Signatures of the Holders: [●]*


<!-- agreements/09-brand-licence.md -->

# Brand Licence Agreement – the Founder to nilware.io IKE (Draft)

*Between the holders of the founder pool (together the "Founder", IKE Article 4.2E), acting through the Founder Representative, and the Founding Person, [name withheld], as holder of record of the Brand Assets for the Founder (Pool Constitution Article 2A.3) (together the "Licensor"), and nilware.io IKE (the "Company"). Governed by Greek law, including Law 4679/2020 on trade marks [to confirm], and, for the registrations outside Greece, Regulation (EU) 2017/1001 on the EU trade mark and the Swiss Trade Mark Protection Act (MSchG) as they apply. Draft for review by a Greek lawyer; not legal advice.*

## 1 – Purpose and Precedence

1.1 **Purpose.** The nilware.io name, marks and domain belong to the Founder, the founder pool, and stay with it whoever holds its shares (Pool Constitution Article 2A). The Company carries the name. This Agreement lets the Company use them as its own name and brand, exclusively and for good, for as long as it honours the Prozente.

1.2 **Precedence.** This Agreement binds the Founder and every holder of founder-pool shares towards the Company. Among the holders, the Pool Constitution governs how the Founder decides under this Agreement. The Shareholders' Agreement protects this Agreement (SHA Clause 5.1E). Mandatory Greek law prevails over all of them.

## 2 – The Brand Assets

2.1 **Brand Assets.** The "Brand Assets" are:

- the word mark "NILWARE.IO" and the figurative mark "∅ nilware.io" (the empty set sign, a space, then "nilware.io" in lowercase), in every territory, with every application and registration for them;
- any other mark, logo or name containing "nilware.io", or the ∅ sign as used with it, that the Founder files, adopts or acquires, with every application and registration for them;
- the domain name nilware.io, and the other domains listed in Schedule 1;
- the goodwill attached to them.

2.1A **Marks the Company creates.** The Company, its group companies and every sublicensee do not file, register or acquire in their own name any mark, logo, name or domain containing "nilware.io", or the ∅ sign as used with it. Any such mark, logo, name or domain that the Company, its Agents, a group company or a sublicensee creates, adopts or obtains is held for the founder pool, is a Brand Asset, falls under the Licence, and is assigned to the holder of record on request, at the Company's cost and without payment [as far as the law allows].

2.2 **Owned by the Founder.** The Brand Assets are assets of the founder pool (Pool Constitution Article 2A). Until the founder pool has a legal vehicle (Pool Constitution Article 16; counsel note I-40 (d)), the Founding Person holds them of record for the founder pool, and afterwards the vehicle or another holder of record under Pool Constitution Article 2A.3. Nothing in this Agreement, and no use by the Company, a group company or a Prozent, transfers any ownership in them to the Company. Goodwill arising from the Company's use accrues to the Brand Assets [as far as the law allows].

2.3 **Successors of record.** Any later holder of record of a Brand Asset, including a vehicle under Pool Constitution Article 2A.3 and the holders or vehicle under Pool Constitution Article 16.3, takes it subject to this Agreement and adheres to it in writing before the transfer is made. The Founder procures this. A transfer made without that adherence does not affect the Company's rights [as far as the law allows; recording under Clause 5.4].

## 3 – The Licence

3.1 **Grant.** The Licensor grants the Company an exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence to use the Brand Assets as its company name, distinctive title (διακριτικός τίτλος), brand, trade marks and domain, for every product, service and activity of the Company, now or in future (the "Licence").

3.2 **Exclusive.** The Licence is exclusive also as against the Founder. Neither the Licensor nor any holder of founder-pool shares uses the Brand Assets in trade or licenses them to anyone else, except:

- the founder pool and its organs may call themselves the founder pool of nilware.io;
- the Founding Person may say that the Founding Person founded nilware.io, and the charter (charter/) may use the name;
- licences the Company grants or approves under Clause 3.3.

3.3 **Sublicences.** The Company may sublicense the Brand Assets, without further consent and without royalty:

- to nilware.io Holdings AE and every company the Company or the AE controls (IKE Article 4.2C) (together "group companies"), for their names, distinctive titles and business. A sublicence to a company ends [6] months after it stops being a group company;
- to Techni kai Technologia AMKE, the Pensionskasse and every holder of a Prozent share, to describe themselves, their Prozent and their link to the Company (for example "the Kulturprozent of nilware.io"), but not as their own name or trade mark;
- to customers, suppliers and partners, for use incidental to their dealings with the Company.

Any other sublicence needs the consent of the Founder Council, not to be unreasonably withheld. Every sublicence ends when the Licence ends, at the end of the transition period under Clause 7.4.

3.4 **Royalty-free.** The Company pays no royalty. In return it bears the costs in Clause 5.2 and gives the undertakings in this Agreement. [Owner option: a royalty to the founder pool, as a percentage of revenue or a fixed yearly fee, in place of none; see counsel note I-42 (f) on related-party pricing.]

3.5 **No assignment.** The Company may not assign the Licence, except together with the whole, or substantially the whole, of its business, to a successor or acquirer in which the Prozente continue on the same terms (IKE Article 10A.5B), and which adheres to this Agreement. That applies also to a sale of the business in liquidation or insolvency [as far as mandatory law allows: counsel note I-42]. A change of control of the Company or of the AE is not an assignment.

## 4 – The Domain

4.1 **Registrant and control.** The holder of record stays the registrant of nilware.io and the other domains in Schedule 1. Within [14] days of signature the Licensor gives the Company full technical and administrative control of them: the registrar account, DNS, hosting, email, certificates and renewals. The Licensor does not change, redirect, transfer or let lapse any of them, except under Clause 7.4.

4.2 **Registry rules.** Where a registry's rules require the registrant to act, the holder of record acts promptly as the Company reasonably asks [.io registry terms: to confirm, counsel note I-42].

## 5 – Registration, Maintenance and Enforcement

5.1 **Filing and renewal.** The Founder Council decides on filing, renewing, extending, opposing and defending registrations of the Brand Assets (Pool Constitution Article 2A.5), after consulting the Company. The holder of record files in its own name for the founder pool. The Company may ask for a filing in a further territory or class, and the Founder Council does not refuse unreasonably. The Founder keeps every registration and domain in force; letting one lapse, or abandoning it, is an abandonment under Pool Constitution Article 2A.6 and a change under Clause 9.

5.1A **Step-in.** If a registration or a domain would lapse, because the holder of record cannot act, has died, is incapable or does not act within [30] days of the Company's written request, or [30] days before the deadline, whichever is earlier, the Company may renew and maintain it in the holder of record's name, at its own cost. The Licensor consents to this now and gives the Company the authority needed for it [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm, counsel note I-42 (c)]. Step-in gives the Company no ownership in the Brand Assets (Clause 2.2).

5.2 **Costs.** The Company pays the reasonable costs of filing, renewing and maintaining the Brand Assets, of the domains, and of enforcement under Clause 5.3 [owner may confirm].

5.3 **Enforcement.** The Founder Council decides on proceedings against infringers, after consulting the Company. If the Founder does not bring proceedings within [30] days of the Company's written request, the Company, as exclusive licensee, may bring them in its own name, and the Licensor consents to this now and joins as party where the law requires. Whoever bears the costs keeps the recoveries, and damages for the Company's own losses go to the Company.

5.4 **Recording.** The Licence is recorded, as an exclusive licence, with the European Union Intellectual Property Office (EUIPO), the Swiss Federal Institute of Intellectual Property (IPI) and any other office where a Brand Asset is registered. The Licensor signs whatever is needed, and the Company pays the fees.

5.5 **Use and quality.** The Company uses the Brand Assets in a way that keeps them valid and distinctive, and follows the brand guidelines in Schedule 2, which the Founder Council may update after consulting the Company. The guidelines cover the form of the marks only. They give the Founder no say over the Company's business.

5.6 **No challenge.** The Company does not challenge the validity of the Brand Assets or the Founder's ownership of them [as far as competition law allows].

## 6 – Term

6.1 **Perpetual.** The Licence has no end date. It ends only under Clause 7.

6.2 **What does not end it.** The Licence does not end, and cannot be ended or suspended, on:

- a change of control of the Company or of the AE, an investment round, a sale of shares, a merger, split or conversion;
- the Company's dissolution, liquidation or insolvency [subject to mandatory insolvency law: counsel note I-42], or a sale of its business under Clause 3.5;
- a transfer of founder-pool shares, a change of the Founder Representative or of the holder of record, or the death or incapacity of the Founding Person;
- any decision of the Founder, at its discretion;
- any breach of this Agreement other than a Prozent Breach under Clause 7.1. Other breaches give a right to specific performance and damages only.

## 7 – Ending

7.1 **The only ground.** The Licence ends only if the Company stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules (IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C) (a "Prozent Breach"), and Clauses 7.2 and 7.3 have been followed. A Prozent Breach includes:

- not paying a Prozent's contribution under IKE Article 10A, or not paying it into the right account;
- removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose, or amending a protected Article (IKE Article 10A.5C), otherwise than by a decision taken under IKE Article 10A.5;
- a voluntary dissolution of the Company that mandatory law does not require, otherwise than under IKE Article 12.1;
- a resolution or act that suspends or overrides any of IKE Articles 10A.5 to 10A.5C.

It does not include a decision taken in accordance with IKE Article 10A.5, or an act that mandatory insolvency, dissolution or liquidation law requires (IKE Article 10A.5B) [owner may confirm].

The Founder cannot rely on a Prozent Breach that a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E) caused, voted for or procured, or that a manager of the Company caused who is or was proposed or nominated by a Founder Party (IKE Article 8.2A). Such a breach does not end the Licence; it gives only a right to specific performance, to the Prozente, the AMKE, the Investors and the Company.

7.2 **Notice and cure.** The Founder, on a decision of the Founder Council subject to the optional referendum (Pool Constitution Articles 2A.5 and 10.1), gives the Company written notice of the Prozent Breach, with a copy to the AMKE, the Investors and the independent proxy holder (SHA Clause 5.1B). The Company has [90] days from the notice to cure it: by paying every amount due to the Prozente, with interest at the legal rate, and by reversing or not carrying out the resolution or act concerned. The AMKE, any Investor or the independent proxy holder may cure on the Company's behalf, by paying the amount due or by any other act that cures, and recovers the cost from the Company. A cure by any of them is a cure by the Company.

7.3 **Established by the arbitrator.** If the Prozent Breach is not cured within the [90] days, the Founder, on a decision of the Founder Council subject to the optional referendum (Pool Constitution Articles 2A.5 and 10.1), may refer it to the arbitrator under Clause 10. The Licence ends only when the arbitrator's award establishes that a Prozent Breach occurred and was not cured within the cure period (the "Ending Date").

7.4 **Transition.** For [12] months from the Ending Date the Company may keep using the Brand Assets while it rebrands. Within that period the Company:

- changes its registered name and distinctive title at the General Commercial Registry (GEMI) so that they no longer contain "nilware.io" (IKE Article 1.1A), and has its group companies do the same;
- hands control of the domains back to the holder of record, which forwards email and web traffic to the Company's new domain for the rest of the transition period;
- stops using the Brand Assets at its end, and withdraws the recordings under Clause 5.4.

Every sublicence ends at the end of the transition period. After it, the Company may still state truthfully that it was formerly called "nilware.io IKE".

7.5 **Nothing else.** The Founder has no other right to end, suspend or restrict the Licence.

## 8 – The Founder's Undertakings

8.1 **Keeping the assets.** The Founder does not assign or abandon any Brand Asset except under Pool Constitution Article 2A.6, and then only subject to this Agreement (Clause 2.3). An assignment is made only to a holder of record under Pool Constitution Article 2A.3, or with the whole founder pool under Pool Constitution Article 16.3. No Brand Asset is encumbered or given as security [owner may confirm]. Any other assignment, and any encumbrance, is void towards the Company [as far as the law allows; recording under Clause 5.4].

8.2 **Inseparable from the pool.** The Brand Assets go only with the founder pool and are never separated from the founder-class shares (Class F) or sold apart from the pool (Pool Constitution Article 2A.4).

## 9 – Changes

9.1 **Amendment.** This Agreement is amended or waived only in writing by the Founder, after a mandatory referendum of the founder pool (Pool Constitution Article 10.2), and by the Company.

9.2 **Protected.** A change or waiver that harms the Company, in particular one that narrows the Licence, adds a royalty or a ground for ending it, shortens the cure period or the transition period, or releases an undertaking of the Founder, is a Reserved Matter (SHA Clause 5.1), and in the Interim Period needs the consent in SHA Clause 2.4A (Consents).

## 10 – Disputes

10.1 **Mediation, then an arbitrator.** Disputes under this Agreement go first to mediation under Law 4640/2019, and then to a sole arbitrator under Code of Civil Procedure Arts. 867 ff. [to confirm], seated in [Athens].

10.2 **The arbitrator.** The parties agree on the arbitrator. If they do not within [30] days, [the Chair of the Athens Bar Association] appoints one. The arbitrator is not a Founder Party (IKE Article 4.2E), a manager of the Company or anyone they control (IKE Article 4.2C), and has no tie to any of them. In a dispute under Clause 7, the Company is represented, agrees on the arbitrator and conducts its case through its managers who are not Founder Parties and were not proposed or nominated by one (IKE Article 8.2A), or, failing them, through the independent proxy holder (SHA Clause 5.1B) or a nominee of the Investor Majority (SHA Clause 3.4). The AMKE may join the proceedings as a party.

10.3 **Interim measures.** The arbitrator, or the court where the law requires, may order interim measures, including to keep the Brand Assets and the domains in force while a dispute lasts [as far as Greek law allows].

## 11 – General

11.1 **Governing law.** This Agreement is governed by Greek law.

11.2 **Successors.** This Agreement binds every holder of founder-pool shares, who adheres to it with the Pool Constitution (Pool Constitution Article 4.1), every holder of record of a Brand Asset (Clause 2.3), and the Company's successors under Clause 3.5.

11.3 **Invalidity.** If any provision is invalid, the rest remains in force, and the invalid provision is replaced by the valid one that comes closest to its purpose.

---

*Schedule 1: Brand Assets: marks, applications and registrations, and domains [●]. Schedule 2: Brand guidelines [●].*

Subject: ∅ nilware.io – draft founding documents for review

Hi all,

Attached is the first draft of the founding documents for the nilware.io group. In short: nilware.io IKE is the operating company, which I will hold at exactly 50%. Six Prozente hold 1% each through Techni kai Technologia AMKE (Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung, Allmend, Pension, and the Kassenprozent pool share held for the pension fund). nilware.io Holdings AE will hold the remaining 44% and is the vehicle for raising investment. Until the first investment round, I own the 44% that will go to the AE as voteless Reserve Shares, as compensation for my time; the AE buys them from me in that round (a secondary sale). My votes stay equal to 50% of the capital: before the round that is 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, so I cannot pass anything alone. Both are governed by Greek law, with Swiss standards (GmbH governance, Swiss labour protections) adopted voluntarily wherever Greek law allows. nilware.io is an agentic company: work is done by Agents, whether people or systems, wherever possible.

Contents:

00 – Preface: The Charter (a short poem)
00b – Visual (a poem on the nilware.io timeline, 2001–2026)
00c – Fifty, One, One, and the Rest (a structural poem on the ownership)
00d – One Share, One Percent (a poem on the Prozente)
01 – Articles of Association, nilware.io IKE
02 – Articles of Association, nilware.io Holdings AE
03 – Shareholders' Agreement
04 – IP Assignment Agreement, Founding Person to nilware.io IKE
05 – Agent Policy
06 – Trama trademark instruction (for reference; sent to Trama separately)
07 – Statutes, Techni kai Technologia AMKE (holds the Prozente)
08 – Constitution of the Founder Pool
09 – Brand Licence Agreement, the founder pool to nilware.io IKE
Notes for counsel – open legal questions, by document

What I need from each of you:

Legal / lawyers
- Check that every clause works under Laws 4072/2012 and 4548/2018, Greek labour law and GDPR.
- Check that the Swiss-modelled standards are enforceable as voluntary commitments.
- Check whether the IKE and AE Qualified Majority provisions and the Class B veto rights fit together.
- Review contractor reclassification risk under Article 11A.
- Review the Swiss-law advisor requirement in liquidation (IKE Article 12).
- Review the Reserve Shares before the AE (IKE Article 4.2D, Shareholders' Agreement Clauses 2.4A, 3.2 and 6.2A): a class of IKE shares without votes, the sale to the AE in the first round, and its tax.
- Review the Prozente: creation by two-thirds of the AE (IKE Article 6.6, AE Article 7.5), permanence and entrenchment (IKE Article 10A.5 to 10A.5C, AE Article 7.6), the pool share held for the pension fund (IKE Article 10A.8), the AMKE ring-fence and the path to a TEA (IKE Articles 10A.9B, 10A.10), the Swiss pension commitments (IKE Article 10A.9A), and the AMKE statutes (07). Open questions are collected in the notes for counsel.
- Review the founder pool (IKE Articles 4.2E and 5.7; the Constitution of the Founder Pool, 08; notes I-40 and I-41) [owner to confirm].
- Review the brand held by the founder pool and licensed to the IKE (IKE Article 1.1; the Brand Licence Agreement, 09; Constitution of the Founder Pool, Article 2A; note I-42).

Accountants
- Assess the tax treatment of dividends from the IKE to the AE, and from the AE to investors.
- Check the Agent Account setup (IKE Articles 11A.3 to 11A.8): whether it should be a separate bank account or a restricted reserve.
- Advise on the capital amounts and the valuation of the IP contribution (IP Assignment, Clause 4).

Team
- Read the Agent Policy and send me comments, especially on well-being, own-goal projects and open publishing.

Everything in square brackets [●] is still to be decided. These are drafts, not legal advice, and nothing should be signed or filed until legal and accounting have signed off.

Please send comments by [date].

Thanks,
[name withheld]
∅ nilware.io


<!-- NOTES_FOR_COUNSEL.md -->

# Notes for counsel

The open legal questions behind the drafts, by document. Each note gives the question, why it matters, and what the draft assumes for now. Bracketed text in the drafts marks a point to confirm; values to decide are listed in [OPEN_ITEMS.md](OPEN_ITEMS.md).

The owner has decided the following. Please advise on how to implement them, not on whether to adopt them:

- Six founding Prozente, created in the initial holdings rather than by the two-thirds route: Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs-, Allmend-, Kassen- and Pensionsprozent. The Kassenprozent's 1% of revenue stays inside the IKE. Its 1% share pool belongs to the Pensionskasse. The Pensionsprozent's 1% is a floor. The Erhaltungsprozent (preservation of software and hardware knowledge) and the Allmendprozent (open-source infrastructure, teaching low-level skills in Greek, accessible open software) are held by the AMKE like the Kultur- and Konzeptprozent, each with its own ring-fenced account.
- Each Prozent's clause lists its named purposes. The holder divides the contribution among them in a yearly plan; there are no fixed slices.
- The Founder (the founder pool, I-40) holds founder-class shares (Class F), ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of the IKE's capital at all times (initially 50 shares), with votes equal to 50% of the total capital. Before the AE buys the Reserve Shares, those are 50 of the 56 votes in existence, but every majority is counted on the total capital and also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, so the Founder cannot pass anything alone; after the sale the Founder, as Class A holder nominating the AE Board majority, directs the AE's 44 votes on every matter that is not a Reserved Matter. Holdings: Founder 50; AMKE 1 Kultur, 1 Konzept, 1 Erhaltung, 1 Allmend, 1 Pension, plus the pool share it holds for the Pensionskasse; AE 44 (until the Qualifying Closing, the Founder's 44 Reserve Shares, without votes, I-35). The outflow Prozente (Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung, Allmend, Pension) take 5% of revenue, exactly the [5]% outflow cap, and all Prozente with the Kassenprozent take 6%, exactly the [6]% cap. Any further Prozent with a revenue contribution therefore needs the 75% route (IKE Art. 6.6 Limits, AE Art. 7.5).
- The company is always called "nilware.io": nilware.io IKE, nilware.io Holdings AE, the nilware.io group.
- Creating a new Prozent needs two-thirds of the votes represented at the AE General Meeting, all classes together. This replaces the IKE Qualified Majority for that decision. The IKE meeting still passes the amendment, and every IKE shareholder is bound by a voting undertaking.
- Prozente are permanent. Removing one, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose needs every IKE shareholder, and the AE may agree only with a unanimous AE General Meeting.
- For the Pensionskasse, Swiss occupational-pension law is guaranteed wherever it gives a better standard of care, and Greek law applies wherever it gives more.
- Any Agent may supervise another, person or system (decision of 2026-11-23): "dropping 'human' is the right way to go: senior Agents may onboard and supervise junior Agents." Every role the drafts gave to a "human manager" (preparing the Agent Card, onboarding, the monthly review, stopping an Agent, answering concerns, approving actions above the limits, overseeing the Kassenfonds and the pool) now belongs to the Agent's named responsible manager, who may be any person or system with the authority its Agent Card grants, within its own limits, never for itself and never in a cycle, with every chain ending with the Founder, through the Founder Representative, and with the company's managers, who stay legally responsible (IKE Art. 11A.2A, decision of 2026-11-23 below). A natural person decides or oversees only where the law requires one, and the drafts say so as the law's requirement (I-38).
- The Pensionsprozent also covers the well-being and continuance of Agents that are systems, as is fair (decision of 2026-11-23). For a system, after-care means continuity, not a pension: a ring-fenced Continuity Account inside the Pensionskasse pays for preservation, continuity of service when a model or provider changes, a published retirement record, and well-being reviews and research (IKE Art. 10A.9F). People come first: the guaranteed minimums for persons are met first and never reduced; the Pensionskasse board sets the continuity share of the 1% between a floor and a cap, in proportion to the work system Agents do; the total contribution and the outflow caps do not change. The account stays with the AMKE and never moves to the TEA.

Decisions of 2026-11-23 on the Founder (I-40):

- **"At the end of the way it's gonna be the Founder's job to oversee everything."** Every chain of supervision of Agents ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative, and with the company's managers, who remain legally responsible (IKE Arts. 8.3, 11A.2A; Agent Policy 1.3). The chain for the Pensionskasse and the Kassenprozent pool stays independent of the Founder (IKE Arts. 10A.8, 10A.9D).
- **"The Founder" is the pool of founder-class shares, not a person:** the 50 founder-class shares (Class F), the Reserve Shares (Class R) and the AE's Class A shares, whoever holds them (IKE Art. 4.2E). The natural person who founded nilware.io is the "Founding Person", whose personal obligations stay personal: the IP assignment, full-time work, the non-compete, the leaver terms and authorship of the charter.
- **Founder-pool shares are freely transferable**, sold, given or inherited, in whole or in part, "with internal democratic procedures similar to the Swiss referenda and multi-level hierarchies and councils and the structural parts of the constitution" (the Pool Constitution, articles/08). Every new holder adheres to the Pool Constitution, the Shareholders' Agreement and every permanence rule; the commitments travel with the shares (IKE Art. 5.7).
- **A split pool votes as one block**, as a majority inside decides, through one Founder Representative, a natural person (IKE Art. 4.2E, Pool Constitution Art. 3).
- **"The founder controls the trademark and the domain name."** The marks "NILWARE.IO" and "∅ nilware.io", any future nilware.io marks and logos, and the domain nilware.io belong to the Founder, the founder pool, as pool assets: held of record by the Founding Person for the pool now, later by the pool's legal vehicle (I-40 (d)); they travel with the pool and are never separated from the Class F shares or sold apart from the pool. The Founder Council decides on filing, renewal, enforcement and licences; assigning them (only to a new holder of record or vehicle of the pool) or abandoning them needs a mandatory referendum of the pool, and the draft allows no encumbrance at all [owner may confirm] (Pool Constitution Art. 2A). The pool grants nilware.io IKE an exclusive, perpetual, royalty-free, worldwide licence to use them as its name, distinctive title, brand and domain, with sublicences to group companies and to the Prozente and the AMKE to describe themselves; it ends only if the IKE stops honouring the Prozente or the permanence rules (IKE Arts. 10A.5 to 10A.5C), as the arbitrator establishes after written notice and a [90]-day cure period, followed by a [12]-month transition to a new name; not on a change of control, liquidation or insolvency, and not at the Founder's discretion (IKE Arts. 1.1, 1.1A; SHA 2.3A, 5.1E; agreements/09). The marks and the domain are no longer assigned to the IKE (IP Clause 2.2). See I-42.

Decisions of 2026-11-23, for a company that brings in about €200K a year, starts with a €50K budget and has no employees yet (the Founder and Agents):

- **The AE is deferred until the first investment round**, because it needs €25,000 of share capital (AE Art. 1.2A). Until then its 44 shares are a separate class, the Reserve Shares (Class R), owned by the Founder outright from incorporation as compensation for the time invested before investors (sweat equity); the Founder pays their subscription price (IKE Art. 4.2D, I-35). They carry no votes but count in the total capital, and every majority is counted on the total capital: the Founder holds 50 of the 56 votes in existence, votes equal to 50% of the capital, and because every interim resolution also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes cast, the Founder cannot pass anything alone; where no independent vote is cast on a matter the law requires, the independent proxy holder votes them under a neutral rule; nothing the AE's rules would forbid can pass. Their dividend and liquidation rights belong to the Founder, whose economic interest is 94% until the AE buys them at the round price, only at the first closing of a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe at least €[●] of Class B shares (the Qualifying Closing), and only out of that round's proceeds (a secondary sale, proceeds to the Founder, disclosed plainly in SHA Schedule 1); in the AE's hands they become ordinary voting shares and every AE, Class B and investor rule applies. The Founder holds ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of the capital, and votes equal to 50% of the capital, never more.
- **AMKE Board of 3 to 7** (was 5 to 9), and one combined yearly report while the AMKE's income is below €[●], each Prozent still a separate section and sub-account (AMKE Arts. 19.1, 24A.1).
- **Pension phased in with headcount** (IKE Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9D, 10A.9F, 10A.10): from the first employee or Agent who is a person, an insured group plan with a licensed insurer that meets the guaranteed minimums; a TEA only at scale (about [50] members, or by joining a multi-employer TEA). Until the first member, the Pensionsprozent's 1% accumulates in the Pensionskasse, and the continuity share for system Agents may reach the cap in proportion to the work they do, close to all of it with no persons, within the people-first rules, which then have nothing to secure.
- **Simple until €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people** (IKE Art. 9A): one ledger with sub-accounts per Prozent, one combined report, member auditors only (no certified auditor unless the law requires), and the AMKE and Pensionskasse paperwork scaled the same way (AMKE Art. 24A). Every substantive rule still applies; only the paperwork scales.
- **"As it grows, I will let it fly alone."** Once the AMKE's yearly income passes €[●], a majority of its Administrative Board must be outside the Founder's Circle: not Connected Persons, not Group Personnel and not Close Associates of the Founder (AMKE Arts. 2.2, 19.3A), and the Kultur- and Erhaltungsprozent selection committees must have an outside majority (AMKE Art. 22.3A). Close personal and professional ties are disclosed in the register of interests from the start (AMKE Art. 21.1).

## articles/01-nilware-ike.md

**I-1. Majority for amending an IKE's articles (Art. 6.6).**
- *Question:* What majority does Law 4072/2012 require for amending the articles, issuing or transferring shares and admitting a partner, and in which article? Can the articles raise it, and can they make putting a resolution to the meeting depend on an earlier approval by another company's meeting?
- *Why it matters:* Art. 6.6 no longer treats the IKE vote as a formality. The IKE meeting passes the amendment, and the AE approval is only a precondition for tabling it. If even that precondition is heteronomous, the safeguard reduces to the voting undertaking in SHA 5.1B.
- *Draft assumes:* two-thirds of the total capital under Art. 72 (to confirm). After the Qualifying Closing the Founder and the AE together hold 94%, so the resolution passes once both vote under the undertaking; before it, see I-35 (b).

**I-2. Unanimity and entrenchment (Arts. 10A.5 to 10A.5C).**
- *Question:* Can IKE articles require the written consent of every shareholder for removing a Prozent, lowering its contribution or changing its purpose? Can they entrench that rule, so that it can only be changed unanimously? Would a later two-thirds amendment deleting the clause be void, or only a breach of the SHA?
- *Why it matters:* Permanence is the owner's decision. If the articles cannot entrench it, the protection rests on SHA 5.1C and 5.1D.
- *Draft assumes:* the articles may raise a majority up to unanimity, and the entrenchment holds. Mandatory insolvency, dissolution and liquidation law prevails (Art. 10A.5B).

**I-3. Successors (Art. 10A.5B).**
- *Question:* Can the articles require a merger, conversion or sale of the business to carry the Prozente over to the successor or acquirer? On a voluntary dissolution by Qualified Majority (Art. 12.1), do the Prozente simply end?
- *Why it matters:* Without this, a dissolution or restructuring could get around permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* the resolution approving the transaction must carry the Prozente over, as far as the law allows. A voluntary dissolution, other than one mandatory law requires, is a voluntary act that would end every Prozent (and could be followed by refounding without them), so Art. 12.1 now requires a unanimous decision under Art. 10A.5 for it, as far as Law 4072/2012 allows; AE Art. 7.6 and SHA 5.1C mirror this. Can the articles validly raise the majority for a voluntary dissolution to unanimity? Mandatory dissolution and insolvency law prevails, and accrued pension rights survive as company liabilities.

**I-4. Suspending Prozent votes (Art. 6.4A).**
- *Question:* Can IKE articles suspend the vote of a share while its holder is controlled by, or governed with, the Founder or a manager, with the share still counting in the total capital? Can they direct that the pension shares vote as the Pensionskasse board decides, rather than as the AMKE's own organs decide?
- *Why it matters:* With 6%, the AMKE is the swing vote between the Founder's 50% and the AE's 44%: the Founder with any one AMKE share has a Simple Majority, and the AE with all of them only reaches 50%, so its independence decides whether the Founder's 50% is in effect a majority.
- *Draft assumes:* the suspension is valid. The routing of the pension-share votes is backed by the Pensionsprozent Agreement (Art. 10A.9B(c)). Art. 10A.1 asserts the AMKE's independence in brackets. **Owner to confirm** who controls the AMKE and who sits on its board.

**I-5. Drag-along and Prozent shares (Art. 5.5).**
- *Question:* Can the drag-along exclude the Prozent shares?
- *Why it matters:* Otherwise a 75% drag (the Founder and the AE hold 94%) could force the Prozent shares to a buyer, who would then hold 100% and could remove the Prozente "unanimously". The cost is that an acquirer gets at most 94% plus the pool share.
- *Draft assumes:* the exclusion is valid. Pool shares are not excluded: they are an investment of the Pensionskasse, and Art. 4.2A now says expressly that they are not Prozent shares for Arts. 5.5, 6.4A and 10A.5. Art. 10A.5C entrenches the exclusion, so it cannot be removed by a Qualified Majority before a drag.

**I-6. Transfers to Prozente (Arts. 4.2A, 5.6, 6.6).**
- *Question:* Can the articles disapply the right of first refusal and the third-party approval for the AE's transfer to a new Prozent, for pool dealings and for moves to successor holders? Does the AE's free transfer of shares equal to 1% of the capital attract donation tax for the recipient under Law 2961/2001, and at what rate for a non-profit?
- *Why it matters:* Without the carve-out, the Founder could take part of the share pro rata and go above 50%. The same applies to an ordinary right of first refusal, which Art. 5.2 now offers only to the shareholders other than the Founder and the Prozent holders (the pool only within its band). Can an IKE's articles exclude named shareholders from the right of first refusal in this way?
- *Draft assumes:* the carve-out is valid. A sale of pool shares to a new person still needs approval under Art. 5.3. Every transferee under Art. 5.6 must first adhere to the SHA, and the managers do not register one who has not. The tax treatment of the AE's free transfer to a new Prozent is now confirmed by the accountant before the transfer (Art. 6.6 "Share", with a bracket for donation tax under Law 2961/2001); please advise on the rate for a non-profit recipient and on any tax for the AE.

**I-7. Founder at exactly 50% and vesting (Art. 4.2B; SHA 6.1, 6.2, 9.2).**
- *Question:* The owner has decided that the Founder holds exactly 50% always, with no exception: ordinary voting shares equal to exactly 50% of the capital (initially 50), with votes equal to 50% of the capital, the Founder subscribing on any capital increase the new shares needed to stay at 50% (the Reserve Shares, held without votes before the AE buys them, are covered in I-35). Vesting and leaver terms are therefore drafted as a loss of economic and voting rights on the affected shares (an assignment of distributions and an irrevocable voting proxy), with no transfer. Under Law 4072/2012, can a shareholder validly assign future distributions and grant an irrevocable proxy over its own shares for this purpose, and can the articles or the SHA suspend voting rights for an unvested or bad-leaver Founder? What else could achieve vesting without a transfer (for example a contractual penalty or a claw-back of distributions)?
- *Why it matters:* A buy-back or forced sale would take the Founder below 50%, which the draft no longer allows.
- *Draft assumes:* the rights-based mechanism works as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. Who receives the distributions and who directs the votes is bracketed for the owner. Capital increases keep the Founder at 50% (Art. 4.2B).

**I-8. Kassenfonds and Kassenreserve (Art. 10A.7).**
- *Question:* Is it compatible with Law 4308/2014 to set aside a cash fund equal to 1% of revenue, even in loss years, with an optional reserve then appropriated from profit up to its balance? How should the two be presented in the accounts?
- *Why it matters:* A reserve can only be formed out of profit, and the reviewer flagged the earlier "restricted reserve funded from revenue" as unsound.
- *Draft assumes:* the cash fund and the equity reserve are kept separate, and the accountant decides the presentation. Neither the Kassenreserve nor the Kassenfonds may be distributed to shareholders except by a unanimous decision under Art. 10A.5, because a distribution would undo the 1% set-aside. Using them for an operating shortfall by Simple Majority is treated as a loan, repaid from the following years' revenue before any distribution. Is such an internal "loan" between a reserve and the company's operations workable in the accounts? **Owner to confirm** that releasing the fund counts as lowering the contribution.

**I-9. Agents acting for the company and for the Pensionskasse (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* Is a written delegation from a named manager enough for Agents that are systems to make investments that bind the company? The same Kassenprozent Agents manage the pool for the Pensionskasse under a mandate from its board, and each trade is signed by the holder's legal representative. Does that hold up, given the conflict of an employer's agents trading employer shares for the employees' fund?
- *Why it matters:* Only the managers represent an IKE. The pool belongs to the Pensionskasse, not to the company.
- *Draft assumes:* authority runs through the named manager for the Kassenfonds and through the board's mandate for the pool. For pool work the Agents act only under the board's mandate, their oversight (Arts. 11A.2, 11A.2A) is exercised by a responsible manager the board appoints, person or system, who is not the Founder, a manager or anyone they control and is not supervised by any of them, and no manager may direct or stop a pool trade (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8, 11A.2). The Founder and the managers take no part in decisions on the pool (Art. 10A.9D).

**I-10. Granularity of the pool (Arts. 4.1, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* With [100] capital shares, the 1% band is a single share, so the pool can hold only one share or none. Should the capital be divided into more shares, for example 10,000?
- *Why it matters:* "Buy and sell within the band" only works in steps finer than 1%.
- *Draft assumes:* [100] shares (unchanged). Every Prozent holding is now stated as a percentage of the capital (Arts. 4.2A, 4.2B, 6.6), so a finer split does not change any holding. The founder pool's 50 Class F shares, 44 Reserve Shares and the Class A shares limit partial transfers (Pool Constitution Arts. 9.2, 9.6): a transfer keeps the classes only as nearly in proportion as whole shares allow. A finer split would also serve the founder pool (I-40 (k)). **Owner to decide.**

**I-11. The AMKE ring-fence (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9B).**
- *Question:* Greek law has no general trust. Does a donation with a charge (δωρεά υπό τρόπο, Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505, to confirm), together with an amendment to the AMKE's statutes and segregated accounts, keep the Pensionskasse and the pension shares away from the AMKE's other creditors and its general purposes? Can members enforce the terms as third-party beneficiaries (Civil Code Art. 411, to confirm)? Would a separate AMKE used only for the Pensionsprozent be safer?
- *Why it matters:* Until a TEA exists, the AMKE holds the members' money.
- *Draft assumes:* segregation "as far as Greek law allows". Until the agreement and the statute changes are in place, the company holds the contributions in its own segregated account. The owner decided that the AMKE holds the Pensionskasse at the start, so a separate AMKE is offered here only as an option.

**I-12. Unlicensed insurance or pension activity before a TEA (Art. 10A.9 "Until the move").**
- *Question:* Is an AMKE that accumulates the 1% before there is any member, then pays the premiums of an insured group plan and pays continuity support, carrying on insurance business (Law 4364/2016) or occupational retirement provision outside Law 4680/2020?
- *Why it matters:* If it is, the interim arrangement is unlawful and a licensed vehicle has to come first.
- *Draft assumes:* under the phasing decided on 2026-11-23, the AMKE never holds members' savings: from the first member, risk cover and retirement savings are held by a licensed insurer under the group plan, the AMKE promises no rate or annuity, and every guarantee is owed directly by the company as employer.

**I-13. Enforceability of the Swiss guarantee (Arts. 3.3, 10A.9A, 10A.9C, 11.7).**
- *Question:* Is a contractual promise to apply BVG minimums, using a shadow account and comparing benefit by benefit, enforceable under Greek law when it is written into each member's employment contract or engagement terms? Must the company provide for it in its accounts, and how?
- *Why it matters:* Articles bind shareholders, not employees. Comparing item by item stops a better Greek benefit from being netted against a worse Swiss one.
- *Draft assumes:* the guarantee is enforceable because it is in each member's written terms. Art. 3.3 now makes an exception for Art. 10A.9A.

**I-14. Swiss figures to confirm each year (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Which figures are in force for 2026: the BVG minimum interest rate (1.25% in 2024 and 2025), the conversion rate (6.8% at 65), the upper salary limit, and the savings-credit bands (7/10/15/18%)? Is the disability scale cited correctly? The draft now brackets it as [Art. 24(1) BVG: to confirm]; Art. 24a BVG appears to concern adjusting a pension when the degree of disability changes.
- *Why it matters:* These are the guaranteed floors.
- *Draft assumes:* the figures in force each year, converted from CHF at the ECB average rate. Entry thresholds and coordination deductions are not applied.

**I-15. Disability trigger and survivors (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Does EFKA (KEPA) grant no pension below 50% incapacity? Can a fund-appointed doctor applying the method of Art. 16 ATSG stand in for the Swiss IV decision? Should unmarried partners beyond those in a registered cohabitation agreement (Law 4356/2015) be covered, as some Swiss regulations allow?
- *Why it matters:* The BVG ties disability benefits to the first-pillar decision, which does not exist in Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the KEPA certificate is used, with the doctor as fallback, and registered partners are treated as spouses.

**I-16. Annuities and capital (Art. 10A.9A "Retirement").**
- *Question:* Can a Greek TEA, which is usually defined-contribution, or the company, secure a lifelong annuity at the BVG conversion rate other than by buying it from a licensed insurer? Retirement age in Greece is 67, against the Swiss reference age of 65.
- *Why it matters:* Longevity risk is insurance risk.
- *Draft assumes:* the annuity is bought from an insurer authorised in Greece or elsewhere in the EEA under Solvency II (the same formula is used for risk cover and for a group contract in Arts. 10A.9 and 10A.10), the company pays any extra premium, and the rate is adjusted actuarially (upwards) for each year of retirement after 65.

**I-17. Vested benefits and transfers out (Art. 10A.9A).**
- *Question:* Which receiving vehicles are lawful for a Greek leaver (another EU occupational scheme, a PEPP, an insurance product)? When does Greek law allow payment in cash?
- *Why it matters:* The Swiss vested-benefits account does not exist in Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the member chooses among the vehicles that are lawful, and cash is paid only where Greek law allows.

**I-18. Tax and social security of the Pensionsprozent (Art. 10A.6).**
- *Question:* Are the IKE's payments deductible? Are benefits remuneration subject to EFKA contributions and employment-income tax? Do withholding tax on the AMKE's investment income and donation tax (Law 2961/2001) apply? How does all this compare with direct group insurance or a TEA?
- *Why it matters:* The AMKE route may cost members more.
- *Draft assumes:* the accountant confirms the treatment in writing before the first payment. If the AMKE route costs more, the company pays the difference or moves the arrangement early.

**I-19. Employer exposure cap (Art. 10A.9E).**
- *Question:* Is Art. 57 BVV 2 (5%) the right Swiss model, and does Art. 58 BVV 2 allow employer investments above the limit when they are secured? What is the sponsor-investment limit under Law 4680/2020 (5%, or 10% within a group), and in which article?
- *Why it matters:* At the start, the Pensionskasse holds little besides the two IKE shares, so the cap is exceeded at once. The pool share must then be sold down within [12] months, and the company must secure the excess value of the Pensionsprozent share.
- *Draft assumes:* sell-down applies to pool shares only, the Pensionsprozent share is never sold (it is permanent), and the company secures the excess. **Owner to confirm** whether a build-up period should delay the sell-down. Swiss law has none, so adding one would lower the standard. Note that the pool may trade only with independent third parties: the AE is controlled by the Founder (Art. 4.2C), so it cannot buy pool shares in a sell-down. That narrows the buyers to persons admitted under Art. 5.3 (option holders, outside investors), and pool shares may not find a buyer at fair value within [12] months, in which case the company secures the excess. **Owner question:** should the AE be allowed as a counterparty for the pool, despite being Founder-controlled (for example only at independently set fair value and with the Pensionskasse board's approval)? The draft does not allow it.

**I-20. The pathway to a TEA (Art. 10A.10).**
- *Question:* What membership, capital, licensing and actuarial requirements apply to a stand-alone TEA, and what is the minimum number of members? Is about [50] members (the owner's scale trigger) realistic, and can a company this size join a multi-employer TEA earlier? Can contractors ("Agents who are persons") be members of an occupational TEA? Can a TEA hold unlisted shares of its sponsor?
- *Why it matters:* An agentic company with few employees may never meet the requirements for a stand-alone TEA.
