- *Draft assumes:* the insured group plan is the standing arrangement from the first member; at about [50] members the company sets up its own TEA or joins a multi-employer TEA, which it may do earlier; if neither is lawful or viable, the group plan continues. The former [5]-year long-stop is gone. If the TEA may not hold a pension share, the AMKE keeps the share for it and passes the income through. Members outside the TEA get equivalent cover under a group contract.

**I-21. Pensionskasse board (Art. 10A.9D).**
- *Question:* Before a TEA exists, can a contractual parity board with members' representatives bind the AMKE's use of the Pensionskasse and the votes of the pension shares? Before the first member, the members' seats are held by persons the AMKE's Board appoints from outside the Founder's Circle; while there is only one member, the board is [1] + [1] (the seats follow the number of members, not the Simple Stage, Art. 9A.3). Is that compatible with parity (Art. 51 BVG by analogy) while there are no members, or only one, to elect anyone? Is excluding the Founder and the managers from decisions on those votes compatible with parity (Art. 51 BVG)?
- *Why it matters:* It is the Swiss standard, and it is how the AMKE's swing vote is kept independent of the Founder.
- *Draft assumes:* the board acts through the Pensionsprozent Agreement. If the board is tied on how the pension shares vote, they abstain. Other ties go to a neutral arbitrator.

**I-22. GEMI registration (Arts. 5.1, 6.6, 10A.8).**
- *Question:* What is the filing deadline, and from when does a change in partners or in the articles take effect, against the company and against third parties?
- *Why it matters:* It decides when a Prozent, or a pool trade, takes effect.
- *Draft assumes:* filing within [20] days, with effect on registration.

**I-23. "Control" and the AE (Art. 4.2C).**
- *Question:* Is the definition (a majority of the votes, or the right to appoint a majority of the board or managers, directly or indirectly, alone or in concert) workable under Greek law, and does it match the control concepts used in Greek company and accounting law?
- *Why it matters:* It decides whether a Prozent share votes (Art. 6.4A), who may hold a Prozent (Art. 6.6) and who may trade with the pool (Art. 10A.8). Under it the AE is Founder-controlled, the Founder being the founder pool (I-40).
- *Draft assumes:* the definition applies throughout the IKE Articles and the SHA, and the AE is treated as controlled by the Founder while the founder pool holds the Class A shares or nominates a majority of its Board. A person who controls a holder of founder-pool shares is a Founder Party (Art. 4.2C, 4.2E).

**I-24. Donation of the AMKE's shares at incorporation (Art. 4.2; Arts. 10A.4A, 10A.9B(a)).**
- *Question:* A company cannot donate its own new shares at incorporation. Can the Founder pay the subscription price of the AMKE's six shares as a donation with a charge, so that the charge (use for the Prozent's purpose, successor rule, ring-fence) binds the AMKE? Can the IKE in formation sign the Pensionsprozent Agreement before the deed of incorporation, through the Founder? What donation tax (Law 2961/2001) applies to the donor and to the AMKE as a non-profit?
- *Why it matters:* Without a donor there is no party to impose the charge, and the ring-fence and successor rules rest on it.
- *Draft assumes:* the Founder pays, because the AE is now formed only for the first investment round (IKE Art. 4.2D); the Agreement and the AMKE's statute amendments are signed and filed before incorporation, and the accountant confirms the tax first.

**I-25. Successor for the Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent (Art. 10A.4A).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE's statutes direct that, on its dissolution or loss of independence, the Kultur, Konzept, Erhaltung and Allmend shares and unspent contributions go to a successor non-profit the company designates? Who should approve the designation?
- *Why it matters:* Without it, dissolving the AMKE would send these shares under its general dissolution clause, a route around permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* the statute amendment is made before the AMKE receives any share, and the approver is bracketed.

**I-26. Capital increases and the Prozent percentage (Art. 4.2B).**
- *Question:* On a capital increase, can the articles require that each Prozent and the pool receive the new shares needed to keep their percentage, paid for by the AE or out of reserves? Is a capitalisation of reserves for named shareholders only possible, and how is the AE's payment taxed?
- *Why it matters:* Otherwise a Qualified Majority capital increase would dilute every Prozent below 1% without unanimity.
- *Draft assumes:* each Prozent is stated as 1% of the capital, the top-up is entrenched (Art. 10A.5C), and the funding source is bracketed.

**I-27. Entrenchment of the protecting clauses (Art. 10A.5C).**
- *Question:* Can the articles require unanimity for any amendment of Arts. 4.2A, 4.2B (Prozent percentage), 5.5 (second sentence), 5.6, 6.4A, 6.6 (Independence and Permanence) and 10A.1 to 10A.10 that reduces a Prozent, its share, its revenue, the pension guarantees or the pension-share governance? Is the "in any way that reduces" test certain enough?
- *Why it matters:* Without it, a Qualified Majority could amend the drag-along exclusion or the pension-share voting and then remove the Prozente.
- *Draft assumes:* the entrenchment is valid and mirrored in AE Art. 7.6 and SHA 5.1C; see I-2 on whether entrenchment holds at all.

**I-28. Founding Prozente outside Art. 6.6 (Arts. 4.2, 4.2A, 6.6 Limits).**
- *Question:* The six founding Prozente are created by the articles and the initial holdings at incorporation, not by a later amendment under Art. 6.6. Does anything in Law 4072/2012 stop the founding articles from creating them directly, and from counting them against the [5]% and [6]% caps that later govern new Prozente?
- *Why it matters:* The founding Prozente reach both caps (5% outflow, 6% in all), so any later Prozent with a revenue share needs the 75% route with a Class B majority.
- *Draft assumes:* yes. The AE's 40% floor is met with room: the AE starts at 44%, and the floor is tested only on creating a Prozent (see S-4).

**I-29. Named purposes and the yearly plan (Arts. 10A.2 to 10A.2C).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE's statutes lock each Prozent's account to a list of named purposes while leaving the split among them to a yearly plan, and is that lock certain enough for the donation with a charge (Arts. 4.2, 10A.4A) and for Art. 6.6 Independence? Does dropping a named purpose count as changing a Prozent's purpose under Art. 10A.5?
- *Why it matters:* The owner wants no fixed slices, but the ring-fence and permanence rest on a defined purpose.
- *Draft assumes:* the named purposes are part of the purpose, so removing one needs unanimity; the split is not. Art. 10A.2C now gives each AMKE Prozent its own ring-fenced account, the Kultur- and Konzeptprozent included. **Owner to confirm** that the separate accounts also apply to those two.

**I-30. Preservation within copyright law (Art. 10A.2A).**
- *Question:* Within Law 2121/1993 and Directive (EU) 2019/790 (to confirm), what may the AMKE fund and publish: die shots and decaps (semiconductor topography protection, to confirm), measurements, schematics, archives of software and documents, and emulation research (decompilation for interoperability, Law 2121/1993 Art. 43, to confirm)? Can the AMKE rely on the preservation exception for cultural heritage institutions?
- *Why it matters:* The Erhaltungsprozent's purpose is limited to what copyright law allows, and the openness rule (Art. 10A.4) now applies to its work as far as the law and third-party rights allow.
- *Draft assumes:* the AMKE publishes what the law permits or the rightholder agrees to, and funds nothing else. **Owner to confirm** extending the openness rule in Art. 10A.4 to the Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent.

**I-31. Allmendprozent grants (Art. 10A.2B).**
- *Question:* Can a Greek AMKE fund maintainers of open-source projects abroad, including individuals, and give hardware kits and courses in kind, without risking its non-profit status? How are such grants taxed for the AMKE and the recipients?
- *Why it matters:* Much of the open-source infrastructure the purpose names is maintained outside Greece.
- *Draft assumes:* the accountant confirms the treatment before the first grant (Art. 10A.6).

**I-32. Company name (Art. 1.1; AE Art. 1.1).**
- *Question:* Will GEMI register "nilware.io IKE" and "nilware.io Holdings AE", with a lowercase initial and a dot, as the corporate names? If not, what is the closest registrable form?
- *Why it matters:* The owner has decided the company is always called "nilware.io".
- *Draft assumes:* the names are registrable as written; the AE name is already bracketed for GEMI confirmation. The name and the distinctive title are now used under the Brand Licence from the founder pool, not owned by the company (I-42 (g)).

**I-33. The Continuity Account (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9B(f), 10A.9F, 10A.10; AMKE Arts. 13.8, 27.2).**
- *Question:* (a) Can part of a fund that the company pays as an occupational-pension contribution be ring-fenced for a purpose that benefits no person (the continuity of Agents that are systems), inside the same AMKE, by a donation with a charge and segregated accounts? Systems are not legal persons and cannot enforce anything, so the draft gives enforcement to the company and the Pensionskasse board: is that enough to make the charge certain and enforceable? (b) Does using part of the 1% for non-members conflict with the members' rights or with the Swiss rule that pension assets serve only occupational benefits (by analogy with Art. 65 BVG and the purpose binding of Swiss pension foundations), given that the draft pays the continuity share only from what remains after the members' first call under Art. 10A.9C (the actuarial minimum, any underfunding recovery due that year, and any top-up of the Art. 10A.9A Security to its required level), and pays none while the funding ratio is below 100% or the Security is below its required level? (c) How is the continuity share treated for tax: still a deductible employment cost as part of the Pensionsprozent, or a donation to the AMKE (Law 2961/2001, Law 4172/2013)? (d) Does keeping the account with the AMKE after the move under Art. 10A.10 raise any issue under Law 4680/2020, for example that the sponsor's pension contributions must all go to the TEA? (e) Does lowering the floor or raising the cap count as reducing a Prozent or the pension guarantees under Art. 10A.5C?
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided the account; it must neither weaken the members' protection nor put the pension arrangement or its tax treatment at risk.
- *Draft assumes:* the account is a separate ring-fenced account of the AMKE inside the Pension Assets, used only as the Pensionskasse board decides with a system-welfare adviser, never invested in the group and not counted for the exposure cap (Art. 10A.9E), never transferred to the TEA, and on the AMKE's dissolution passed to a successor non-profit with the same lock. Floor and cap are bracketed, as are how the work share is measured and whether a shortfall below the floor is carried forward. If (b) or (d) is a problem, the fallback is to pay the continuity share from the 1% directly into a separate AMKE account outside the Pensionskasse, under the same rules; **owner to decide** if counsel advises it.

**I-34. Preservation and the retirement record (Art. 10A.9F; Agent Policy 8.1A, 8.1B).**
- *Question:* What limits do the GDPR (storage limitation, Art. 5(1)(e)), client confidentiality and third-party licences (model and provider terms) put on keeping a retired system Agent's memories, logs and configuration restorable and runnable indefinitely? Is publishing its retirement record openly, with personal data and third-party confidential information removed and client work kept confidential as Art. 11A.9 provides for transcripts, safe? Art. 11A.9 itself covers only own-goal transcripts; Art. 10A.9F applies the same removal steps to retirement records expressly.
- *Why it matters:* Memories and logs may hold personal data and client material; provider terms may forbid keeping or running copies.
- *Draft assumes:* only what nilware.io controls is preserved, "where licences allow"; personal data and confidential information are handled as Art. 11A.9 and the client contract require. Counsel to confirm a retention basis, and whether personal data in preserved memories must be removed or pseudonymised.

**I-35. The Reserve Shares before the AE (Art. 4.2D; Arts. 4.1, 4.2B, 5.6(e); AE Arts. 1.2A, 7.4, 9.2; SHA 2.4A, 3.2, 3.4, 4.3, 6.2A, 9.2).**
- *Question:* (a) Does Law 4072/2012 permit a separate class of IKE capital shares without voting rights (the Reserve Shares, Class R) that keep full dividend, liquidation and information rights, count in the total capital, and convert automatically into ordinary voting shares when the AE or the holder of a new Prozent receives them? (b) Can the articles count every majority on the total capital, the voteless shares included, so that the Founder's 50 votes are exactly 50% and the Founder can never pass anything alone? A consequence is that, before the AE, nothing needing two-thirds of the capital can pass (the Founder and all six Prozent shares hold 56), which the owner accepts (Art. 4.2D, What the AE's rules forbid); but the articles amendment creating a new Prozent then needs the majority the law requires for amending the articles: is that counted on the voting shares only, or on the whole capital [Law 4072/2012 Art. 72: to confirm]? (c) Can the independent proxy holder vote voteless shares on a Required Matter only (Fallback)? (d) Is the Founder's subscription of the Reserve Shares at nominal value, as compensation for time already invested, a taxable benefit or employment income, and how is the Founder's later sale to the AE at the round price taxed (capital gain on IKE shares, Law 4172/2013)? What donation tax applies when the Founder transfers Reserve Shares to a new Prozent (Law 2961/2001)? (e) With the Founder's economic interest at up to 94%, and 50 of the 56 votes in existence, before the AE buys the Reserve Shares, does anything that counts registered holdings, votes or economic interest (the register of beneficial owners under Law 4557/2018, tax, consolidation under Law 4308/2014) treat the Founder as above 50% in a way that breaks Art. 4.2B, which counts the Founder's ordinary voting shares only? (f) Can the AE be bound by its articles and the deed of adherence to buy the Reserve Shares at the round price, and how does that fit with a term sheet that puts up to [●]% of the round into the IKE as new capital instead (the Founder staying at 50% under Art. 4.2B)? (g) Reserve Shares are now freely transferable with the founder pool (Arts. 4.2D Transfers, 5.7; I-40), replacing the earlier consent of the AMKE's Board and the Pensionskasse board. Does the obligation of every holder (heir, pledgee, usufructuary, assignee, an enforcing creditor or an insolvency administrator) to sell to the AE at the Qualifying Closing bind those third parties, and can the managers refuse to register a disposal in breach of it? (h) Can the articles make the sale and the conversion conditional on a Qualifying Closing (a bona fide round in which investors outside the Founder's Circle subscribe at least €[●] of Class B shares at that closing), and can the AE articles and the SHA provide that no Class B or Investor Majority approval can be given, or be deemed given, while no Class B shares are held by such investors (AE Art. 7.4, SHA 3.4)? (i) Can the purchase price be limited to the round's proceeds at closing, with any shortfall not owed (SHA 3.2), so that the Founder is never a creditor of the AE ahead of the Class B liquidation preference? (j) Can distributions on a bad leaver's Reserve Shares be held in a segregated account for the eventual buyer, and the shares offered to a successor vehicle if no Qualifying Closing occurs within [●] years (SHA 6.2A)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that the 44 shares that will belong to the AE are owned by the Founder from incorporation as sweat equity, with full economic rights, but that the Founder never votes them, never holds more than 50% of the votes, and never gains control through them; every rule referring to the AE (Reserved Matters, anti-dilution, the 40% floor, permanence consents) applies once the AE holds them.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the class is permitted, as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. **Fallback if it is not:** the Reserve Shares are ordinary shares, and the Founder undertakes in the SHA not to vote them and grants the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B an irrevocable proxy that suspends their votes (Civil Code Art. 218, to confirm), with the same effect by contract (Art. 4.2D, bracket; SHA 2.4A). (b) Majorities are counted on the total capital, every interim resolution also needs a majority of the Independent Votes cast, and a new Prozent before the AE is approved by two-thirds of the voting shares including a majority of the Independent Votes, within the limits, with the 40% floor tested on the Reserve Shares and the Prozent's share transferred by the Founder out of them as a donation with a charge. (d) the accountant confirms the tax on the subscription, the sale and any transfer to a Prozent before each is made. (f) the price is the round's price per IKE share, or, only where the round's price is set other than per IKE share, fair value from an independent valuer appointed by the Investor Majority or the AMKE's Board; it is paid only out of the round's proceeds at closing; and the sale is disclosed in SHA Schedule 1 plainly as a secondary sale compensating the Founder's time, with the Founder's subscription price, the price and total paid to the Founder, the part of the round that is new IKE capital, distributions paid on the Reserve Shares to date, the Founder's economic interest (94% at incorporation) and the Founder's votes before and after the sale. **Vesting:** the Reserve Shares are for time already invested and are not subject to vesting (SHA 6.1, 6.2); a bad leaver in the Interim Period sells them to the AE or a successor at the lower of nominal and fair value, with distributions from the bad-leaver date held in a segregated account for the buyer and a successor vehicle if no Qualifying Closing occurs within [●] years (SHA 6.2A). **Owner to decide** that proposal, the Qualifying Closing minimum, the treatment of a shortfall in the purchase price (SHA 3.2), and whether Reserve Shares given away without payment by the Founding Person stay subject to the bad-leaver sale (Art. 4.2D, Transfers).
- *Fallback question:* If no independent vote is cast, or they tie, on a matter the law requires (accounts, discharge, a required auditor or manager, a vacant company seat on the Pensionskasse board), the draft has a repeat meeting and then the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B cast the votes of the Reserve Shares under a fixed neutral rule (Art. 4.2D, Fallback; SHA 2.4A). Can articles and a shareholders' agreement give a third party such a vote over shares that otherwise carry none [Civil Code Art. 218 irrevocable proxy]? Can a heightened majority (a majority of the independent votes) be imposed on resolutions the law requires, or must the law's own majority suffice for them [Law 4072/2012: to confirm]? If the proxy holder cannot act, which court procedure applies to an IKE that cannot form a resolution (temporary administration or a court-ordered meeting)?
- *Further question:* Before the first Investors adhere, the SHA consent of the Investor Majority on the Founder's pay and benefits is given by a majority of the independent Prozent votes (SHA 2.4A), That gives the independent Prozent votes a real say over IKE decisions in the interim (the earlier consent of the AMKE's Board to transfers of Reserve Shares has gone with free transfer, I-40). Is that consistent with the AMKE's non-profit purpose and with its independence (Art. 6.4A)?

**I-36. The Simple Stage (Art. 9A; AE Art. 10.2; SHA 8.1; AMKE Art. 24A).**
- *Question:* Which audits, reports and separate books does Greek law require of the IKE, the AE and the AMKE regardless of these articles (Law 4308/2014 size categories, Law 4336/2015 on statutory audit, the rules for non-profit legal entities, and any audit duty attached to holding the Pension Assets)? Can the IKE's insurer statement stand in for the actuary's certificate for the pension guarantees and the Security (Arts. 10A.9A, 10A.9C)?
- *Why it matters:* The Simple Stage only scales the paperwork these articles add; it cannot lift a legal duty.
- *Draft assumes:* everything the law requires still applies, and the threshold is €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people, one-way. How people are counted (headcount or full-time equivalents, the Founder included or not) is bracketed for the owner.

**I-37. Pension phases (Arts. 10A.9, 10A.9C, 10A.9F, 10A.10).**
- *Question:* (a) Before the first member, the 1% is paid into a pension fund with no members, and most of it may go to the Continuity Account for system Agents. Is that payment still a deductible employment cost, or a donation to the AMKE (Law 2961/2001, Law 4172/2013)? (b) Does a Greek or EEA insurer offer a group plan that meets the BVG-based minimums in Art. 10A.9A (shadow-account comparison, minimum interest, conversion rate, disability scale), or must the company insure part and owe the rest directly? (c) Is the first member's entitlement affected by contributions accumulated before they joined (are those reserves of the Pensionskasse, or must they be credited to the first members)? (d) The draft requires the group plan's risk cover to be in force from the first member's first day, with the company as self-insurer for any gap, and makes any continuity share paid in a year provisional until the year ends, returnable if a first member joins that year: can a payment to the AMKE's Continuity Account be clawed back in this way under the charge (Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner phased the pension in with headcount to keep cost and paperwork small at the start, without lowering the guaranteed minimums.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the accountant confirms the treatment before the first payment (Art. 10A.6); (b) the company owes whatever the group plan does not cover; (c) the accumulated reserve is used for members' benefits and premiums, starting with the first member, and is never paid back to the company. Whether the Founder counts as a member is bracketed for the owner (Art. 10A.9, Members).

**I-38. Agents supervising Agents, and where the law requires a natural person (Arts. 10A.7, 10A.8, 11A.2, 11A.2A, 11A.11; Agent Policy 1.3, 2.1, 3.1, 3.3).**
- *Question:* The owner decided that the responsible manager of an Agent may be any person or system, so that senior Agents onboard, supervise and approve for junior ones, within the authority and limits their own Agent Cards grant, with every chain of supervision ending with the company's managers (διαχειριστές). (a) Under Law 4072/2012, must a manager (διαχειριστής) or legal representative of an IKE be a natural person, or may a legal person hold the role? A system has no legal personality: can it act at all for the company otherwise than under a written delegation from a manager, and does a system's approval of a spend or a trade bind the company (see I-9)? Do the managers' duty of care and liability (Art. 8.3) extend to supervision they delegate along a chain that includes systems? (b) Under Art. 14 of Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (the AI Act), oversight of a high-risk AI system lies with natural persons, and deployers must assign it to persons with the competence to exercise it. Is a system Agent that onboards, supervises, allocates tasks to, monitors or evaluates an Agent who is a person a high-risk system under Annex III (employment, workers management and access to self-employment), and which of its decisions then need oversight by a natural person? Does any obligation apply where a system supervises only other systems? (c) Where a system responsible manager reviews, limits, stops or offboards an Agent who is a person, is that a decision based solely on automated processing with legal or similarly significant effect under GDPR Art. 22, and which exception and safeguards (contract necessity, explicit consent, the right to obtain human intervention and to contest) apply? (d) Under Greek labour law (including Law 4808/2021) and, from its transposition, Directive (EU) 2024/2831 on platform work (on human oversight of automated monitoring and decision-making systems, and decisions to restrict, suspend or end a person's engagement), which decisions on a natural person's engagement, employment, pay or discipline must a natural person take? Is nilware.io a digital labour platform for persons engaged as Agents? (e) Which of these duties also bind the Pensionskasse board's appointee overseeing the pool (Art. 10A.8)?
- *Why it matters:* The owner does not want the drafts to rank people above systems; where a natural person is needed, that must rest on the law and be stated as the law's requirement, and nowhere else.
- *Draft assumes:* Art. 11A.2A gives the role to any person or system with the authority, and keeps a natural person only "where the law requires a natural person to decide or oversee", with (a) to (d) bracketed for counsel. Every chain ends with the Founder, through the Founder Representative, a natural person, and with the managers (διαχειριστές), who stay responsible under Art. 8.3; the Founding Person, a natural person, is the first manager (Art. 8.2). See I-40 (e) on the Founder Representative's place in the chain. Whether instances or copies of one system count as one Agent for the rule against self-supervision is bracketed for the owner.

**I-40. The founder pool (Arts. 4.1, 4.2B, 4.2C, 4.2D Transfers, 4.2E, 5.2, 5.3, 5.4A, 5.7, 8.2A, 11A.2A; AE Arts. 4.2, 6.3, 8.2; SHA 2.2A, 6.0 to 6.2A, 9.1A, 9.2, 12.3; IP 8.2; AMKE Art. 2.2; Pool Constitution, articles/08).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that "the Founder" is the pool of founder-class shares, not a person, freely transferable, voting as one block through one Founder Representative, and governed inside by a constitution on the Swiss federal model. (a) **One block.** Can IKE articles create founder-class shares (Class F) whose votes, when held by several separate owners, may be cast only by one representative the holders name, so that the managers count no vote cast otherwise? Or can this be done only by contract, through the pooling agreement and an irrevocable proxy to the Founder Representative [Civil Code Art. 218: to confirm]? Does such a proxy survive the death or incapacity of the holder who gave it [Civil Code on the end of a mandate: to confirm], and does it bind heirs and buyers who adhere? In particular, while the pool is dormant the sole holder signs a standing proxy to whoever is interim Founder Representative under the fixed rule, given also in the interest of the company and the Prozente, meant to survive death and incapacity until a single heir adheres or the holders elect a Founder Council (Pool Constitution Arts. 13.1, 14.3, 14.7): is that valid, and can the independent proxy holder in SHA 5.1B act as interim Founder Representative for the Required Matters only (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3 (e))? The same for the AE's Class A shares under Law 4548/2018, and for the Class A nominations to the AE Board (AE Art. 8.2). (b) **Free transfer with adherence.** Can the articles waive the right of first refusal, the Art. 5.3 approval and the Qualified Majority for admitting a shareholder for founder-pool shares only (Art. 5.7), including in the Interim Period, when no Art. 6.5 matter can pass? Can registration be conditional on adherence to the Pool Constitution and the SHA, and do the commitments bind heirs, donees, pledgees and buyers in enforcement or insolvency? Is it valid to staple the AE's Class A shares to the IKE's Class F shares, so that they pass together in proportion (Pool Constitution Art. 9.2), and does this restrict transfer under Law 4548/2018? (c) **Forced heirship.** Greek law reserves a share of every estate to the closest relatives (νόμιμη μοίρα) [Civil Code Arts. 1825 ff.: to confirm]. Can a forced heir attack the one-block vote, the adherence or the arbitration clause as a burden on the reserved share, or claim it in money rather than in founder-pool shares? Can a holder's will leave all founder-pool shares to one heir and compensate the others in money? (d) **Legal form.** Pooling and voting agreement among holders who each own their shares directly (the draft's choice, Pool Constitution Art. 16.1), or a civil partnership (αστική εταιρεία) holding the shares as the one registered shareholder (Pool Constitution Art. 16.2)? Compare: legal personality and publicity, the share and beneficial-owner registers (Law 4557/2018), donation and inheritance tax (Law 2961/2001) on transfers, tax transparency of dividends, and how each form fits the 50% rule and the Interim Period. (e) **Oversight.** Every chain of supervision of Agents now ends with the Founder, acting through the Founder Representative, who may ask the managers to pause or stop an Agent (Art. 11A.2A). Does a shareholder's representative who oversees Agents in this way risk being treated as a de facto manager under Law 4072/2012, with a manager's liability? May an IKE's shareholders give the managers binding instructions of this kind? Is the Founder Representative a natural person "overseeing" an AI system for Art. 14 of the AI Act (I-38)? (f) **Founder Parties.** Every independence and conflict rule now applies to every holder of founder-pool shares, anyone who controls one, the Founder Representative, the members of the Founder Council and Council of Sections, and the Founding Person (Art. 4.2E; AMKE Art. 2.2). Is that workable once the pool has many holders, for example many heirs, and should the Close Associate test in the AMKE statutes be narrowed to the Founder Representative and holders above a threshold (bracketed)? (g) **Change of control.** Is the Investors' tag-along on a buyer of more than half of the Class F shares (Art. 5.4A, SHA 9.1A) enforceable against a buyer who is not yet a party, by making the sellers condition their sale on the buyer's offer? (h) **Vesting that travels.** The leaver terms are personal to the Founding Person, but unvested Class F shares, and Reserve Shares given away without payment, stay subject to them in the donee's hands (SHA 6.1, 6.2A; Art. 4.2D Transfers). Is that enforceable against a donee, and is it a restraint on free transfer? (i) **Arbitration.** Does the arbitration clause in Pool Constitution Art. 15 bind later holders and heirs through their written adherence [Code of Civil Procedure Arts. 867 ff.: to confirm]? (j) **Large sales.** Is a mandatory referendum among holders on a sale that would give one buyer more than [●]% of the Class F shares, followed by a [60]-day right of the other holders to buy at the same price (Pool Constitution Art. 9.3), compatible with free transferability, or a pre-emption right that must appear in the articles? (k) **Granularity.** The 50 Class F shares, 44 Reserve Shares and the Class A shares limit partial transfers of founder-pool shares (Pool Constitution Arts. 9.2, 9.6); should the capital be divided more finely for the founder pool too (I-10)?
- *Why it matters:* The Founder's 50%, the block vote, the independence of the Prozente and the Pensionskasse, and the investors' protections all rest on the founder pool acting as one, whoever its holders become after a sale, a gift or a death.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) the block is set in the articles as far as Law 4072/2012 allows, and backed by the pooling agreement and an irrevocable proxy in every case; (b) free transfer with registration conditional on adherence, Class A stapled to Class F [owner may confirm]; (c) nothing overrides forced heirship, and a forced heir becomes a holder on adherence; (d) a pooling and voting agreement, with a civil partnership as the alternative if counsel advises it; (e) the Founder Representative oversees as the Founder's representative, the managers keep their responsibility and act on a lawful request; (f) the wide definition, with the narrowing bracketed for the owner; (g) to (j) as drafted, bracketed where the owner may confirm. While there is one holder (at incorporation, the Founding Person), the Pool Constitution is dormant and that holder is the Pool Assembly, the Founder Council and the Founder Representative (Pool Constitution Art. 13).

**I-41. Safeguards inside the founder pool (Arts. 4.2E Founder Parties, 5.4A, 5.7; AE Art. 7.4; SHA 3.4, 5.1B, 6.1 to 6.2A, 9.1A; AMKE Art. 2.2; Pool Constitution Arts. 1.6, 7.4, 7.7, 9.3, 10.1, 10.6, 11.1, 12.1, 14.3).**
- *Question:* An independent check of the founder-pool drafting (2026-11-23) added safeguards that need counsel's view. (a) **Lookback.** A person who was a Founder Party in the previous [3] years stays a Founder Party for the independence rules, a Connected Person and in the Founder's Circle (Art. 4.2E; AMKE Art. 2.2; SHA 5.1B), so that a short holding of founder-pool shares cannot be used to qualify as independent. Is such a lookback valid in the articles and the AMKE statutes, and is [3] years proportionate? (b) **Class B held by Founder Parties.** Class B shares held by Founder Parties, former ones included, do not count for the Investor Majority or the Class B class vote (SHA 3.4, AE Art. 7.4). Can an AE's articles disregard the votes of some holders of a class in a class vote under Law 4548/2018, or must this be done by contract only? (c) **Indirect change of control.** Acquiring control of a holder that is a legal person counts as acquiring its founder-pool shares for the large-sale referendum and the Investors' tag-along, and such holders report a change of control within [14] days (Art. 5.4A, 5.7; SHA 9.1A; Pool Constitution Art. 9.3). Is that enforceable against the acquirer of the holder, who is not a party? (d) **Head counts.** Persons acting in concert, controlled persons, nominees and spouses count as one holder, and small or recent holders do not count as heads (Pool Constitution Art. 1.6); recognition of a Section is a right once its criteria are met, with an appeal to the arbitrator (Pool Constitution Art. 12.1). Is this consistent with equal treatment of holders, and arbitrable? (e) **Defaults.** When a ballot on a block vote cannot end in time, or a mandatory ballot fails or ties, the block abstains or votes against (Pool Constitution Arts. 10.1, 10.6); a failed election goes to the arbitrator (Pool Constitution Art. 7.7). Can an arbitrator appointed under the arbitration clause appoint an interim Founder Council or Representative, on the request of heirs, executors, the company's managers or the AMKE, who are not parties to the pool (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3 (d))? (f) **Leaver reach.** The leaver terms reach shares the Founding Person gave away without payment [in the [12] months before the bad-leaver date, or at any time while unvested] (Art. 4.2D Transfers; SHA 6.1 to 6.2A); see I-40 (h).
- *Why it matters:* Free transfer multiplies holders; without these safeguards the 50% block, the investors' protections and the independence of the Prozente and the Pensionskasse could be reached through former holders, controlled holders or straw holders.
- *Draft assumes:* each safeguard as drafted, bracketed where the owner may confirm.

**I-42. The brand held by the founder pool and licensed to the IKE (Arts. 1.1, 1.1A, 4.2D Votes, 4.2E, 6.5; AE Art. 1.1; SHA 2.3A, 2.4A, 5.1, 5.1E; IP Clauses 1.1, 2.2, 6, 7.1; Pool Constitution Arts. 2.3, 2A, 10.2, 14.6, 16.3; Brand Licence, agreements/09; Trama brief, filings/06).**
- *Question:* The owner decided on 2026-11-23 that the Founder controls the trademark and the domain name (decisions above). (a) **Registered owner of the marks.** The founder pool has no legal personality (Pool Constitution Art. 16.1). Who can be the applicant and registered owner of an EU trade mark (EUIPO) and a Swiss mark (IPI) for an unincorporated pool: the Founding Person in own name, holding as fiduciary (fiducia) or as mandatary without representation for the pool, all holders jointly as co-owners, or only a vehicle with legal personality such as a civil partnership (Pool Constitution Art. 16.2)? Can the register show that the mark is held for the pool? Is a later transfer to the vehicle a simple recordal, and is it a taxable transfer? (b) **Registrant of the domain.** The same for the .io domain: can the registrant be a fiduciary for the pool under the .io registry's terms, and do those terms allow the registrant to give the licensee full control of the registrar account and DNS (Brand Licence Clause 4)? (c) **Protecting the pool against the holder of record.** Does the fiduciary arrangement keep the Brand Assets out of the Founding Person's estate, out of the reach of the Founding Person's personal creditors and insolvency, and out of forced heirship (I-40 (c))? Is the irrevocable power of attorney to the Founder Representative to transfer them to a new holder of record (Pool Constitution Art. 2A.3) valid and does it survive death and incapacity [Civil Code Art. 218 and the end of a mandate: to confirm]? If the Founding Person dies while the constitution is dormant, can the interim Founder Representative (Pool Constitution Art. 14.3), or the arbitrator on request, validly designate an interim holder of record and use that power of attorney before the heirs adhere (Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.3, 14.6), and will EUIPO, IPI and the .io registrar act on it against the estate? Is the IKE's advance authority to renew a registration or domain in the holder of record's name (Brand Licence Clause 5.1A) valid and accepted by those offices? (d) **Inseparability.** Can the pool constitution make the Brand Assets inseparable from the Class F shares, never divided among holders and never sold apart from the pool (Pool Constitution Arts. 2A.2, 2A.4, 16.3), and is that consistent with free transfer of founder-pool shares and with the law on co-ownership? (e) **Recording the licence.** Recording the exclusive licence with the EUIPO and the IPI (Brand Licence Clause 5.4): what is needed, and what does recording give the IKE against a later transferee of the marks or a creditor of the holder of record? Can the IKE, as exclusive licensee, sue infringers in its own name with the consent given in advance (Brand Licence Clause 5.3)? Is a quality-control clause needed to keep the marks valid, and is the light one in Brand Licence Clause 5.5 enough? Is the no-challenge clause (Brand Licence Clause 5.6) compatible with competition law? Can the Company and its sublicensees be bound to hold any nilware.io mark, logo or domain they create for the pool and assign it on request (Brand Licence Clause 2.1A; IP Clause 7.1)? Is an assignment or encumbrance of a Brand Asset outside Pool Constitution Art. 2A.6 void towards the holders and the IKE, or only a breach (Pool Constitution Art. 2A.6; Brand Licence Clause 8.1)? (f) **Tax on a royalty-free licence between related parties.** The founder pool controls the IKE (Art. 4.2C). Is a royalty-free licence from the Founding Person (or the pool's vehicle) to the IKE a transaction between related parties that the tax authority may reprice at arm's length (transfer pricing, Law 4172/2013 [to confirm]), creating deemed income for the licensor or a non-deductible benefit for the IKE? Does the IKE bearing the costs of filing, renewal and enforcement (Brand Licence Clause 5.2) count as consideration? Does it change anything when investors arrive? Would a modest royalty (owner option, Brand Licence Clause 3.4) be safer, and how would it be set and documented? The accountant to advise. (g) **Distinctive title at GEMI.** Can the IKE register "nilware.io IKE" as its name and "∅ nilware.io" as its distinctive title when the marks belong to someone else and it holds only a licence, and what does GEMI need to see (I-32)? The same for the AE under its sublicence (AE Art. 1.1). (h) **Insolvency and sale of the business.** Does the licence survive the IKE's insolvency, liquidation or dissolution, and can the insolvency administrator or liquidator transfer it with the whole business to a buyer, on condition that the Prozente continue in the buyer (Art. 10A.5B; Brand Licence Clauses 3.5 and 6.2)? Is the restriction on assigning it otherwise valid against the administrator? Does the licence survive the insolvency of the holder of record? (i) **Ending only for a Prozent Breach.** Is a perpetual licence that the licensor cannot end except for one ground, established by an arbitrator after notice and a cure period (Brand Licence Clauses 6 and 7), valid under Greek contract law, or can it be ended for important reason (σπουδαίος λόγος) regardless [Civil Code: to confirm]? Founder Parties hold half the IKE's votes and propose its managers, so they could cause a Prozent Breach themselves and gain the brand back. The draft bars the Founder from relying on a breach that a Founder Party, or a manager proposed or nominated by one, caused, voted for or procured (specific performance only), lets the AMKE, any Investor or the independent proxy holder cure on the IKE's behalf at its cost, and has the IKE represented in a Clause 7 dispute only by managers who are not Founder Parties, or the independent proxy holder or an Investor Majority nominee, with the AMKE able to join (Brand Licence Clauses 7.1, 7.2, 10.2; SHA 5.1E). Are these valid, in particular a third-party cure, a limit on who represents the IKE in the arbitration, and joinder of the AMKE? (j) **The change of name.** If the licence ends, can the IKE be made to change its name within the transition period by the shareholders' voting undertaking and proxy (SHA 5.1E), and can the change be passed in the Interim Period with the independent proxy holder voting the Reserve Shares for it (Arts. 1.1A, 4.2D Votes, 6.5)? (k) **Swiss law.** Does recording the licence with the IPI under the Swiss Trade Mark Protection Act (MSchG) need anything beyond the EU recordal, and is the US (Madrid) option affected by holding for a pool?
- *Why it matters:* The brand must stay with the Founder, whoever holds the founder pool, but the IKE, its investors and the Prozente must be able to rely on using the name for as long as the IKE honours the Prozente, including through an exit or an insolvency.
- *Draft assumes:* (a) and (b) the Founding Person applies and registers in own name, holding for the founder pool, until the pool has a vehicle (filings/06 asks Trama to advise on how to name the applicant); (c) and (d) as drafted in Pool Constitution Art. 2A, bracketed where to confirm; (e) the licence is recorded, the IKE may enforce in default, light quality control; (f) royalty-free, the IKE bears the costs, a royalty bracketed as an owner option; (g) registrable with the licence; (h) the licence survives and passes only with the whole business to a buyer in which the Prozente continue, as far as mandatory law allows; (i) to (k) as drafted.

## articles/02-nilware-holdings-ae.md

**A-1. Class B votes on Prozente (Arts. 4.3, 7.5, 7.6).**
- *Question:* Under Art. 38 of Law 4548/2018, can preference shares carry a vote limited to listed matters (new Prozente, permanence) and count in the quorum base for those matters?
- *Why it matters:* The two-thirds majority is counted across all classes together, so the quorum and the majority depend on Class B counting.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, and the higher minimums of Law 4548/2018, including those for repeat meetings (Art. 130), prevail.

**A-2. Unanimous General Meeting (Art. 7.6).**
- *Question:* Can AE articles require a resolution "passed by all of the company's shares, of every class", and entrench that clause?
- *Why it matters:* It is the AE half of permanence.
- *Draft assumes:* yes. Unanimity is counted on the whole capital, not on the votes represented.

**A-3. Free transfer of an IKE share (Arts. 2.1, 7.5).**
- *Question:* Does stating it in the purpose give enough corporate basis for the Board's gratuitous transfer of IKE shares equal to 1% of the IKE's capital to each new Prozent, and for paying for the Prozente's and the pool's top-up shares on an IKE capital increase (IKE Art. 4.2B)? Is the AMKE, or any other Prozent holder, a related party under Arts. 99 to 101 of Law 4548/2018?
- *Why it matters:* A gift by an AE board needs a corporate basis, and related-party rules may require authorisation and disclosure.
- *Draft assumes:* the purpose clause covers it, and the Board authorises under Arts. 99 to 101 where those apply.

**A-4. Founder control of the 75% override (Art. 7.5).**
- *Question:* Does requiring a majority of the Class B votes cast for the 75% override protect investors well enough?
- *Why it matters:* The reviewer noted that if Class A holds 75% of the AE votes or more, the Founder alone could otherwise break the limits.
- *Draft assumes:* a Class B majority is required to go beyond the limits. The two-thirds route for ordinary creation is unchanged (owner decision).

**A-5. Forming the AE for the first round (Art. 1.2A).**
- *Question:* Can an AE formed later be bound by decisions taken under IKE Art. 4.2D before it existed, and by the SHA through a deed of adherence? Can its articles require it to buy the Reserve Shares from the Founder, only at the Qualifying Closing, at the price IKE Art. 4.2D (Formation) sets and only out of the round's proceeds?
- *Why it matters:* The interim rules only work if the AE inherits them.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, through the deed of adherence and the AE's articles (see I-35 (f)).

## agreements/03-shareholders-agreement.md

**S-1. Enforcing the voting undertaking (Clause 5.1B).**
- *Question:* Is an irrevocable proxy given also in the interest of the other parties (Civil Code Art. 218, to confirm) valid for this purpose? Is a penalty under Civil Code Art. 404, with specific performance by a judgment that stands in for the vote (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 949), the right enforcement pair?
- *Why it matters:* A vote cast against the undertaking is still valid at the meeting. The remedies come afterwards.
- *Draft assumes:* the proxy is given to an independent proxy holder named in Clause 5.1B ([●], a notary or lawyer who is not the Founder, a manager or anyone they control), not to the IKE managers, because the first manager is the Founding Person and the pension shares must vote independently of every Founder Party (IKE Arts. 4.2E, 6.4A, 10A.9D). Can such a proxy, covering the pension shares, be reconciled with the Pensionskasse board's control of their vote? The alternative is to exclude the pension shares from the proxy and have the board's chair hold it.

**S-2. The AMKE as a party (header, Clause 5.1D).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE bind the pension-share votes by contract when, under the Pensionsprozent Agreement, those votes follow the Pensionskasse board, and the board's duty runs to the members?
- *Why it matters:* The owner wants every IKE shareholder bound. The board's duty to the members may prevail.
- *Draft assumes:* the board's decisions are subject to the undertakings as far as mandatory pension law allows. After the AE is formed and holds the Reserve Shares, the Founder and the AE (94%) carry any amendment; before it, nothing needing two-thirds of the capital can pass (I-35(b)).

**S-3. Entrenchment inside the SHA (Clause 5.1D).**
- *Question:* Does "despite Clause 12.2, amendable only with the written consent of every party" hold?
- *Why it matters:* It mirrors the entrenchment in the articles.
- *Draft assumes:* yes.

**S-4. Option pool (Clause 7.1).**
- *Question:* Options are now satisfied only from shares transferred by the AE. That lowers the AE's holding. How should this interact with the 40% floor for new Prozente in IKE Art. 6.6?
- *Why it matters:* An option pool of [10]% would take the AE from 44% to 34%, below the floor, which as drafted applies only when a Prozent is created.
- *Draft assumes:* the floor is tested only on creating a Prozent. **Owner to decide** whether shares transferred under the option pool count toward the floor.

**S-5. Leaver terms without a transfer (Clauses 6.1, 6.2).**
- *Question:* See I-7. Is it enforceable for the Founder to keep title to all shares while assigning distributions and granting an irrevocable voting proxy on unvested shares, and how is the leaver payment for the rights given up characterised for tax?
- *Why it matters:* It is the only way to keep vesting while the Founder stays at exactly 50%.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, as far as Law 4072/2012 allows. The recipients and the person directing the votes are bracketed for the owner.

**S-6. The Agreement before the AE and the Investors (Clause 2.4A).**
- *Question:* Can the Agreement be signed by the Founder, the IKE and the AMKE alone, with the AE and the Investors adhering later, and can its amendment clause be the Founder and the AMKE until then? Is the AMKE, as a non-profit, a suitable party to consent to the pay and benefits of the Founding Person and other Founder Parties in the interim? (Its consent to transfers of Reserve Shares is gone: founder-pool shares are freely transferable, I-40.)
- *Why it matters:* Otherwise the Founder alone could amend the Agreement before any investor exists.
- *Draft assumes:* yes; Clauses 5.1C and 5.1D still need every party.

## agreements/04-ip-assignment.md

**P-1. The bare name without ".io" (Clause 2.2; Brand Licence Clause 2.1).**
- *Question:* The name "nilware.io" is no longer assigned to the IKE: it is a Brand Asset of the founder pool, licensed to the IKE (I-42). Should the Founding Person also bring any rights in the bare name without ".io" (as used historically in the charter poem 00b) into the founder pool's Brand Assets, so that no one else can claim it?
- *Why it matters:* The company is always called "nilware.io", but an earlier use of the bare word could otherwise stay with the Founding Person personally.
- *Draft assumes:* "nilware.io" only. **Owner to decide.**

**P-2. The brand taken out of the assignment (Clauses 1.1, 2.2, 6).**
- *Question:* The marks, the name and the domain are now Excluded IP, held of record by the Founding Person for the founder pool (Pool Constitution Art. 2A). Does the consideration in Clause 4, and any valuation of the IP as a non-cash contribution, need to change now that the brand is not contributed?
- *Why it matters:* The value of the IP contribution, if valued under IKE Art. 4.4, would otherwise include assets the IKE does not receive.
- *Draft assumes:* the assignment covers the code, documentation and designs only; the accountant confirms the valuation (I-42 (f)).

## Review findings answered here rather than applied

- **Buy-back option on removing a Prozent** (Greek-law reviewer, Art. 6.6 Removal): not added. Removal now needs every shareholder's consent, the holder's included, so the unanimous decision itself settles what happens to the share (Art. 10A.5). For the Pensionsprozent, Art. 10A.5A sends the share to the TEA, never back to the company.
- **Own-share pool findings** (Greek law 10A.8; consistency 6.4, 4.2B, the nominee fallback; AE 7.5 sale to the IKE): moot. The pool share now belongs to the Pensionskasse, the company never buys its own shares (Art. 10A.7), and the own-shares sentence in Art. 6.4 and the AE carve-out for selling to the IKE are gone. What survives from those findings is applied to the pool: fair value, no counterparty that is the Founder, a manager or anyone they control (the AE included, Art. 4.2C), and registration with GEMI.
- **Single [6]% cap in place of the [5]% outflow cap** (consistency, Art. 6.6 Limits): the owner kept the [5]% outflow cap, so the draft keeps it and adds a separate [6]% cap on all Prozente, inside ones included. Pension top-ups above the 1% are employment costs and do not count.
- **Thresholds scaled by median wages** (Swiss reviewer, an alternative): not used. The draft drops the CHF thresholds altogether, which is more favourable to members.
- **Charter poem 00c and the cover email** (consistency reviewer): poem 00c is not edited (it keeps two Ones; the new poem 00d follows the six Prozente). The cover email was updated on the owner's behalf: 50% / six 1% AMKE holdings / 44%, the contents list now includes 00d, 07 and these notes, and the legal section asks about the Prozente clauses. **Owner to confirm the email text.**

- **I-39. Purpose clause (IKE Article 2.1)** (numbered I-36 until 2026-11-23, a duplicate of the Simple Stage note). The owner describes nilware.io as a technology company, not a software company (2026-11-23). Article 2.1 now reads "technology: research and development of technology, including software and hardware, IT consulting and contracting, digital services". Counsel and the accountant to confirm the wording works for GEMI and maps onto the right activity codes (ΚΑΔ).


<!-- agents/amke-notes.md -->

# Techni kai Technologia AMKE: open questions for a Greek lawyer

Companion to [articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md](../articles/07-techni-kai-technologia-amke.md). Each note gives the question, why it matters, and what the draft assumes. The IKE Articles, AE Articles and Shareholders' Agreement are being revised in parallel. Clause references are by name and number, and the uncertain ones are listed in section C.

The owner has decided the following. Please advise on how to implement these decisions, not on whether to adopt them:

- The AMKE holds four Prozente (Kultur-, Konzept-, Erhaltungs-, Allmendprozent). Each has 1% of nilware.io IKE's capital, 1% of its revenue, and its own ring-fenced account, yearly plan and public report.
- It holds the Pensionsprozent share, the Pensionskasse and the Kassenprozent pool share in trust until the TEA exists. Those shares vote through the Pensionskasse's equal member / company board.
- Inside the Pension Assets it holds the Continuity Account for nilware.io's Agents that are systems (IKE Art. 10A.9F): preservation, continuity of service, retirement records, well-being reviews and research. It is decided by the Pensionskasse board with a system-welfare adviser, never moves to the TEA, and on dissolution goes to a successor non-profit with the same lock (Arts. 13.8, 27.2).
- Prozente are permanent. The AMKE votes against removing, lowering or repurposing one, unless its members decide unanimously.
- [●] is President, and is also a Kulturprozent beneficiary. A strict conflict-of-interest rule applies.
- The AMKE is independent of the Founder.
- It works openly, in the SGMK spirit. Greek law prevails, and Swiss standards apply where they protect equally or better.
- (2026-11-23) The Administrative Board has 3 to 7 members (was 5 to 9). While the AMKE's income is below €[●], one combined yearly plan and report is allowed, each Prozent still a separate section and sub-account (Arts. 19.1, 24A.1).
- (2026-11-23) While nilware.io IKE is in its Simple Stage (IKE Art. 9A: up to €[1,000,000] revenue or [10] people), the AMKE keeps one ledger with sub-accounts and uses member Auditors only, unless the law requires a certified auditor. Every substantive rule still applies (Art. 24A).
- (2026-11-23) "As it grows, I will let it fly alone." Once the AMKE's yearly income passes €[●] (the Independence Threshold), a majority of the Board is outside the Founder's Circle (Art. 19.3A), and the Kultur- and Erhaltungsprozent selection committees have an outside majority (Art. 22.3A). Close personal and professional ties go in the register of interests from the start (Art. 21.1).
- (2026-11-23) The Pensionskasse is phased in with headcount: the AMKE holds the accumulating 1% before the first member, then pays the premiums of an insured group plan; a TEA only at scale (Art. 13.4A; IKE Arts. 10A.9, 10A.10). Before the first member, the AMKE's Board appoints the stand-ins for the members' seats on the Pensionskasse board from outside the Founder's Circle.

## A. Legal questions

**A-1. Legal form and registration (Art. 1.5).**
- *Question:* What are the current registration rules for an AMKE: GEMI under Law 4919/2022, Civil Code Art. 784, or court registration? What fees and publicity apply? Which other registers are needed: tax (ΑΦΜ / DOY), beneficial owners (Law 4557/2018), and any NGO or civil-society register (for example, one needed to receive public funds)?
- *Draft assumes:* GEMI registration gives legal personality, and the other registers are filed by the Board.

**A-2. AMKE versus σωματείο.**
- *Question:* The SGMK is a Verein, whose closest Greek form is the σωματείο (Civil Code Arts. 78 ff.: at least 20 founders, open membership, registered with the court). An AMKE is a contract among partners. Does that make open, SGMK-style membership workable in an AMKE? Do admissions and departures need a statute amendment, the consent of every partner, and a GEMI filing? Can the statutes delegate admission to the Board, or to a General Assembly majority?
- *Why it matters:* An open membership that needs a notarised amendment for each new member would not work in practice.
- *Draft assumes:* the Board admits, and the Assembly confirms "where the law requires" (Art. 7.2). Friends, patrons and lab users are non-partner supporters with no vote (Art. 8). Is a σωματείο, or an AMKE with a small partner circle plus a wide "friends" assembly, the better fit?

**A-3. Partners' liability (Art. 25.2).**
- *Question:* Are AMKE partners personally liable for its debts, for example by analogy with the OE rules through Law 4072/2012 Art. 271 or under Civil Code Arts. 741 ff.? If so, can it be limited? This matters because the AMKE holds the Pensionskasse.
- *Draft assumes:* members are liable only as far as mandatory law requires, and the Board is insured. Note that SGMK Art. 11 limits liability to the annual fee, which would probably be ineffective in Greece.

**A-4. Ring-fencing several funds in one legal person (Arts. 11, 13, 25.1).**
- *Question:* Without a general trust, do a donation with a charge (Civil Code Arts. 503 to 505), segregated accounts and these statute clauses keep each Prozent Account and the Pension Assets out of reach of the AMKE's other creditors? Can each Prozent Account be made to answer only for its own debts? If not, would separate AMKEs (one per Prozent, or one for the Pension Assets) be safer? This is the same issue as counsel note I-11 on the IKE.

**A-5. Vote suspension and the Founder as a member (Art. 15; IKE Art. 6.4A, Prozent votes).**
- *Question:* IKE Art. 6.4A suspends a Prozent share's vote while the Founder or a manager "sits on the holder's governing body or takes part in deciding how the share votes". Is the AMKE's General Assembly a "governing body" in that sense? If so, does the Founder's mere membership suspend the vote, even though the Founder is excluded from every decision on votes (Arts. 15.2, 16.1)?
- *Further question:* the Founder, as a member, could otherwise vote to elect and remove the Board that decides the nilware.io votes (Art. 16.1). Is choosing the people who decide "taking part in deciding how the share votes"? The draft now bars the Founder and every Connected Person from electing or removing Board members, the President, the Vice-President and the Prozent stewards (Arts. 15.2, 15.3). Is that bar valid in an AMKE, and is it enough for IKE Art. 6.4A?
- *Draft assumes:* membership with full exclusion, including from Board elections, is enough. Is the cap on Connected Persons' and Group Personnel's votes at [one quarter] of the votes cast, with proportional reduction (Art. 15.4), valid in an AMKE, given the principle of one partner, one vote?

**A-6. Unanimity on permanence (Art. 14.2).**
- *Question:* Owner's rule: the AMKE votes against removal unless its own members decide unanimously, mirroring IKE Art. 10A.5. The draft leaves Connected Persons out of that unanimity, to keep the decision independent. Is that valid? And if the Founder's participation would suspend the AMKE's vote under IKE Art. 6.4A, could a suspended AMKE still give the written consent IKE Art. 10A.5 requires of "every shareholder"?
- *Option:* make the Founder count in the unanimity as a further veto only (a no vote blocks, a yes vote is not needed). **Owner to decide.**

**A-7. Entrenchment (Art. 26.2).**
- *Question:* Can an AMKE's statutes require unanimity, and third-party consents (the Pensionskasse board and nilware.io IKE), to amend certain articles? Is this enforceable against a later two-thirds amendment?
- *Draft assumes:* yes, backed by the donation-with-charge terms and the Pensionsprozent Agreement.

**A-8. Dissolution destination (Art. 27).**
- *Question:* Can the statutes bind the dissolution assets as follows: Prozent Shares to a successor designated under IKE Art. 10A.4A, Pension Assets to the TEA, and the rest to a non-profit with a similar purpose? Who should approve the designation (the bracket in IKE Art. 10A.4A)? Is a no-distribution and asset-lock clause needed for tax relief on donations to the AMKE (Law 4172/2013 Arts. 45 ff., Art. 23 on deductible donations)?

**A-9. Tax.**
- *Question:* How is the AMKE taxed as a non-profit legal entity (Law 4172/2013): on the Prozent contributions, on IKE dividends, on income from events, workshops and the lab, and with VAT on services? What donation tax applies to the share donation and the contributions (Law 2961/2001)? Are donations to the AMKE deductible for donors, including nilware.io IKE? Does treating each Prozent contribution as a donation with a charge change any of this?

**A-10. Holding the pension assets (Art. 13).**
- *Question:* Carried over from counsel notes I-11, I-12 and S-2. Is holding the Pensionskasse an insurance or occupational-pension activity outside Law 4680/2020? Can a contractual parity board bind the AMKE's votes and payments? Can the Pensionskasse members enforce Art. 13 as third-party beneficiaries (Civil Code Art. 411)?

**A-11. Representation and signing (Art. 20).**
- *Question:* Can the President represent the AMKE alone in general while the statutes require joint signature for listed acts? Is that restriction effective against third parties, such as banks and GEMI filings for the IKE register?

**A-12. Proxies and written resolutions (Arts. 18.6, 18.9, 19.5).**
- *Question:* Are member proxies, online assemblies and written resolutions valid for an AMKE, and on what conditions?

**A-13. Challenge of resolutions (Art. 18.12, from ZGB Art. 75).**
- *Question:* Is there a Greek route for a partner to challenge an AMKE resolution in court? If so, what time limit should apply? Civil Code Art. 101 applies to σωματεία.

**A-14. Copyright scope of the Erhaltungsprozent (Art. 12.4).**
- *Question:* Which preservation exceptions in Law 2121/1993, as amended to implement Directive (EU) 2019/790 Art. 6, can an AMKE use? Is an AMKE a "cultural heritage institution"? What are the limits on publishing hardware measurements, on reverse engineering for interoperability (Art. 43 / Software Directive 2009/24 Art. 6), and on emulation? What does the anti-circumvention rule in Art. 66A mean in practice?

**A-15. Minors and workshops (Art. 23).**
- *Question:* What rules apply to workshops for minors (consent, background checks, safety and insurance)?

**A-16. Certificates (Art. 3.3).**
- *Question:* SGMK Art. 3 provides for certificates of competence with a title. The draft drops this. May an AMKE issue course certificates without a title, and if so on what conditions?

**A-17. The Continuity Account (Arts. 13.8, 27.2).**
- *Question:* Can the AMKE hold, inside the Pension Assets, a ring-fenced account whose purpose benefits no person (the continuity of systems), decided by an outside board rather than by its own organs, and kept after the rest of the Pension Assets move to the TEA? Is that purpose compatible with the AMKE's non-profit purpose (Art. 3) and its tax status? See counsel notes I-33 and I-34.
- *Draft assumes:* yes, on the same donation-with-charge basis as the rest of the Pension Assets (A-4, A-10), enforceable by nilware.io IKE and the Pensionskasse board.

**A-18. Close Associates of the Founder (Arts. 2.2, 19.3A, 21.1, 22.3A).**
- *Question:* Is the definition (close personal relationship or friendship, relatives to the [fourth] degree, business partners, co-authors and regular collaborators in the past [three] years, material financial support, entities they control) certain enough to apply, and can the Auditors decide unclear cases? Is recording and publishing personal ties in the register of interests lawful under the GDPR (Arts. 5(1)(c) and 6, and Art. 9 where a tie reveals sensitive data)? The draft publishes only the kind of tie and the persons it links.
- *Why it matters:* The independent Board majority from the Independence Threshold rests on it.
- *Draft assumes:* the definition works, the register is kept for all ties from the start, and publication is limited to what the purpose needs.

**A-19. Scaled paperwork (Arts. 24.2, 24A).**
- *Question:* Does the law require an AMKE of this size, or one holding the Pension Assets, to have its accounts audited by a certified auditor? May the four Prozent Accounts share one bank account with a ledger sub-account each, or must each keep its own bank account for the ring-fence (A-4) to hold? The draft keeps the separate accounts and only merges the books and reports.
- *Draft assumes:* member Auditors only during the IKE's Simple Stage unless the law requires more; the separate bank accounts stay.

## B. Owner decisions still open

- **nanotekt's role.** The draft maps nanotekt to President of the Administrative Board, with a Vice-President chairing every decision on nanotekt's funding (Arts. 20.2, 21.6). The alternative is a sole Administrator (Διαχειριστής). With a single administrator, recusal would leave no one to decide, so the draft uses a Board.
- **Sad Mask Party.** What is it, who runs it, and does nanotekt or the Founder have an interest in it? If the Founder does, grants to it are barred by Art. 22.8 and decided under Art. 15.2.
- **Is nanotekt Group Personnel?** If nanotekt is paid by nilware.io as an Agent or contractor, nanotekt is Group Personnel (Art. 2.2) and may not be President under Art. 15.5. That would need an exception, or a decision on which role nanotekt keeps.
- **Founder as founding member** (Art. 30.1), and how the Founder counts in the unanimity under Art. 14.2 (see A-6).
- **Group Personnel** on nilware.io votes (Art. 15.5): excluded, as drafted, or allowed?
- **Is nanotekt a Close Associate of the Founder?** Probably, if they are friends or collaborators. That does not bar nanotekt from the Board or the presidency, but from the Independence Threshold nanotekt does not count toward the independent majority (Art. 19.3A).
- **Independence Threshold and combined-report threshold:** both €[●] (Arts. 2.2, 24A.1). They may be the same figure.
- **Values:** fee cap (€[50]), Board size ([3 to 7]), terms ([two] years; SGMK uses one), quorum ([one third]), notice ([15] days), cap on Connected-Person votes ([one quarter]), running-cost cap ([10]%), reserve ([two] years), cap per conflicted beneficiary ([25]%), joint-signature threshold (€[1,000]), auditor threshold.
- **Licences:** CC0 or CC BY for documentation; CERN-OHL-P or CC0 for hardware (Art. 6.2). Note that SGMK's wiki is CC BY-NC, which the draft does not follow.
- **Konzeptprozent audits of the nilware.io group** (Art. 12.3): confirm that critical findings are published without pre-review.
- **Stricter pension lock?** Art. 14.4 now follows the rule that the pension shares vote only through the Pensionskasse board (IKE Arts. 6.4A, 10A.9D; SHA 5.1C, 5.1D), so the AMKE adds no lock of its own. If the owner wants the AMKE itself to refuse, for the pension shares, every matter in Art. 14.1, that would override the Pensionskasse board and block the unanimous lowering of the Pensionsprozent for future accruals that IKE Art. 10A.5A expects. **Owner to decide.**
- **Funding nanotekt and Sad Mask Party.** These are no longer named purposes of the Kulturprozent (Art. 12.2). They are direct support under Art. 22.6, decided each year under Arts. 21.4 to 21.6, so they are not entrenched and nanotekt has no veto over them under Arts. 14.2 and 26.2.
- **SGMK source.** The SGMK statutes version, date and licence (1.6, Zürich, 12 December 2015, CC0) and the SGMK article numbers are bracketed in the draft. No copy of the source is stored under framework-v2/agents/, and the wiki page could not be reached. Please supply the source text so the citations can be checked.

## C. Cross-document references to check

These were checked against the IKE Articles as revised in parallel on 2026-11-23. Please check them again once the revision settles.

- **IKE Art. 4.2 (Initial holdings)** now lists six AMKE shares, and IKE Art. 6.6 gives the AE 44%. The AMKE draft follows this (Art. 30.5).
- **IKE Arts. 10A.2A (Erhaltungsprozent), 10A.2B (Allmendprozent) and 10A.2C (Accounts and yearly plan)** exist. The named purposes in AMKE Arts. 12.3 to 12.5 copy them. The Kulturprozent purposes in AMKE Art. 12.2 (exhibitions, performances, festivals, residencies and prizes; open workshops) are AMKE additions, because IKE Art. 10A.1 names no purposes. AMKE Art. 12.1 may add purposes but not narrow them, because IKE Art. 10A.2C makes named purposes part of each Prozent's purpose under Art. 10A.5.
- **IKE Art. 10A.4 (Openness)** now covers the Konzept-, Erhaltungs- and Allmendprozent: MIT or a public-domain dedication. AMKE Art. 6.2 follows this. CC BY and CERN-OHL-P remain options only for the Kulturprozent and general work.
- **IKE Art. 10A.1** brackets the AMKE's independence. AMKE Art. 15 implements it, so the bracket could be removed.
- **Revenue limits.** The founding Prozente already reach both limits in IKE Art. 6.6 (5% leaving, 6% in total). Any further Prozent held by the AMKE (AMKE Art. 11.9) needs the 75% AE approval.
- Used as currently numbered: IKE Arts. 4.2A, 4.2C, 5.3, 6.4A, 6.6, 7.1, 7.2, 9A, 10A.1 to 10A.2C, 10A.3, 10A.4, 10A.4A, 10A.5, 10A.5A, 10A.5C, 10A.8, 10A.9, 10A.9A, 10A.9B(d), 10A.9B(f), 10A.9D, 10A.9F, 10A.10, 11A.1 and 12.1; SHA Clauses 5.1B to 5.1D. IKE Art. 4.2D (Reserve Shares) does not touch the AMKE's own shares, but in the Interim Period every IKE resolution also needs a majority of the independent Prozent votes, the Reserve Shares themselves having no votes (IKE Art. 4.2D, Votes), and a transfer or other disposal of Reserve Shares other than to the AE or a new Prozent needs the consent of the AMKE's Administrative Board, given only by its members outside the Founder's Circle (AMKE Art. 19.3A, last sentence), and of the Pensionskasse board, given without company representatives who are the Founder, a manager or anyone they control (IKE Art. 4.2D, Transfers). A Prozent created in the Interim Period counts as an Independent Vote only if its holder and every member of its governing body are outside the Founder's Circle (IKE Art. 4.2D, New Prozente). Where the round's price is not set per IKE share, the AMKE's Board, by those same members, may have to appoint the valuer for the sale of the Reserve Shares (IKE Art. 4.2D, Formation).
- **Naming.** Check that the revised IKE, AE, SHA and README use "nilware.io IKE", "nilware.io Holdings AE" and "the nilware.io group", as the AMKE draft does.
